- **Alternatives considered:** *Keep OV-1 UNVERIFIED (the stricter prior reading) despite Ace's answer* — rejected: the process makes Ace the authority on rights, and he has now given the confirmation the register was waiting for; holding it open indefinitely would block the project on a question its designated authority has answered. *Resolve it with no caveat* — rejected: the screenshot-provenance finding is a real, documented tension that a future reader (and the 2.09 publish step) must see; silently dropping it would hide a genuine risk. *Block 2.09 entirely on written proof now* — rejected as premature: nothing publishes photos before 2.09, and the §5 gate already prevents an accidental publish, so the written-proof step can run in parallel rather than halt the project.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: the launch blocker is cleared on a **verbal** assurance about material that is demonstrably third-party in origin; if that assurance proves narrower than "may republish publicly," the 2.09 publish step (not the build) is where it bites, and the §5 unpublished-until-cleared gate is the safety net. `facts.md` and the register now agree. The 8 already-published demo photos are Ace's own and were never in question.
- **Links:** Phase 1.06 (Ace sit-down, Task 7); OV-1 in `src/_project-state/current-state.md` §Owed-verification register; `facts.md` §Content provenance; `docs/content-ingestion-plan.md` §5; Phase 2.09; supersedes the contradictory prior OV-1 states.

### D-1.06-4 · 2026-07-16 · Ace sit-down captured — OV-3 confirmed as-is, locked model validated, Archive/Season direction set; Part-1 human work closed
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The Ace sit-down was the last owed Part-1 human step and the precondition 2.01 ran without (D-2.01-7). It happened on 2026-07-16 and is captured in `docs/ace-demo/feedback.md`. Ace's inputs: (a) likes the homepage, project is "on the right path," nothing he flagged as missing or factually wrong; (b) the footer unofficial-archive wording (OV-3) is **correct as-is** — confirmed, not corrected; (c) Archive & Season pages should be **very similar to crnobelanostalgija.com**; (d) no Legends/About/Contact input yet — a second sit-down is expected in ~a week; (e) he was not prescriptive about a featured season ("all seasons are in the Drive").
- **Decision:** Record the sit-down as closing the Part-1 owed human work. **OV-3 → Resolved** (wording VERIFIED as-is in `facts.md`; no code change, since it was confirmed not corrected — the "correction ships via a separate Code PR" path is moot). **The locked 4-type model is validated against Ace's stated needs:** he named no content requirement `siteSettings`/`season`/`person`/`photo` cannot hold, so the lock **stands** and the D-2.01-7 "model unchecked against Ace" risk is cleared. **2.02 direction is set:** model the Archive/Season templates on crnobelanostalgija.com. **2.05 stays gated** on the *second* sit-down's feedback (Legends/About/Contact), which 2.02 does not depend on.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Treat "similar to crnobelanostalgija.com" as a content-model signal and reopen the lock* — rejected: it is a layout/design-direction ask, satisfiable within the four existing types; reopening the lock for it would be unwarranted churn. *Hold 2.02 until the second sit-down too* — rejected: 2.02 (Archive/Season) has its direction now; only 2.05 (people/about/contact) needs the later input, so coupling them would idle a ready phase. *Log nothing and just flip the register* — rejected: the model-validation outcome closes a logged known-risk (D-2.01-7) and a future reader needs the reasoning, not just a status flip.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: 2.05 now has an explicit external dependency (the ~1-week second sit-down) that could slip; it is tracked in §Remaining human steps and does not block 2.02. If the second sit-down surfaces a genuinely new content shape, the lock is revisited then — but nothing from *this* sit-down requires it. Part 1's remaining human steps are marked done in `current-state.md`.
- **Links:** Phase 1.06 (Ace sit-down, Task 7); `docs/ace-demo/feedback.md`; D-2.01-7 (risk now cleared); D-1.06-3 (OV-1); `facts.md` (OV-3); Phases 2.02 / 2.05; crnobelanostalgija.com (reference site per project instructions).

### D-2.02-1 · 2026-07-16 · Season-card meta overline is the decade in neutral-500 — not orange, not a league
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** `brand.md` §Components specified the Season card as "3:2 image, **orange overline (league)**, serif title". Two problems: the locked content model has **no league/competition field** (D-2.01-2 retired `match`; there is no competition taxonomy), so a league label could only be invented — and orange text on a white card measures **3.08:1**, failing WCAG AA for normal text and breaking D-1.02-1.
- **Decision:** The season-card overline is the **decade label** („1990-ти", derived from the required `season.decade`), rendered in **neutral-500** (4.9:1 on paper, AA). `brand.md` §Components was corrected to say so.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Keep an orange overline* — rejected: fails AA (3.08:1) and violates D-1.02-1. *Name a league/competition* — rejected: no such field exists; it would be invented content, which content-truth forbids. *Omit the overline entirely* — rejected: the decade is real, free (already required on every season), and gives the card a second scannable axis. *Use the year-span* — rejected as redundant: the title already carries it („Сезона 1992/93").
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: the card's overline repeats information the decade section header already states, so within a decade section every card's overline reads the same. Accepted because the card is also reachable out of that context (and a season page links back by decade). The brand's orange signature is carried elsewhere on the page (decade rule, section headings), not here.
- **Links:** Phase 2.02; `brand.md` §Components (corrected) / §Contrast; D-1.02-1; D-2.01-2; handover §5.4 / §12 BC-3.

### D-2.02-2 · 2026-07-16 · Archive orders seasons by `slug.current desc`, not by `title`
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The Archive groups by `decade desc` and needs a deterministic order for seasons *within* a decade. The homepage's featured-season rule (D-1.05-1) sorts by `decade desc, title desc`, which works when every title is „Сезона 1985/86". But `docs/content-ingestion-plan.md` shows titles will **not** be uniform after 2.09: single-year folders become „Сезона 1950", and era folders become „Беласица 1922–1926" (§3.4/§3.5, flagged `manual-title` for a human to rewrite). A title sort would interleave those wrongly, and gets worse as editors rewrite titles by hand.
- **Decision:** Order within a decade by **`slug.current desc`**. Every slug the ingestion derives starts with a 4-digit year (`1992-93`, `1950`, `1922-26`), so a lexical descending sort is chronological descending for **all** folder shapes.
- **Alternatives considered:** *`title desc` (the D-1.05-1 precedent)* — rejected: titles are non-uniform by design and are hand-edited, so the sort key would drift with editorial copy. *Add a numeric `startYear` field* — rejected: the content model is **locked** (2.01); adding a field to fix a sort is not justified when the slug already encodes the year. *`_createdAt`* — rejected: reflects ingestion order, not history.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: the sort silently depends on the ingestion's slug convention holding (year-first). If a human ever hand-writes a slug that does not start with a year, that season sorts wrongly — a real but low risk, since D-2.01-6 makes slugs unique-per-type and the convention is fixed in the ingestion plan. This intentionally **diverges** from D-1.05-1's key; the homepage rule is untouched.
- **Links:** Phase 2.02; D-1.05-1; D-2.01-6; `docs/content-ingestion-plan.md` §3; handover §4.5.

### D-2.02-3 · 2026-07-16 · Archive sections use an orange rule marker + serif H2, not the homepage's overline-only label
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The homepage labels each section with `SectionOverline` alone — a 12px uppercase overline, with the real `<h2>` visually hidden. That suits a short marketing-shaped page. The Season page is a **long document** with five data sections (Приказна / Конечна табела / Состав / Тренери / Фотографии) that a reader scans and links into; a 12px overline is too quiet to anchor them.
- **Decision:** For the Archive templates, each section is labelled with a short **orange rule marker** above a **visible serif H2** (`text-h2`, navy). `SectionOverline` is unchanged and still used on the homepage.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Reuse `SectionOverline` for consistency* — rejected: too quiet for a scannable archive document, and it hides the heading from sighted users while the page's whole value is its structure. *Overline **and** H2 together* — rejected: two stacked labels saying the same word is noise. *Plain H2, no marker* — rejected: drops the orange signature that ties these pages to the homepage.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: the Archive pages and the homepage now label sections **differently**, so the site has two heading patterns. Justified by page type (document vs. landing), and both keep the orange-rule + navy-text signature so they read as one family. If this proves jarring once 2.03 renders it, the homepage is the cheaper one to align.
- **Links:** Phase 2.02; `src/components/home/SectionOverline.tsx`; D-1.02-1; handover §4.3.

### D-2.02-4 · 2026-07-16 · ФК Беласица standings row is marked with a 2px orange left edge; the rank stays navy
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** `brand.md` §Components specified the Stats table as "ФК Беласица row highlighted `#FBF3EA` with **orange rank**". Orange `#E4741C` on that highlight surface measures **2.80:1** — it fails WCAG AA outright, and it contradicts **D-1.02-1**, the rule stated in the same file. This is the same trap D-1.03-1 hit in the shell: the component prose drifted from the locked rule.
- **Decision:** The Беласица row keeps the `highlight` surface and ink/navy text; the club and rank are **navy**. The orange becomes a **2px left-edge marker** on the row's first cell — a marker, which is orange's only legal role on a light surface. `brand.md` §Components was corrected, and the failing ratio recorded in §Contrast so it cannot be reintroduced.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Follow `brand.md` literally (orange rank)* — rejected: ships a documented AA failure. *Darken orange until it passes on `#FBF3EA`* — rejected: that is a new colour value; the palette is locked (D-0.00-12) and inventing tokens is forbidden. *Bold navy rank with no orange at all* — rejected: loses the instant "find our row" scan cue that the highlight exists to provide. *Row highlight only, no marker* — rejected: the surface tint alone is subtle at a glance on a long table.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: `brand.md`'s original visual intent (an orange numeral) is not delivered; the accent moves to the row edge. Net gain: the row is *more* findable (a full-height edge marker reads faster than one coloured digit) and AA holds. Root cause also fixed — D-1.02-1's scope was widened from "on paper" to "any light surface".
- **Links:** Phase 2.02; `brand.md` §Components / §Contrast (both corrected); D-1.02-1; D-1.03-1 (same conflict, shell); handover §6.4 / §12 BC-1.

### D-2.02-5 · 2026-07-16 · Breadcrumb renders on paper above the hero, not over the photo
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The Season page needs a breadcrumb (Почетна / Архива / <season>) and a full-bleed hero. `brand.md`'s Hero anatomy puts a **navy gradient at the bottom** of the photo, so the top of the image is unmuted and arbitrary — a crumb placed there could land on a bright sky or a white shirt and be unreadable. The page also has a **photo-less hero variant** (D-2.02-8 context / handover §6.2b).
- **Decision:** The breadcrumb sits **on the paper surface above the hero**, inside `Container`, `py-5`. Navy links, mist separators, current crumb neutral-700 + `aria-current="page"`.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Overlay the crumb on the photo* — rejected: unreadable over an unmuted top edge, and unverifiable against ~915 unseen scans. *Add a second top gradient to darken the crumb area* — rejected: eats into the photo and fights "photos are the heroes". *Only show the crumb on the photo-less variant* — rejected: two different breadcrumb positions on the same route is worse than one consistent one.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: the hero starts slightly lower on the page than a true edge-to-edge top-of-page hero (the homepage keeps its hero flush at the top — the two heroes differ by design). One treatment now serves both hero variants, and legibility is guaranteed regardless of the photo.
- **Links:** Phase 2.02; `brand.md` §Components (Breadcrumb / Hero); handover §6.1 / §6.2b.

### D-2.02-6 · 2026-07-16 · „Фотографии" renders every season photo, including the one used in the hero
- **Status:** Superseded by D-3.04-2
- **Context:** The hero uses the season's first back-referenced photo (`order(coalesce(date,"9999") asc)[0]`, the homepage's pattern). The photo grid could either show `[1...]` (no repeat) or all of them (`[0...]`, hero repeats). The hero displays **no caption** — it carries the season title.
- **Decision:** „Фотографии" renders **all** the season's photos, including the hero's. The hero is treated as a cropped *presentation band*; the grid is the complete *captioned set*.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Grid = `[1...]`, excluding the hero* — rejected: the hero photo's `caption` and `date` would then appear **nowhere on the site**, permanently hiding archive data for the sake of avoiding a repeat. That is unacceptable for an archive whose substance is captioned scans. *Show the caption inside the hero instead* — rejected: the hero's text slot belongs to the season title; stacking a photo caption under it confuses what the H1 refers to. *Exclude the hero only when it has no caption* — rejected: conditional, surprising, and it makes the grid's contents depend on an unrelated field.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: on a season with photos, the lead image is visibly shown **twice** (once cropped as the hero, once matted in the grid). Accepted deliberately: data completeness beats visual tidiness here, and the two presentations differ (cover-crop vs. contain-on-mat, per D-2.02-7).
- **Links:** Phase 2.02; D-2.01-1 (back-reference); D-2.02-7; handover §6.2 / §6.7.

### D-2.02-7 · 2026-07-16 · Archival photo grid uses `object-contain` on the mat; hero and cards keep `object-cover` (PhotoFrame gains a `fit` prop)
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** `brand.md` §Photo treatment specifies a **mixed-quality rule**: "every scan sits in a fixed-ratio frame on a Mist mat… Smaller/lower-res scans get a **wider mat**; the outer frame is identical… Low-res images are matted, never stretched." The existing `PhotoFrame` uses `object-cover` unconditionally, which **fills** the frame and crops — meaning the mat is only ever visible in the empty state and the rule has never actually been implemented. The P0.1 audit says ~915 scans of wildly varying size/quality are coming (2.09), so this matters at scale.
- **Decision:** The season's „Фотографии" grid renders `PhotoFrame` with **`object-contain`**, so each scan sits whole on the mist mat and smaller scans naturally show more mat inside an identical outer frame. Presentation surfaces (hero, season-card lead, homepage) keep **`object-cover`**. This needs one **additive** prop on `PhotoFrame` — `fit="cover" | "contain"`, defaulting to `"cover"`, so no existing caller changes.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Keep `object-cover` everywhere* — rejected: `brand.md`'s mixed-quality rule would remain decorative prose that no code honours, and low-res scans would be upscaled and cropped — exactly what the rule forbids. *Switch `PhotoFrame` to `contain` globally* — rejected: it would letterbox the homepage hero and every card, a visual regression across shipped pages. *A separate `ArchivePhotoFrame` component* — rejected: duplicates hotspot/placeholder/`next/image` logic for a one-line difference.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: the photo grid has **visually uneven interiors** (varying mat widths) where a cover-crop grid would be flush and uniform — that unevenness *is* the intended honesty, and the identical outer frames keep the grid aligned. `PhotoFrame` grows one prop; the default keeps every current caller byte-identical. 2.03 must implement the prop.
- **Links:** Phase 2.02; `brand.md` §Photo treatment (a `Fit` line was added); `src/components/home/PhotoFrame.tsx`; handover §6.7.

### D-2.02-8 · 2026-07-16 · A season with all five sections empty renders one archive notice, not a bare hero
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** Every Season-page data section (story, table, squad, trainers, photos) **omits itself when empty** (per the brief). But `docs/content-ingestion-plan.md` says that right after 2.09 **~all 74 seasons** will be shells with only `slug`/`title`/`decade` — story/table/squad/trainers are hand-curated later, and ingested photos land **unpublished** behind the rights gate (§5). With every section omitted, the page would render a hero sitting directly on top of the footer: a page that looks broken on the archive's most common state, on day one.
- **Decision:** When **all five** sections are empty, render a single **archive notice** — a bordered white card with a serif heading („Оваа сезона сѐ уште нема објавени детали."), one structural line, the five `PlaceholderChip`s naming what is still to come, and a link back to `/arhiva`. Rendered **only** when all five are empty; if even one has content, the notice is omitted and empty sections simply do not render.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Let the sections omit and accept the bare hero* — rejected: the default state of the whole archive would look like a bug. *404 an empty season* — rejected: the season is real, it is legitimately in the archive, and its title/decade are true facts; hiding it would misrepresent the archive's coverage. *Show placeholder chips per section (five stub sections)* — rejected: five hatched chips down an empty page is louder and sadder than one honest notice, and the brief explicitly forbids placeholder prose for `story`.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: one more conditional branch in the template, and a piece of structural UI copy that must stay factually neutral (it describes the archive's state and claims nothing about the season). Every string is structural, so content-truth holds.
- **Links:** Phase 2.02; `docs/content-ingestion-plan.md` §4/§5; `src/components/home/PlaceholderChip.tsx`; handover §6.9 / §7.

### D-2.02-9 · 2026-07-16 · Photo caption = orange rule marker + neutral-500 date overline, placed below the frame
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** Two conflicts in one component. (1) `brand.md` §Photo treatment **and** the 2.02 brief both specify "**orange overline** (year)" for captions — orange on paper is **2.80:1**, failing AA and contradicting D-1.02-1 (which the same brief also mandates). (2) The homepage gallery renders captions as a **navy-gradient overlay** with `line-clamp-2`; that suits a decorative mosaic but truncates, and a season's captions are archive data (date + description) that must be read in full.
- **Decision:** The date overline is **neutral-500 text preceded by a short orange rule marker**; the description is Inter small (neutral-700). The whole caption sits **below** the frame, **unclamped**. Orange overline *text* stays legal on navy (4.68:1) — e.g. the hero. `brand.md` §Components and §Photo treatment were both corrected.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Follow `brand.md`/the brief literally (orange overline text)* — rejected: a documented AA failure; the brief contradicts itself and its accessibility clause is the one to honour. *Reuse the homepage's gradient overlay* — rejected: `line-clamp-2` silently truncates provenance-bearing captions, and overlaying text on a `contain`-fitted scan (D-2.02-7) would put it on the mat, not the image. *Drop the orange entirely* — rejected: the marker is free, on-brand, and passes.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: the season photo grid looks **different from the homepage gallery** (caption below vs. overlaid), and rows become slightly uneven where captions differ in length. Accepted: this grid is a reading surface, the homepage's is a display surface.
- **Links:** Phase 2.02; `brand.md` §Components / §Photo treatment (both corrected); D-1.02-1; D-2.02-7; handover §6.7 / §12 BC-2.

### D-2.02-10 · 2026-07-16 · Standings on mobile scroll horizontally inside the frame; all nine columns kept; `#` + `Клуб` sticky-left
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** `season.finalTable` has nine columns (position/club/played/wins/draws/losses/goalsFor/goalsAgainst/points). Nine columns do not fit 375px. The brief allowed either horizontal scroll in a bordered frame **or** a defined column priority (i.e. hiding columns).
- **Decision:** Keep **all nine columns** and scroll horizontally **inside the bordered frame** (`overflow-x-auto`, `min-w-[560px]` on the table); `#` and `Клуб` are **sticky-left** so every row keeps its identity while scrolling. The scroll container gets `tabindex="0"` + `role="region"` + an `aria-label`, since a scrollable region must be keyboard-reachable. The page body never scrolls sideways.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Column priority — hide Поб/Нер/Пор/ДГ/ПГ on mobile* — rejected: the final table **is** the archival artifact; a mobile visitor would silently see less history than a desktop one, and there is no "expand" affordance that does not reinvent the scroll. *Card-per-row layout on mobile* — rejected: destroys column comparison, which is the only reason a standings table exists. *Let the table overflow the body* — rejected: sideways-scrolling the whole page is a known defect (and the existing `DecadeTimeline` already establishes the scroll-in-container pattern).
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: horizontal scrolling is a mediocre mobile interaction and the sticky cells need an explicit background per row state (paper / zebra / highlight) or they render transparent over scrolled text — a real implementation trap for 2.03, called out in the handover. Accepted because no data is hidden from anyone.
- **Links:** Phase 2.02; `src/components/home/DecadeTimeline.tsx` (precedent); D-2.02-14; handover §6.4.

### D-2.02-11 · 2026-07-16 · „Состав" is a light table capped at `max-w-measure`, not the navy Stats table
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The squad (player · appearances · goals) is tabular data on the same page as the navy-headed Конечна табела. Reusing the Stats-table component would put two heavy navy-header tables back to back.
- **Decision:** „Состав" is a **real `<table>`** (semantics kept: `<caption class="sr-only">`, `<thead>`, `<th scope="col">`) styled **light** — no navy fill; the header row is a neutral-700 overline over a mist rule, rows separated by mist hairlines, numbers right-aligned `tabular-nums`. It is capped at **`max-w-measure`**, matching the story column.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Reuse the navy Stats table* — rejected: two stacked navy-header tables read as a data dump and fight the calm editorial tone the direction requires. *A `<ul>` in CSS `columns` (two columns on desktop, shorter scroll)* — rejected: name/appearances/goals is genuinely tabular; a list would lose header semantics and column alignment. *Full-width table* — rejected: three columns stretched across 1200px puts metres of whitespace between a name and its numbers.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: a large squad is a **long single column** (no two-up on desktop), so the page gets tall. Accepted — it matches the reference site's appearance lists and keeps the name→number relationship readable. Two visually different tables now coexist on one page; the standings' navy header signals "this is the hard data", which is the intended hierarchy.
- **Links:** Phase 2.02; `brand.md` §Components (Stats table); handover §6.5.

### D-2.02-12 · 2026-07-16 · Decade counts pluralise: `1 → „сезона"`, otherwise „сезони"
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The Decade section header shows a real count of seasons in that decade („10 сезони"). A naive template renders „**1 сезони**" for a single-season decade — wrong Macedonian on a Macedonian-only site. Early decades (1920s–1940s, era folders) are exactly where counts of 1 are likely.
- **Decision:** `n === 1 ? "1 сезона" : n + " сезони"`.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Always „сезони"* — rejected: grammatically wrong, and visible on the pages most likely to have one entry. *Add `Intl.PluralRules`* — rejected: overkill for one string in one locale, and Macedonian's rule here is a single special case. *Drop the count* — rejected: it is a real, computed, truthful number that helps scanning.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: a hand-rolled plural rule that only covers this one phrase; any future counted noun needs its own handling (at which point `Intl.PluralRules` earns its place).
- **Links:** Phase 2.02; `brand.md` §Components (Decade section header); handover §4.6.

### D-2.02-13 · 2026-07-16 · The sticky decade jump-nav is built (the brief made it optional)
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The brief listed a decade jump-nav as "optional but recommended". The Archive will list **~74 seasons across 11 decades** (P0.1 / ingestion plan) — a very long page where reaching the 1930s means scrolling past forty cards.
- **Decision:** Build it: a `<nav aria-label="Скок по деценија">` **sticky beneath the site header** (`top-header`, D-2.02-17), listing only decades that have ≥1 published season, newest first, linking to `#d<decade>` anchors. On mobile it is a horizontally scrollable rail. Scroll-spy for the current decade is **optional** and not required of 2.03.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Skip it* — rejected: 74 seasons is exactly the scale that makes an archive index unusable without one. *Paginate by decade (a page per decade)* — rejected: breaks "every season on one page", adds routes, and loses the sense of the whole archive. *Non-sticky nav at the top* — rejected: useless once you have scrolled past it, which is immediately.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: a **second sticky bar** below the header eats vertical space on mobile, and anchored sections must offset for **both** (`scroll-mt-header` plus the rail's height) or headings hide underneath — an implementation trap flagged for 2.03. It also creates a hard dependency on the header-height token (D-2.02-17).
- **Links:** Phase 2.02; D-2.02-17; `src/components/home/DecadeTimeline.tsx` (rail precedent); handover §5.3.

### D-2.02-14 · 2026-07-16 · Standings headers show an abbreviation with the full Macedonian label `sr-only`
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** Nine standings columns must fit. The schema's own titles are full words (Одиграни, Победи, Нерешени, Порази, Дадени голови, Примени голови, Бодови) which will not fit as headers, and the obvious one-letter abbreviations **collide**: „**П**обеди" and „**П**орази" both start with П.
- **Decision:** Each `<th scope="col">` shows an unambiguous short form (**#, Клуб, Од, Поб, Нер, Пор, ДГ, ПГ, Бод**) with the schema's full Macedonian label in a **visually-hidden span**, so assistive tech announces the real word.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Single letters (О/П/Н/П…)* — rejected: П means two different things; ambiguous to every reader. *Full words as headers* — rejected: cannot fit nine columns, even on desktop. *`<abbr title>`* — rejected: `title` tooltips are unreliable on touch and inconsistently announced by screen readers. *Invent a standard abbreviation set* — rejected: no verified Macedonian convention was available to cite, so the short forms are presentational only and the full schema label remains the source of truth.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: the visible abbreviations are a design choice, not a cited standard — a Macedonian reader may know a different convention; the `sr-only` full label and the schema keep the meaning unambiguous. Slightly more markup per header cell.
- **Links:** Phase 2.02; `src/sanity/schemaTypes/season.ts` (field titles); D-2.02-10; handover §6.4.

### D-2.02-15 · 2026-07-16 · Mockups use deliberately non-plausible schematic filler, plus a disclaimer strip
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The mockups must show a populated standings table, squad and captions to be useful — but **no historical fact is VERIFIED in `facts.md`** (its "Historical facts" section is empty until Part 2 ingestion). Realistic-looking club names, players and scores in a design artifact are exactly the kind of thing that gets screenshotted, or pasted into code as seed data, and quietly becomes "content".
- **Decision:** All mockup content is **deliberately non-plausible** (`Клуб А`, `Клуб Б`, `Играч А`, `Тренер А`; self-describing filler prose that states it is schematic), and every mockup carries a **disclaimer strip** naming it a mockup with schematic content. The only real strings are the VERIFIED wordmark „ФК Беласица", the footer line „неофицијална архива", and route/nav labels from `src/lib/nav.ts`. Photos are greybox frames (OV-1 caveat / 2.09). The mockups README repeats the warning.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Plausible Macedonian names and realistic scores* — rejected: indistinguishable from real content in a screenshot; a direct content-truth hazard. *Reuse the published demo docs (Васо Цветков, Панче Пантазиев, „Сезона 1992/93")* — rejected: they are real people, so placing them in a fabricated squad with invented appearance counts would assert a false association. *Empty/`—` everywhere* — rejected: shows nothing about zebra striping, the highlight row, or column alignment, which is what the mockups exist to specify.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: the mockups look less "finished" than a polished comp with realistic data, and reviewers cannot judge how real Macedonian names (≈20–28 chars) will wrap — noted in the handover instead. Accepted: no artefact of this phase can be mistaken for content.
- **Links:** Phase 2.02; `facts.md` §Historical facts; OV-1; `docs/design-handovers/Part-2-Phase-02-mockups/README.md`.

### D-2.02-16 · 2026-07-16 · `zebra` and `highlight` exposed as real tokens (owner-directed)
- **Status:** Accepted (owner decision)
- **Context:** `brand.md` §Components named the Stats table's **zebra `#FCFBF7`** and **ФК Беласица highlight `#FBF3EA`** since 1.02, but neither was ever added to `globals.css @theme` — so no `bg-zebra` / `bg-highlight` utility existed and 2.03 would have had to hardcode two hexes, breaking the "never hardcode a colour" rule. Flagged as owed in the handover; the owner directed the fix in-session.
- **Decision:** Add `--color-zebra: #fcfbf7` and `--color-highlight: #fbf3ea` to `@theme`, and add both to `brand.md` §Color as proper token rows. Four contrast rows were computed and added to §Contrast (ink/zebra 16.7 · navy/zebra 13.9 · ink/highlight 15.7 · navy/highlight 13.1 — all AAA as surfaces).
- **Alternatives considered:** *Leave it and let 2.03 hardcode the hexes* — rejected: a hardcoded colour in a component is exactly what `brand.md` exists to prevent. *Reuse an existing near-neighbour (`paper`/`mist`)* — rejected: they are visibly different values and would change the designed table. *Define them as new colours* — not applicable: **no value was invented** — both hexes were already published in `brand.md`; this only exposes them.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: two more colour tokens in the palette, both narrow-purpose (table surfaces only). Verified in the running app: `bg-zebra` → `rgb(252,251,247)`, `bg-highlight` → `rgb(251,243,234)`. `npm run lint` + `npm run build` clean.
- **Links:** Phase 2.02; `src/app/globals.css` (`@theme`); `brand.md` §Color / §Contrast; D-2.02-4; handover §12 MT-1.

### D-2.02-17 · 2026-07-16 · `--spacing-header` (77px) added, measured from the rendered header (owner-directed)
- **Status:** Accepted (owner decision)
- **Context:** `SiteHeader` is `sticky top-0` (1.06b). The Archive's decade jump-nav (D-2.02-13) must stick **directly beneath** it, and anchored decade sections must clear it — both need the header's height. No token existed, so 2.03 would have hardcoded a magic number in two places.
- **Decision:** Add **`--spacing-header: 4.8125rem` (77px)** to `@theme`, generating `top-header` / `h-header` / `scroll-mt-header`, and document it in `brand.md` §Spacing & layout. The value was **measured on the rendered header** in-browser at both 375 and 1280 (16px padding + 44px crest tile + 16px + 1px border = 77px), not derived on paper.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Hardcode `top-[77px]`* — rejected: a magic number repeated in two files that silently breaks when the header changes. *Set the height from the header at runtime via a CSS variable / `ResizeObserver`* — rejected: client-side JS and a layout dependency for a value that is static by design. *Apply `h-header` to the `<header>` so the token **drives** the height* — the more robust option and **recommended to 2.03**, but rejected **for this phase**: it edits shipped 1.03/1.06b chrome, which is outside a design phase's remit.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted — this is a real fragility: the token **mirrors** the header's rendered height rather than driving it, so changing `SiteHeader`'s padding or crest size silently makes every sticky offset wrong. Mitigated only by a keep-in-sync note in `brand.md` **and** the `@theme` comment. Verified: `top-header` / `h-header` / `scroll-mt-header` all resolve to `77px`.
- **Links:** Phase 2.02; `src/app/globals.css` (`@theme`); `brand.md` §Spacing & layout; `src/components/SiteHeader.tsx`; D-2.02-13; handover §5.3 / §12 MT-2.

### D-2.03-1 · 2026-07-16 · The 2.03 brief's spec references are stale; built to the merged repo handover + `brand.md`
- **Status:** Accepted (owner decision, in-session)
- **Context:** The 2.03 brief arrived with a `Part-2-Phase-02-Handover.md` attached from `~/Downloads/`. That file is **not** the handover in the repo: it is an earlier draft (15 sections vs the repo's, different §0 palette, `mockup-*.png` filenames that don't exist). The brief's "Resolved decisions — bake in, do not re-open" list quotes that draft's *proposed* `D-2.02-1 … D-2.02-6`, which contradict the **actually logged** `D-2.02-1 … D-2.02-17` at the same IDs. Following the brief literally would have: rendered the season-card overline in **orange** (3.08:1 on white — fails AA), added four tokens (`orange-onDark`, `mist-fill`, `mist-line`, `muted-2`) to `brand.md`, and hidden two standings columns on mobile. The first two directly re-introduce the AA bugs that commit `faaf8ba` fixed the same day (§12 BC-1/BC-2/BC-3), and the brief's §0 palette is a different one entirely (navy `#12243B` vs the locked `#12294F`).
- **Decision:** Build to **`docs/design-handovers/Part-2-Phase-02-Handover.md` + `brand.md` + the real `decisions.md`**, and treat the attached draft's §0 table, its four candidate tokens and its `D-2.02-1…6` list as superseded. Surfaced the conflict to Lazar before writing code; he chose this option. Consequently **no tokens were added to `brand.md` this phase** — every value the templates need already exists (`neutral-500` for muted-2, `mist` for the greybox, `zebra`/`highlight`/`--spacing-header` from D-2.02-16/-17), and `orange-onDark` is unnecessary because `brand.md` §Contrast already clears plain `orange` on navy at 4.6:1.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Follow the brief's resolved-decisions list literally* — rejected: it fails WCAG AA in three places, overwrites owner-directed corrections, and cites a palette this project does not use. *Add the four tokens anyway as harmless aliases* — rejected: `orange-onDark #F0855A` is a **new invented colour** (nothing in `brand.md` sampled it), and the other three duplicate existing tokens under second names — the exact drift `brand.md` exists to prevent. *Stop and have the brief regenerated* — offered to Lazar; he chose to proceed against the repo spec instead.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: this phase's shipped UI **deliberately diverges from the brief text it was given**, so anyone auditing brief-vs-code will find mismatches — that is what this entry is for. The 2.03 brief should be regenerated from the merged 2.02 handover before it is used as history. No `brand.md` change was needed or made.
- **Links:** Phase 2.03; `docs/design-handovers/Part-2-Phase-02-Handover.md` §12; `brand.md` §Contrast; D-1.02-1; D-2.02-1 / -4 / -9 / -10 / -16 / -17; commit `faaf8ba`.

### D-2.03-2 · 2026-07-16 · `@portabletext/react` declared as a direct dependency (pinned 6.2.0)
- **Status:** Accepted (owner decision, in-session)
- **Context:** Handover §6.3 requires `season.story` (Portable Text) to render paragraphs, h2/h3, lists, blockquote, links and `strong`/`em`. The package was already physically in `node_modules` as a transitive dependency of `sanity`, but was **not declared** in `package.json` — importing it would have meant depending on another package's dependency tree by accident. The brief forbids adding runtime dependencies and says to stop and flag instead.
- **Decision:** Flagged it to Lazar with the alternatives; he chose to **declare it** at pinned `6.2.0` in `dependencies` and log it in `00_stack-and-config.md`. Because the package was already resolved in the tree, `npm install` added **one line** to `package-lock.json` and no new code to the install.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Hand-write a local ~60-line serializer* — rejected: it would put the correctness of nested lists, overlapping marks and link annotations on us, for an archive whose entire substance is text. *Render only `normal` blocks as `<p>`* — rejected: silently drops formatting an editor actually entered. *Import it undeclared from `node_modules`* — rejected: works today and breaks silently the day `sanity` reorganises its deps.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: the runtime dependency count goes from 16 to 17, and `@portabletext/react` must now be upgraded deliberately rather than riding along with `sanity`. Net bundle change is nil (it was already installed). `npm run build` + `npm run lint` clean.
- **Links:** Phase 2.03; `package.json`; `src/_project-state/00_stack-and-config.md`; `src/components/archive/SeasonStory.tsx`; handover §6.3.

### D-2.03-3 · 2026-07-16 · Photo ordering key is `coalesce(date,"9999") asc`; `[0]` is the lead/hero
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** `photo` has no `isLead`/rank field (handover §13 flags this; the brief's §9.3 flagged it too). The archive card's lead image and the season hero both need **one** deterministic photo, and „which photo leads" must not shuffle between builds.
- **Decision:** Order a season's back-referenced photos by **`coalesce(date, "9999") asc`** and take `[0]` — the same key `HOME_QUERY` already uses (D-1.05-3), so all three surfaces agree. Undated photos sort last rather than winning the hero by accident.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Add an `isLead` boolean or an order field* — rejected: the content model is **locked** (2.01) and this is out of scope; flagged for the model owner instead. *Sanity default (`_createdAt`)* — rejected: reflects data-entry order, not history, and would reshuffle on re-ingestion. *Newest-first* — rejected: inconsistent with the homepage and with an archive's oldest→newest reading.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: the "lead" is whichever photo has the earliest free-text `date` — **not an editorial choice**. `date` is free text („околу 1985"), so ordering within a season is lexical and a curator cannot currently promote a better lead photo without editing dates. If a curated lead is wanted, that is a model change (a rank or `isLead` field) — **surfaced for the 2.01 model owner**, not taken here.
- **Links:** Phase 2.03; `src/app/(site)/arhiva/page.tsx`; `src/app/(site)/arhiva/[slug]/page.tsx`; D-1.05-3; D-2.01-1; handover §4.5 / §13.

### D-2.03-4 · 2026-07-16 · Standings/empty-season states verified against local fixtures, not published content
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The DoD requires verifying the empty-section rule "against a mostly-empty published season" and the standings table against real data. The live `production` dataset has **exactly one** published season („Сезона 1992/93"), which has story/squad/trainers/photos but **no `finalTable`**, and there is no photo-less or fully-empty season at all. So three specified states had no content to render: the standings table (§6.4), the photo-less navy hero (§6.2b) and the fully-empty notice (§6.9).
- **Decision:** Verify the omission rule against the **real** season (its „Конечна табела" section is genuinely absent — confirmed in-browser), and verify the standings table + empty notice against a **temporary local fixture route**, deleted before commit. Do **not** publish test content to the shared `production` dataset to make the check pass.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Publish a throwaway season to `production`* — rejected: it is a shared live dataset feeding a public site, the content would be invented (content-truth), and creating it is not this phase's call. *Ship the states unverified* — rejected: the standings table is the most intricate component here (sticky columns, per-row backgrounds, the highlight marker). *Leave the fixture route in the repo* — rejected: it renders schematic non-facts and would be reachable in production.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: the photo-less hero, the fully-empty notice and the standings table are verified against **fixtures, not published documents** — they are correct in code and in the browser, but no one has yet seen them backed by real Sanity content. Recorded as **owed to Lazar** in the completion report; the natural clearing point is 2.09, when ~74 shell seasons make these the *common* states.
- **Links:** Phase 2.03; `src/components/archive/StandingsTable.tsx`; `src/components/archive/SeasonEmptyNotice.tsx`; handover §6.4 / §6.2b / §6.9.

### D-2.04-1 · 2026-07-17 · "Per-competition balance" reduced to a single all-time club balance
- **Status:** Accepted (orchestrator decision, pre-resolved in the 2.04 brief)
- **Context:** The Plan (§4) asks the Statistics page for a **per-competition** balance. The locked content model (2.01) has **no competition dimension**: `season.finalTable` rows carry `club` but no league/competition name, and `match` — the only type that could have carried one — was retired (D-2.01-2). A per-competition split could therefore only be produced by inventing the competition each season belonged to.
- **Decision:** The balance section aggregates **Belasica's own final-table row across every season that has a `finalTable`**, presented as one all-time total (the summary band) plus a season-by-season record. Not sliced by competition. Adding a `competition`/`league` field is a model change and is **not taken** — the model is locked.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Add a `competition` field* — rejected: the model is locked; a model change needs its own phase. *Infer the league from the season year* — rejected: that is invented content (content-truth), and Macedonian league history across 1922→2025 makes it guesswork. *Defer the balance section entirely* — rejected: the all-time balance is real, derivable from entered data, and is the section a visitor most expects.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: the page cannot answer „how did we do in the Cup vs the League" — every balance figure mixes whatever competitions the entered tables happen to represent. The coverage line states the season count so the aggregate is never read as complete. If a per-competition split is wanted later, it is a model change first, a page change second.
- **Links:** Phase 2.04; `src/lib/stats.ts`; `src/app/(site)/statistika/page.tsx`; Plan §4; D-2.01-2.

### D-2.04-2 · 2026-07-17 · No Statistics design exists; built from the handover's shared conventions + `brand.md`
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The 2.04 brief instructs: read "the section covering the Statistics page" in the 2.02 handover. **No such section exists.** The merged handover explicitly defers this page — §15 Out of scope: „**Not designed:** Legends, Statistics, About, Contact, and the person detail page — 2.04/2.05." The brief's premise is wrong, and there is no mockup, no layout and no component spec for `/statistika`.
- **Decision:** Derive the page from what **is** locked: the handover's shared conventions (§4.3 section heading, §4.4 links/focus, §4.5 ordering, §4.6 count labels), its Stats-table spec (§6.4 — navy header, zebra body, `—` for unknown, mobile horizontal scroll with a sticky first column, D-2.02-10/-14), and `brand.md` for every token. The page reuses `Breadcrumb`, `SectionHeading`, `statCell`, `isBelasicaRow`, `seasonCountLabel` and `focus.ts` rather than inventing a parallel visual language. **No new token, colour, font or spacing value was added.**
- **Alternatives considered:** *Stop and request a Statistics design phase* — considered seriously and rejected for scope: the brief pre-resolves the page's content (three named sections, named columns, named sort orders), so what was missing is visual direction, and the archive already fixes that for tables. *Design something new for this page* — rejected: a second table style on a site with one settled table style is drift, and a code phase is not the place to originate design. *Follow the in-prompt brief text as if it were the handover* — rejected: D-2.03-1 is the standing precedent — the merged handover + `brand.md` win over brief prose.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: `/statistika` ships **without a design review** — it is a faithful extension of the archive's table language, not a designed page, and Lazar sees it for the first time on the PR preview. If 2.05 produces a Statistics/Legends design that disagrees, this page is restyled then; the data layer (`src/lib/stats.ts`) is presentation-free and would survive. Flagged to the orchestrator: the 2.04 brief cites a handover section that does not exist and should be corrected before it is kept as history.
- **Links:** Phase 2.04; `docs/design-handovers/Part-2-Phase-02-Handover.md` §15 / §4 / §6.4; `brand.md`; D-2.03-1; D-2.02-10; D-2.02-14.

### D-2.04-3 · 2026-07-17 · Statistics sections keep their heading + an empty notice; they do not self-omit
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The archive's rule is **self-omission** — an empty section disappears (D-2.02-3), with one all-empty notice as the floor (D-2.02-8). The 2.04 brief asks for "graceful empty states … following the 2.03 self-omit philosophy", but `/statistika` has **exactly three sections and no hero**. With today's live data all three are empty, so literal self-omission would render an H1, a paragraph, and then the footer.
- **Decision:** Each section **keeps its heading** and renders a single `StatsEmptyNotice` — one sentence naming what is not entered yet, plus a `PlaceholderChip`. Never a zero row, never an empty table shell. This honours the *philosophy* (never fabricate, never render a broken shell) while rejecting the *mechanism*, which assumes a page with other content to stand on.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Omit each empty section* — rejected: today that yields a near-blank page that reads as broken, and it hides that the statistics layer exists at all. *Omit the sections and show one page-level notice* (the D-2.02-8 shape) — rejected: the three sections fill independently as 2.09 lands, so a single notice would be wrong the moment one of them populates. *Render an empty table with its headers* — rejected: a header row over no rows implies a queried-and-found-nothing result rather than not-yet-entered.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: `/statistika` **deviates from the archive's self-omit rule**, so the two page families now handle emptiness differently — an inconsistency a reader may trip on, and the reason this entry exists. As 2.09 populates the archive the notices disappear on their own.
- **Links:** Phase 2.04; `src/components/stats/StatsEmptyNotice.tsx`; `src/app/(site)/statistika/page.tsx`; D-2.02-3; D-2.02-8.

### D-2.04-4 · 2026-07-17 · Goal difference and win % are computed — for the aggregate band only, never as a table column
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** A direct conflict. The 2.04 brief **requires** the summary band to show "goal difference … and win %". The merged 2.02 handover §15 states the opposite: „Goal difference is *not* a field — it is not designed and **must not be computed**." Both are authoritative documents.
- **Decision:** Read the handover's prohibition in its own scope — it sits in §15 next to "every section maps to a locked field" and governs the **`finalTable` standings table** it designs, whose columns are exactly the locked row fields (`StandingsTable` has no GD column, and still does not). The all-time band is a different object: it is *by definition* derived arithmetic (sums across seasons), so GD = ГД − ПГ and win % = Поб ÷ Од are the same class of computation as the sums beside them, on values an editor actually entered, each under an explicit label. Accordingly: **GD and win % appear in the balance summary band only**; the season-by-season table keeps the standings columns and adds no GD.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Omit GD and win % entirely* — rejected: it would gut the brief's summary band and drop the two figures that make a balance readable, over a line scoped to a different component. *Add a GD column to the season-by-season table* — rejected: that is squarely what §15 forbids, and the per-season table mirrors `finalTable`. *Escalate before building* — the conflict is narrow and the scoping reading is clean; recorded here instead so it can be reversed cheaply.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: an auditor reading §15 literally will find computed goal difference on the site and must land on this entry to see why. Both figures return `—` rather than `0` when the underlying columns were never entered (a win % needs both halves of the fraction), so neither can state a total the archive does not have.
- **Links:** Phase 2.04; `src/lib/stats.ts` (`formatGoalDifference`, `formatWinRate`); `src/components/stats/BalanceSummary.tsx`; `src/components/archive/StandingsTable.tsx`; handover §15 / §6.4.

### D-2.04-5 · 2026-07-17 · Unknown values sort last in both directions
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** Every stats column is client-sortable, and the archive renders an unrecorded number as `—` (`statCell`, D-2.02-9). A naive comparator treats null as 0 or as "smallest", which puts „unknown" at the top of an **ascending** „Настапи" sort — presenting a player whose appearances were never entered as the player with the fewest.
- **Decision:** In `StatTable`, a null sort value ranks **last regardless of direction**. A missing value is unknown, not small: it is excluded from the ordering rather than assigned a rank. Ranked rows are always the ones with data.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Treat null as 0* — rejected: it invents a fact, the exact failure `statCell` exists to prevent. *Sort nulls first when ascending (strict comparator symmetry)* — rejected: symmetry is not the goal; not making a claim is. *Drop rows with a null in the sorted column* — rejected: the row's other numbers are real archive data and must not vanish because one cell is blank.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: sorting is not a strict reversal — flipping direction does not simply mirror the row order, which a user comparing the two views may notice. Verified in-browser against fixtures: „Настапи" desc → `180, 30, 12, —`; asc → `12, 30, 180, —`.
- **Links:** Phase 2.04; `src/components/stats/StatTable.tsx` (`compare`); `src/lib/archive.ts` (`statCell`); D-2.02-9.

### D-2.04-6 · 2026-07-17 · All three Statistics tables are fixture-verified, not content-verified
- **Status:** Accepted (anticipated by the 2.04 brief)
- **Context:** Same shape as D-2.03-4, one phase later. Live `production` has one published season with **no `finalTable`**, and neither published player has `careerStats`. So with real content all three sections render their empty notice — correct and expected per the brief — and **no table, no summary band and no sort control is exercised by published data**.
- **Decision:** Verify the empty states against **real** content (all three notices confirmed in-browser), and verify the tables, the aggregate and sorting against a **temporary in-file fixture**, stripped before commit. Do not publish test content to the shared `production` dataset to make the check pass.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Publish a throwaway season/player with stats* — rejected: `production` is live and public-read, the numbers would be invented (content-truth), and ingestion is 2.09's call. *Ship the tables unverified* — rejected: the aggregate is the one place this phase can produce a confidently wrong number. *Keep a fixture route in the repo* — rejected: it renders non-facts and would be reachable in production.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: the tables, the summary band, the coverage lines and the sorting are correct in code and in the browser but **have never rendered from published Sanity content**. Fixtures did cover the cases that matter: a 0-goal player (renders `0`, not `—`), a null-stat player, a player with no slug (name, no link), a person with no name (placeholder chip), a partially-entered season row, and a season whose table names no Беласица row (excluded from the aggregate **and** disclosed). Clears naturally at 2.09; sooner if one season with a `finalTable` and one player with `careerStats` are published.
- **Links:** Phase 2.04; `src/lib/stats.ts`; `src/components/stats/StatTable.tsx`; D-2.03-4; D-2.01-3.

### D-2.04-7 · 2026-07-17 · Merged without the Vercel preview gate (owner-directed)
- **Status:** Accepted (owner decision, in-session)
- **Context:** `CLAUDE.md` §Branch & PR rules requires the Vercel preview to load before merge, and D-1.01-4 dropped the review Action — so **the preview returning 200 is one of only two gates this project has** (the other being Lazar's diff review). For PR #14 the preview **never deployed**: Vercel created no deployment for `phase-2.04-statistics` (`DEPLOYMENT_NOT_FOUND` on both branch-alias forms — not a build error), GitHub's REST endpoints for the repo returned 500 „Unicorn!" pages for the whole session while GraphQL stayed up, and the PR showed zero checks. An empty-commit retrigger (`3d25549`) was pushed and polled for ~3.5 minutes: still no deployment. The push never reached Vercel's webhook.
- **Decision:** Lazar was told the gate was open and the preview unobtainable, and directed „merge it". Merged PR #14 to `main` **without a preview check**, on the evidence available: `npm run build` + `npm run lint` clean on the merged commit, `/statistika` rendered and verified at 1280 + 375 on `localhost:3000` (contrast sweep ≥ 10.37:1, sorting/`aria-sort`/keyboard, mobile scroll frames), and the route prerenders static.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Wait for Vercel/GitHub to recover* — rejected by the owner; the outage is third-party with no ETA and the branch is otherwise finished. *Redeploy by hand from the Vercel dashboard* — not available to the executor (no Vercel credentials); still open to Lazar. *Merge and treat the gate as satisfied* — rejected: it was not satisfied, and recording it as such would make the register lie.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: **`/statistika` reached `main` having never been served by Vercel.** Local build ≠ preview, so a Vercel-only failure (env vars, image domains, ISR on their runtime) would surface first in **production**. Two mitigations: the same webhook outage likely also delays the production deploy, so there is a window to check; and the page is static + additive — no existing route's code changed. **Owed:** confirm `https://belasica-v2.vercel.app/statistika` returns 200 once Vercel catches up (tracked as **OV-5**). Does not supersede the `CLAUDE.md` rule — this is a one-off waiver for a third-party outage, not a precedent.
- **Links:** Phase 2.04; [PR #14](https://github.com/DinovLazar/belasica-v2/pull/14); commit `3d25549` (retrigger); `CLAUDE.md` §Branch & PR rules; D-1.01-4; D-2.04-6; OV-5.

### D-2.06-1 · 2026-07-17 · The 2.05 handover was never in the repo; it was landed from the owner and built to
- **Status:** Accepted (owner decision, in-session)
- **Context:** The 2.06 brief names `docs/design-handovers/Part-2-Phase-05-Handover.md` as its instruction source for §1–§9 (all three routes + all three component specs). **That file had never existed** — `git log --diff-filter=AD` over `docs/design-handovers/` shows only the 1.05 homepage and the 2.02 archive handovers ever committed. `current-state.md` line 1 still read `NEXT: 2.05 — People & pages design`, the design phase this brief presumes is finished; the 2.02 handover §15 explicitly defers Legends/About/`/legendi/<slug>` as „Not designed"; and `decisions.md` ended at D-2.04-7 with no **D-2.05-1/-2/-3**, all three of which the brief and handover cite as settled. This is the third instance of the pattern the brief itself flags as „this project's most repeated mistake" (D-2.03-1, D-2.04-2) — but one phase worse, since the source was absent rather than stale. The executor stopped before writing code and escalated.
- **Decision:** Lazar supplied the handover file directly (`~/Downloads/Part-2-Phase-05-Handover.md`). It was **copied into the repo** at `docs/design-handovers/Part-2-Phase-05-Handover.md` and committed as part of this phase, and the build followed it. Its §0 („if any convention disagrees with the merged 2.02 handover or `brand.md`, **the repo wins**") governed every conflict below.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Build from 2.02 conventions + `brand.md` alone (the D-2.04-2 precedent)* — was the fallback had no handover arrived; rejected once one did, and it scaled badly here (three routes + three net-new component specs vs. 2.04's single page). *Stop and run 2.05 design first* — the executor's recommendation while the file was missing, and the answer Lazar first gave; superseded when he produced the file, which **is** the 2.05 design output. *Build the person page only and defer the rest* — unnecessary once the full spec existed.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: **the 2.05 design phase still has no completion report and no decisions of its own.** `D-2.05-1` (About/Contact provisional), `D-2.05-2` (multi-role priority) and `D-2.05-3` (Contact/Formspree) are referenced by this build and by the handover but exist **nowhere in `decisions.md`** — they are described only inside the handover prose. This phase logs its own decisions under `D-2.06-*` and does **not** back-fill someone else's namespace. If 2.05 is ever formally closed, those three IDs must be filed to match the handover, or the references here dangle. The handover also cites a mockup (`People & Pages.dc.html`) that was **not** supplied; the build worked from the handover text alone.
- **Links:** Phase 2.06; `docs/design-handovers/Part-2-Phase-05-Handover.md` (§0); D-2.03-1; D-2.04-2; 2.02 handover §15.

### D-2.06-2 · 2026-07-17 · UI copy is Macedonian — „Сезони" / „Фотографии" / „Сите легенди", not the handover's Serbian
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The 2.05 handover writes its section labels in **Serbian**: „Сезоне", „Фотографије", „← Све легенде" (and §1 says outright „all UI copy is **Serbian**/Macedonian Cyrillic"). The repo is Macedonian and says so three ways: `CLAUDE.md` §Copy phases („Macedonian copy only; `lang="mk"`"), `brand.md`'s own Cyrillic test string („Архива по **сезони**"), and the shipped code — the season page renders „**Фотографии**" and `BalanceSummary.tsx` renders `label: "Сезони"`. The two languages differ in exactly these plural endings, so following the handover would have put Serbian labels beside Macedonian ones on the same site.
- **Decision:** Macedonian throughout, per handover §0's own „the repo wins" rule: „Сезони", „Фотографии", „Сите легенди". „Биографија" and „Кариера" are identical in both and needed no change.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Follow the handover literally* — rejected: it breaks the `CLAUDE.md` standing rule and would ship „Фотографије" on `/legendi/<slug>` directly above a footer and season page reading „Фотографии". *Ask Lazar first* — not needed; three independent in-repo sources agree, so this is reconciliation, not a judgement call.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: this phase's labels now **diverge from the handover text**, so a reader comparing the two will find mismatches on every section heading. The handover should be corrected rather than the code. No downside on the page itself — the site reads as one language for the first time across these routes.
- **Links:** Phase 2.06; `CLAUDE.md` §Copy phases; `brand.md` §Typography (Cyrillic test string); `src/components/stats/BalanceSummary.tsx`; handover §0/§1/§3.

### D-2.06-3 · 2026-07-17 · „Сезони" reads `squad` **and** `trainers`, not `season.squad` alone
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** Handover §3/§8 says the Сезони section reads „seasons this person appears in, **read from `season.squad`**". Taken literally that is players-only: a trainer is linked from a season via `season.trainers[]`, not `squad[]`. Live content makes the gap concrete — Гоце Петровски and Илија Андреев are both trainers on „Сезона 1992/93", and under a squad-only read their Сезони section would self-omit, leaving each page as a hero + a back-link while the season page links **to** them. That one-directional dead end is precisely what the brief's „Why this matters" says this phase exists to remove, and §3 insists the page „applies to EVERY published person" and that a trainer must still be „a complete, non-404 page".
- **Decision:** Match on either arm — `^._id in squad[].player._ref || ^._id in trainers[]._ref`. §3's stated *intent* („seasons this person appears in") is honoured over its stated *mechanism* („`season.squad`"), which appears to have been written with players in mind.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Squad-only, per the literal spec* — rejected: it makes the trainer pages the dead ends this phase is meant to fix, and „appears in" is plainly true of a trainer. *Squad-only now, widen in a later phase* — rejected: same code, same data, no reason to ship a knowingly worse page. *Label the two arms differently on the page („играч" vs „тренер" seasons)* — rejected as scope: the handover specs one flat chip list, and the role is already on the hero.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: the section is a **strict superset of the spec**, so a reader diffing code against §3 will find an extra `||` arm. Both arms read real references and invent nothing. The chips do not say *why* a person is tied to a season, so a trainer's „Сезона 1992/93" chip looks identical to a player's — acceptable, since the hero already states the role. Reversing this is a one-line change if the orchestrator disagrees.
- **Links:** Phase 2.06; handover §3/§8; `src/app/(site)/legendi/[slug]/page.tsx` (`PERSON_QUERY`); D-2.01-1.

### D-2.06-4 · 2026-07-17 · `/za-nas`'s optional hero is omitted — the locked model has no field to hold it
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** Handover §4 specs an „**Optional hero portrait** (`PhotoFrame`, `fit="cover"`), only if a photo is supplied — omits otherwise", and §4 Responsive describes it at 16:9 / 16:10. There is nowhere for that photo to come from: `siteSettings` carries only `title`, `description` and `footerUnofficialArchiveText`; `photo` links to a season or a person, never to a page; and the model is **LOCKED** (2.01) with schema changes explicitly out of scope for this phase.
- **Decision:** The hero is not built. §4's own „omits otherwise" is the rendered state, permanently, until a field exists.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Add a `siteSettings.aboutImage` field* — rejected: a schema change the brief forbids and the lock reserves for its own phase. *Reuse an arbitrary existing photo (e.g. a team shot)* — rejected: it would assert that photo is „about" the archive, which no fact supports — invented content by placement. *Render a `[PLACEHOLDER]` hero frame* — rejected: nothing is *missing*; no one has ever specified a photo for this page, so a chip would report a gap that does not exist and would sit oddly beside the four real PL-1/PL-2 chips.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: the About page ships **flatter than designed** — a text column with no image, which is a real (if quiet) visual shortfall against §4. Whoever revisits About at 3.03 (when PL-1/PL-2 clear) should decide the hero and its field together, since the copy and the image are the same conversation with Ace.
- **Links:** Phase 2.06; handover §4; D-2.01-1; `src/sanity/schemaTypes/siteSettings.ts`.

### D-2.06-5 · 2026-07-17 · Token reconciliation: card-lift carries no shadow; the placeholder chip is not amber
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** Handover §0 requires every token name in §1 to be reconciled against the real `brand.md` before building, since the design was produced without that file present. Two §1/§4 conventions have **no token behind them**: (1) §1 specs card-lift as „subtle `translateY` + **softened shadow**", but `brand.md` §Components says the season card's hover is a „2px lift (**no shadow — there is no shadow token**)"; (2) §4/§9 twice call the `[PLACEHOLDER]` chip **amber**, and there is no amber anywhere in `brand.md` — the real convention is „dashed mist border, hatched fill, mono text", already implemented as `PlaceholderChip`. §9 anticipates exactly this and says to use the existing convention rather than add a token.
- **Decision:** Repo wins on both. Cards lift 2px with no shadow (`hover:-translate-y-0.5`, matching `SeasonCard`); placeholders reuse `PlaceholderChip` untouched. **No `brand.md` token was added** — §9's expected outcome. Full mapping of every §1 name to its real token is in the completion report.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Add a shadow token to `brand.md`* — rejected: `brand.md` names the absence deliberately („there is no shadow token"), the archive's flat dignity is the point, and one new card would not justify a site-wide token. *Add an amber token for placeholders* — rejected: §9 itself says the chip „never ships as real content", and a second placeholder style would fork a convention used on `/`, `/arhiva` and `/statistika`.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: the new cards are **visually flatter than the handover describes**, and the placeholder chips are mist/hatched rather than the amber the handover's mockup presumably shows — so anyone checking the build against the mockup will read both as misses. They are not: they are the repo's own rules, and re-introducing either would break D-1.02-1's sibling constraint that the archive's surfaces stay flat and its placeholders stay uniform.
- **Links:** Phase 2.06; handover §0/§1/§4/§9; `brand.md` §Components; `src/components/home/PlaceholderChip.tsx`; `src/components/archive/SeasonCard.tsx`.

### D-2.06-6 · 2026-07-17 · Кариера replicates `BalanceSummary`'s visual language; the component itself is not reused
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** Handover §3/§7 says to render `careerStats` „in the **`BalanceSummary`** visual language (large navy stat tiles)" and lists `BalanceSummary` under reused components. The actual component takes a `ClubBalance` — a ten-figure, `stats.ts`-shaped aggregate with coverage tracking and `formatGoalDifference`/`formatWinRate` — and is hardcoded to `lg:grid-cols-5`. A person has at most two figures and no `ClubBalance` to hand.
- **Decision:** The Кариера section renders the **same tile treatment** inline (white tile on a mist grid, overline label, serif navy figure, `tabular-nums`) sized to 1–2 tiles. `BalanceSummary` is left untouched. Note the handover's „large **navy** stat tiles" is loose: the real tiles are **white with a navy figure**, and the repo's actual rendering wins.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Generalise `BalanceSummary` to take arbitrary figures* — rejected: refactoring a stats component, which the brief puts out of scope („no existing home/archive/stats component changed except additive reuse"), to serve one new caller. *Fabricate a `ClubBalance` to pass in* — rejected: a nonsense shape carrying eight empty columns. *Extract a shared `StatTiles`* — deferred, not rejected: the right move once a third caller exists; two is not yet a pattern.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: the tile markup now exists **twice** (here and in `BalanceSummary`), so a future change to the tile's look must be made in both places or they drift. Accepted as the cheaper of the two risks — the alternative was touching a shipped stats component this phase is told not to touch. Flagged for the extraction if a third caller appears.
- **Links:** Phase 2.06; handover §3/§7; `src/components/stats/BalanceSummary.tsx`; `src/lib/stats.ts`.

### D-2.06-7 · 2026-07-17 · A photo-less `LegendCard` gets a navy initials tile — deliberately unlike the season card's greybox
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** Handover §1/§2 says „a photoless item gets a **solid navy title tile**, never a greybox (**D-2.02-2/3**)". Two problems. First, the citation is **wrong**: D-2.02-2 is „Archive orders seasons by `slug.current desc`" and D-2.02-3 is the section-heading rule — neither is about photos. The real navy-band rule is 2.02 **§6.2b**, and it has no decision ID. (`current-state.md` repeats the same mis-citation, which is where it appears to have come from.) Second, 2.02 §5.5/§7 says the opposite for *cards*: a photo-less **season card** keeps `PhotoFrame`'s „Mist greybox + chip" — the navy band is specified for the **hero** only.
- **Decision:** `LegendCard` uses a solid navy tile with the person's initials; `SeasonCard` is untouched and keeps its greybox. The two are different components with different content: a person has initials, so a monogram reads as a deliberate plate; a season has none, so its greybox is the honest empty frame. `PersonHero`'s photo-less variant follows §6.2b's navy band, consistent with the season hero.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Greybox + chip for consistency with `SeasonCard`* — rejected: it contradicts the 2.05 handover's explicit §2 „States" spec for this new component, and most trainers/officials will never have a portrait, so the common state would be a wall of greyboxes. *Navy tiles on season cards too, for site-wide consistency* — rejected: changes a shipped 2.03 component on no mandate, and there is no season monogram to put in the tile.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: `/legendi` and `/arhiva` now handle „no photo" **differently** — navy monogram vs. mist greybox — so the site has two photo-less card treatments. Justified by content (initials exist / do not), and both avoid a broken-looking card. The two mis-cited decision IDs in the handover (and in `current-state.md`) should be corrected to „2.02 §6.2b" — recorded here rather than silently followed.
- **Links:** Phase 2.06; handover §1/§2; 2.02 handover §5.5/§6.2b/§7; D-2.02-2 (the actual entry); `src/components/legends/LegendCard.tsx`.

### D-2.06-8 · 2026-07-17 · A missing `playingYears` placeholders for players only; trainers and officials omit the line
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** `playingYears` is optional in the locked model, and `LegendCard`/`PersonHero` must decide what „absent" means. The homepage sets a precedent — its legends grid renders `[PLACEHOLDER: години на играње]` when a player has no years — but that grid is filtered to players, so it never had to answer the question for anyone else. `/legendi` shows all three roles, and live content has exactly this case: Гоце and Илија are trainers with `playingYears: null`.
- **Decision:** The years line renders a placeholder chip only when the person's **primary role is `player`**; for a trainer or president with no years the line omits entirely.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Always placeholder when absent* — rejected: it would stamp „[PLACEHOLDER: години на играње]" on every trainer card, asserting a gap in the archive where none exists — a trainer who never played has no playing years to be missing. That is a false claim about the content, which content-truth forbids as much as inventing a value. *Never placeholder* — rejected: for a player the years genuinely are a known-missing fact, and it would silently drop the homepage's precedent.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: the rule is **role-dependent**, so two cards with identical `null` data render differently — which looks inconsistent until you know why. The alternative was a card that lies. A player who was *also* a trainer still placeholders (player is the higher-priority role), which is correct: he did play.
- **Links:** Phase 2.06; `src/app/(site)/page.tsx` (homepage legends precedent); `src/components/legends/LegendCard.tsx`; `src/lib/people.ts`.

### D-2.06-9 · 2026-07-17 · `/za-nas` renders the OV-3 copy from `facts.md`, not the divergent `siteSettings` field (→ OV-6)
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The brief requires the OV-3 framing „verbatim from `facts.md` (byte-identical)". While sourcing it, a conflict surfaced: `SiteFooter` **hardcodes** „Ова е неофицијална архива посветена на ФК Беласица. Не е официјалната страница на клубот." — which is what `facts.md` records as VERIFIED, noting the wording is „exact, **as rendered**" and that Ace „confirmed this **current** wording". But `siteSettings.footerUnofficialArchiveText` is populated in the live `production` dataset with **different** text („Ова е неофицијална архива на историјата на ФК Беласица, создадена од љубов кон клубот. Сајтот не е официјална страница на клубот и не е поврзан со него.") that **no page reads**. Since it has never rendered, Ace was never shown it — so it is *not* the VERIFIED string, despite living in the CMS and looking authoritative.
- **Decision:** The verified strings were transcribed into a new `src/lib/facts.ts` and rendered from there; `/za-nas` does not read `siteSettings`. Byte-identity against `facts.md` was verified by script, not by eye (Cyrillic homoglyphs make visual comparison unreliable). `SiteFooter` is **left alone** (out of scope) and still holds its own hardcoded copy.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Read `siteSettings.footerUnofficialArchiveText`* — rejected: it would ship unverified copy on the one statement the whole site's honesty rests on, and would silently contradict the footer on the same page. *Hardcode the string a third time in `za-nas/page.tsx`* — rejected: three copies of a verified fact is three places to drift. *Point `SiteFooter` at `@/lib/facts` too* — the right end state, but a refactor of site chrome outside this phase's scope; the 2.03 precedent (`focus.ts` created, homepage's inline copies left alone and noted) is followed instead.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: the OV-3 string still exists **twice in code** (`lib/facts.ts` + `SiteFooter`'s hardcode), plus a **third, divergent, unverified** copy sitting in Sanity that an editor can see and might reasonably assume is live. That last one is the real hazard and is now tracked as **OV-6**: either delete the field, or correct its text and wire both surfaces to it, before anyone wires it up by accident.
- **Links:** Phase 2.06; `facts.md` §Naming & identity (OV-3); `src/lib/facts.ts`; `src/components/SiteFooter.tsx`; OV-6; D-2.03-2 (the `focus.ts` scope precedent).

### D-2.07-1 · 2026-07-18 · Contact form copy is Macedonian, not the handover's Serbian
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The 2.05 handover §5 specs the form's button copy in **Serbian**: „Пошаљи" / „Шаље се…" / „Хвала!" / „Пошаљи поново". The repo is Macedonian-only (`CLAUDE.md` §Copy phases, `brand.md`'s Cyrillic test string „по **сезони**", `lang="mk"`), and 2.06 already made the same correction for its own Serbian labels (D-2.06-2). The 2.07 brief pre-resolves this in its „Handover corrections" table.
- **Decision:** Render the four states in Macedonian: **„Испрати" / „Се испраќа…" / „Ви благодариме!" / „Испрати повторно"**, per handover §0's own „the repo wins" rule. The success line and the disabled/error copy are Macedonian too.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Follow the handover verbatim* — rejected: it ships Serbian strings on a Macedonian site, breaking a `CLAUDE.md` standing rule. *Ask Lazar first* — not needed; three in-repo sources plus the brief's own correction table already agree, so this is reconciliation, not a judgement call.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: the shipped strings **diverge from the handover text**, so a reader diffing spec-vs-code finds mismatches on every button label. The handover should be corrected, not the code. The four Serbian strings appear nowhere in the diff.
- **Links:** Phase 2.07; handover §5/§6.4; `CLAUDE.md` §Copy phases; D-2.06-2; `src/components/contact/ContactForm.tsx`.

### D-2.07-2 · 2026-07-18 · The Formspree endpoint is a public form action read from `NEXT_PUBLIC_FORMSPREE_ENDPOINT`; `.env.example` is committed via a `.gitignore` negation
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The form needs a destination URL, wired as config in 3.03 (D-0.00-7). A Formspree endpoint (`https://formspree.io/f/<id>`) is a **public form action** — it ships in the browser bundle by design and is not a secret, exactly like the existing `NEXT_PUBLIC_SANITY_*` values (D-1.04-2). The repo is public, so nothing secret may be committed. Separately, the repo's `.gitignore` ignores `.env*`, which would also ignore a new `.env.example` template.
- **Decision:** Read `process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_FORMSPREE_ENDPOINT` server-side in the page and pass it to `ContactForm`. Add `.env.example` at the repo root with the key **empty** and a one-line comment, and add `!.env.example` to `.gitignore` so the template is trackable while `.env.local` stays ignored. **No secret or token was added.** The value is not set in `.env.local` or Vercel — that is 3.03's step.
- **Alternatives considered:** *A non-`NEXT_PUBLIC_` var read only server-side* — rejected: the value is genuinely public (it is the form's `action`), and the client component must submit to it, so a server-only var would force an extra plumbing layer for no security gain. *Skip `.env.example`* — rejected: the brief requires a committed template so 3.03 (and any operator) sees the exact var name. *Force-add `.env.example` with `-f` each time* — rejected: fragile and undiscoverable; a one-line `.gitignore` negation is the standard, self-documenting fix.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: the `.gitignore` now carries a negation that a future reader must understand (only `.env.example` is un-ignored; all real `.env*` stay ignored — verified with `git add --dry-run`). If a later phase adds another committed env template it must be negated too.
- **Links:** Phase 2.07; D-0.00-7; D-1.04-2; `.env.example`; `.gitignore`; `src/app/(site)/kontakt/page.tsx`.

### D-2.07-3 · 2026-07-18 · Unset endpoint → the form renders visibly disabled, never enabled-but-silently-failing
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** Until 3.03 supplies the endpoint, `NEXT_PUBLIC_FORMSPREE_ENDPOINT` is unset — which is the **current production state**. An enabled form pointed at nothing would let a visitor type a real message, submit, and get either a silent failure or a thrown error. The brief (task 5) makes this „the important state".
