### D-3.03-3 · 2026-07-23 · `clubRecord` order = category priority (honours→appearances→scorers) then `order`; championship pulled out as a feature
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The brief says render the records "ordered by `category` then `order`". Strict alphabetical category order (appearances < honours < scorers) would bury the two league titles behind the appearances record; the `order` field was authored as a global 1–7 sequence that already leads with honours.
- **Decision:** Order by a category **priority** — honours (0) → appearances (1) → scorers (2) → other (3) — then by `order` within each, with a Cyrillic-name final tiebreak for determinism. The first record (Шампион на Македонија, order 1) is rendered as a full-width feature; the remaining six fall into an even 3-column ledger. Neither `label` nor `value` is reformatted (a record is a factual claim, shipped as curated).
- **Alternatives considered:** *Strict alphabetical category then order* — rejected: leads with the appearances record and buries the titles; not the owner's evident intent (the global `order` starts at the championship). *Order by `order` alone* — equivalent result today; the explicit category-priority is more robust if new records are added with local `order` values.
- **Consequences:** The navy records band leads with the two titles, reads as a records board, and stays deterministic. If a future record needs a different position, set its `category` + `order`.
- **Links:** Phase 3.03; `src/components/home/ClubRecords.tsx`; D-3.01-5; completion report §4.

### D-3.03-4 · 2026-07-23 · "Момент од историјата" photo selected deterministically: captioned + season-anchored + landscape, oldest era then widest crop
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** Section 6 is "one full-bleed real published archival photograph." It must be deterministic, genuinely historical, and distinct from the hero (the modern 2025/26 team photo).
- **Decision:** Select in GROQ: `photo` with a non-empty `caption`, a `relatedSeason`, and landscape aspect (`aspectRatio > 1.2`), ordered `relatedSeason->decade asc, aspectRatio desc, _id asc`, take `[0]`. This resolves today to the 1993 Macedonian-Cup photo („Младата екипа на Беласица со Купот на Македонија, 1993"). A caption is the editor's curation signal (the 2.08 lever); ordering oldest-era-first structurally excludes the modern hero photo and yields a genuinely historical moment; widest-crop-first prefers a photo that reads well full-bleed.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Reuse the featured season's 2nd photo (the old homepage rule)* — rejected: not necessarily historical, and could collide with the hero. *Hardcode the photo `_id`* — rejected: brittle; a GROQ rule survives content edits. *Any captioned photo (no season anchor)* — rejected: a season anchor gives a real date/era and keeps it archival.
- **Consequences:** Deterministic and evocative today; if a new older captioned landscape season-photo is published it would take precedence — an acceptable, content-driven change (logged so a future reader knows the moment is rule-selected, not fixed).
- **Links:** Phase 3.03; `HOME_QUERY` (`moment`); D-2.08-3 (caption-as-curation); completion report §4.

### D-3.03-5 · 2026-07-23 · New `SectionOverline` variant `onPhoto` — orange marker + PAPER text over photos (orange text there fails AA)
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** brand.md §Components describes the hero overline as an "orange overline", and the pre-3.03 `SectionOverline` `onNavy` variant renders orange text (valid on SOLID navy at 4.6:1). But the hero and the moment band are orange text over a **photo + navy gradient**. Pixel-sampling the built hero showed the overline sits over a light photo pixel `[224,206,184]` where even a heavy gradient leaves orange at ~3.7:1 — below the 4.5 AA floor for 12px text. This is exactly the case D-1.02-1 forbids (orange text only where AA holds).
- **Decision:** Add an `onPhoto` variant: the orange **rule marker** (non-text, always fine) + **paper** overline text (measured ≥ 6.8:1 over the worst-case pixel with the chosen gradient). Use it on the hero and the moment band; keep `onNavy` (orange text) only on the SOLID-navy records band (4.68:1, measured). D-1.02-1 (AA) wins over brand.md's looser "orange overline" phrasing — same reconciliation as D-1.03-1.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Darken the gradient until orange reaches AA* — rejected: would require near-solid navy over the overline band, killing the photo-forward hero. *Keep orange text and accept ~3.7:1* — rejected: an AA failure and a D-1.02-1 violation. *Drop the overline* — rejected: it carries the "неофицијална архива" identity the brief wants in/near the hero.
- **Consequences:** The hero/moment overlines read in paper with the orange marker preserved; orange text survives only where it truly passes (solid navy). Hero and moment gradients were tuned (`via-navy/75 via-60%` / `via-navy/70 via-55%`) to keep the photo visible while paper text stays ≥ AA.
- **Links:** Phase 3.03; `src/components/home/SectionOverline.tsx`; D-1.02-1; D-1.03-1; brand.md §Contrast; completion report §5.

### D-3.03-6 · 2026-07-23 · Retired `DecadeTimeline` (dot rail) for `DecadeExplore` (decade grid with real counts)
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The old homepage used `DecadeTimeline` — a horizontal dot rail marking which decades have seasons, all linking to `/arhiva`. The brief's section 5 wants "a clean entry into the 1922→2025 span" grouping seasons by decade.
- **Decision:** Replace the rail with `DecadeExplore` — a grid of decade tiles, each showing the decade label + a real season count (`seasonCountLabel`, Macedonian pluralisation) and deep-linking to that decade's section on `/arhiva` (`#d{decade}`, which the archive already renders as an `id`). `DecadeTimeline.tsx` (homepage-only) was deleted.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Keep the dot rail* — rejected: it conveys less (no counts, no per-decade entry) and reads more like a decoration than an archive doorway. *Keep both* — rejected: two decade widgets on one page is redundant.
- **Consequences:** A more useful, information-bearing archive entry; one fewer component to maintain. `file-map.md` updated (removed `DecadeTimeline`, added `DecadeExplore` + `ClubRecords`).
- **Links:** Phase 3.03; `src/components/home/DecadeExplore.tsx`; removed `DecadeTimeline.tsx`; D-1.05.2-3; completion report §4.

### D-3.04-1 · 2026-07-26 · Prev/next season navigation added, ordered by `decade` then `slug.current` — not by `title` as the brief says
- **Status:** Accepted (executor decision)
- **Context:** The 2.02 handover §15 explicitly deferred prev/next („a natural fit for an archive spine and the data supports it… **not designed**"); the 3.04 brief now requires it and specifies „order by `decade` then `title`". Sorting the 96 live titles that way is **chronologically wrong in six places**, because the titles are not uniform: „Беласица 1945–1948“ sorts *before* „Сезона 1940/41“ (Б precedes С), „Сезона 1950/51“ before „Сезона 1950“ (`/` precedes „), and the one hand-entered title without guillemets (`1992-93`, D-2.09-4) sorts before every quoted 1990s title.
- **Decision:** Resolve the neighbours in GROQ over `decade asc, slug.current asc` — the **slug** key, which is the key the archive index already orders by (D-2.02-2). Verified by walking the whole spine: exactly one season with no previous (`1922-26`), exactly one with no next (`2025-26`), a single unbroken 96-link chain, and every link symmetric (`next(prev(x)) == x`). The three eras title-ordering got wrong (1940s, 1950s, 1990s) all come out chronologically correct.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Follow the brief and order by `title`* — rejected: it demonstrably links six seasons to the wrong neighbour, and the archive index would disagree with the season page about what „next" means. *Normalise the titles in Sanity so title-ordering works* — rejected: this phase writes no content (the Cowork brief owns it), and the slug is already the canonical, normalised sort key. *Order by `decade` + a parsed start year* — rejected: parsing „Беласица 1922–1926“ into a year is derivation, and the slug encodes the same thing without guessing.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: the ordering key silently diverges from the brief's literal wording, so a future reader must read this entry to understand why. In exchange the spine is provably correct, and it stays correct for era-range and single-year seasons, which are exactly where title-ordering breaks. If a future slug is not year-leading, the chain would need re-verifying.
- **Links:** Phase 3.04; `src/app/(site)/arhiva/[slug]/page.tsx` (`SEASON_QUERY`); `src/components/archive/SeasonNeighbourNav.tsx`; D-2.02-2; D-2.09-4; 2.02 handover §15; completion report §4.

### D-3.04-2 · 2026-07-26 · „Фотографии" now EXCLUDES `teamPhoto` and `tablePhoto` — supersedes D-2.02-6
- **Status:** Accepted — supersedes D-2.02-6
- **Context:** D-2.02-6 deliberately repeated the lead photo in the grid. Its whole argument was that **the hero showed no caption**, so excluding it would hide that photo's `caption` and `date` from the site for good. The 3.04 lead is a different object: `teamPhoto`/`tablePhoto` are matted with their **own `figcaption`** rendered beneath them, so the caption is on the page either way. The brief also requires the exclusion.
- **Decision:** Exclude both lead photos from the gallery in GROQ by document `_id` (`!(_id in [^.teamPhoto._ref, ^.tablePhoto._ref])`). Verified across all 96 seasons: 713 gallery photos, zero seasons where a lead `_id` reappears, and the 5 seasons whose only photos are the two leads correctly render no „Фотографии" section at all.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Keep D-2.02-6's repeat* — rejected: its stated reason no longer holds, and with two lead images the page would now repeat **two** photos, not one. *Exclude only when the lead has a caption* — rejected: D-2.02-6 already rejected this shape as "conditional, surprising, and it makes the grid's contents depend on an unrelated field".
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: a photo's `date` is shown in the gallery but not under a lead (the lead renders `caption` only), so a lead photo with a `date` and no `caption` loses its date line. No published lead photo is in that state today (exactly one of the 83 `teamPhoto`s carries a caption; none carry a bare date).
- **Links:** Phase 3.04; supersedes D-2.02-6; `src/app/(site)/arhiva/[slug]/page.tsx`; D-3.04-10; completion report §4.

### D-3.04-3 · 2026-07-26 · Lead photos are matted at their OWN aspect ratio (new `MattedPhoto`), not dropped into a fixed-ratio `PhotoFrame`
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The brief says to render `teamPhoto`/`tablePhoto` „matted via `PhotoFrame`… `fit="contain"`". Measured against the live assets, the two fields are not one shape: `teamPhoto` aspect ratios run **0.49 → 2.63** (83 assets) and `tablePhoto` **0.36 → 2.86** (87 assets). A 0.36 standings scan inside a fixed 3:2 or 16:9 `contain` frame is a thin strip on a mat several times its own area — which reads as broken, not as deliberate, and at that scale the standings are unreadable, defeating the point of showing the table as an image (D-3.01-2).
- **Decision:** Add `src/components/archive/MattedPhoto.tsx`: a real mat (mist ground, hairline border, uniform padding) sized by the asset's **intrinsic** `metadata.dimensions`, which the GROQ now fetches. It never renders wider than the asset's native pixel width (a low-res scan is matted, never upscaled — brand.md's mixed-quality rule) and takes an optional `maxHeightRem` cap. The lead photo caps at 40rem so a 1960×4032 portrait scan cannot become a two-viewport lead; the table image is uncapped, because legibility is the whole reason it is an image. If an asset has no dimension metadata it falls back to the 2.02-spec'd fixed 3:2 `contain` frame. `PhotoFrame` is **untouched** and „Фотографии" still uses `ratio="3/2" fit="contain"` (D-2.02-7).
- **Alternatives considered:** *Follow the brief literally with one fixed ratio* — rejected: measured, it letterboxes the extremes of both fields. *Add a `naturalRatio` prop to `PhotoFrame`* — rejected: `PhotoFrame` is `fill`-based and shared by the archive index, the legends grid and the homepage; making it intrinsically-sized would change the component every one of those callers depends on (the exact blast radius D-2.02-7 avoided by making `fit` additive). *Cap the table image's height too* — rejected: at the 0.44 ratio a height cap forces the scan to ~40% of native width, where the standings rows stop being readable.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: a tall standings scan makes a long section (up to ~1560px at 1280) — mitigated by the anchor nav (D-3.04-5), and it is the archival artifact itself. Two matting components now exist; their split is documented in `MattedPhoto`'s header (solo lead = own shape; grid = identical frames so a mixed set lines up).
- **Links:** Phase 3.04; `src/components/archive/MattedPhoto.tsx`; D-2.02-7; D-3.01-2; brand.md §Photo treatment; completion report §4.

### D-3.04-4 · 2026-07-26 · The season lead is a navy title band with the photo MOUNTED beneath it, replacing 2.02's full-bleed cover-crop hero
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** 2.02 §6.2 specified a full-bleed hero — the season's first back-referenced photo, `object-cover`, `aspect-[4/5]`/`md:aspect-[16/9]`, with the title over a navy gradient. That was right when the lead was „whatever photo sorted first". The lead is now a **curated** `teamPhoto` reference on 83 seasons, and a 16:9 cover crop cuts most of a portrait squad scan away. The brief also separates the two: „Season title / header" is section 2 and „Team photo (the hero/lead)" is section 3.
- **Decision:** One navy band carries the decade overline + the H1, and the matted `teamPhoto` sits inside that same band beneath the title — a print mounted on dark card stock. A season with no `teamPhoto` is **the same band without the photo**, taking the taller `py-16 md:py-24` padding 2.02 §6.2b specifies for the photo-less hero. That keeps §6.2b's rule exactly („a deliberate title band, never a greybox at hero scale") while making the two variants one treatment instead of two.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Keep the full-bleed cover-crop hero* — rejected: it crops archival scans of unknown shape, which is precisely what brand.md's mixed-quality rule forbids for archival sets. *Title band on navy, photo on paper below it* — rejected: it splits the lead into two blocks and the band reads as a stray navy strip. *Photo full-bleed with the title overlaid, `object-contain`* — rejected: `contain` inside a full-bleed fixed-ratio band produces bare mist bars to the page edge.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: the season page no longer opens with an edge-to-edge photograph the way the homepage does, so the two heroes read as related rather than identical. In exchange no scan is ever cropped, and the photo-less season is visually the same object minus one element.
- **Links:** Phase 3.04; `src/app/(site)/arhiva/[slug]/page.tsx`; 2.02 handover §6.2 / §6.2b; D-3.04-3; completion report §4.

### D-3.04-5 · 2026-07-26 · The in-page anchor nav is NOT sticky (unlike the archive index's decade jump-nav)
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The brief asks for anchor navigation „offset by `--spacing-header`". The obvious model is the archive index's `DecadeJumpNav`, which is `sticky top-header`. But D-2.02-13/§8 record the cost: with a second sticky bar, every anchor must offset for **both** the site header and the rail, and D-2.02-17 warns that `--spacing-header` *mirrors* the rendered header rather than driving it — a second such token would drift the moment the rail's padding changed.
- **Decision:** Render the rail in the flow, directly under the navy band, with `border-y border-mist`. Each anchored section then needs exactly `scroll-mt-header` and nothing else. Verified in-browser: jumping to `#trener` lands the section top at 77px, exactly the header's bottom edge, with the `<h2>` clear of it. The rail lists only sections that rendered, and suppresses itself entirely below two entries (verified: `2025-26`, which has one section, shows no rail).
- **Alternatives considered:** *Sticky rail like `DecadeJumpNav` + a new `--spacing-anchor` token* — rejected: it adds a measured token that silently breaks every anchor if the rail's padding changes, for a page that already has a short scroll to the top. *No anchor nav at all* — rejected: the brief requires it and a filled season is genuinely long (a standings scan alone can be ~1560px).
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: the rail scrolls out of view, so jumping between sections deep in the page means scrolling back up. The two archive templates therefore have visibly different jump-nav behaviour — documented here so it reads as a decision, not an oversight.
- **Links:** Phase 3.04; `src/components/archive/SeasonAnchorNav.tsx`; D-2.02-13; D-2.02-17; completion report §4.

### D-3.04-6 · 2026-07-26 · `StandingsTable`, `SquadTable` and `PersonChip` are kept in-repo but unrendered
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The redesigned page renders the new fields, not the legacy `finalTable` / `squad` / `trainers` (the brief puts that rendering out of scope; D-3.01-2 keeps the fields „Задржано за компатибилност"). Those three components were imported only by the season page, so they are now unimported. At 3.03 the repo *deleted* `DecadeTimeline` in the same situation (D-3.03-6).
- **Decision:** Keep all three, each with a header comment stating that it is legacy, unrendered since 3.04, and why. This follows the `match.ts` precedent (D-2.01-2: in-repo, unregistered, with a deferral header) rather than the `DecadeTimeline` one, because the difference is the data: `DecadeTimeline` was *replaced* by an equivalent, whereas `season.finalTable` still holds a real row (`1982-83`) that `/statistika` aggregates, and `StandingsTable` is the only component that can display it.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Delete all three (the D-3.03-6 precedent)* — rejected: it would destroy the only renderer for schema fields the model deliberately retains, in a phase whose brief puts that rendering out of scope. *Leave them with no comment* — rejected: three unimported components with no explanation is exactly the debt a future reader would delete blind.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: three unrendered components ship, and `npm run lint` will not flag them. They must be deleted together with the legacy fields when the model re-locks after 3.06 — recorded in `file-map.md` and the completion report so that clean-up has a trigger.
- **Links:** Phase 3.04; `src/components/archive/{StandingsTable,SquadTable,PersonChip}.tsx`; D-3.01-2; D-2.01-2; D-3.03-6; completion report §4.

### D-3.04-7 · 2026-07-26 · `SeasonEmptyNotice`'s placeholder chips re-worded to the redesigned page's sections
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The notice (D-2.02-8) listed five chips naming the OLD sections — „конечна табела", „состав", „тренери". The page no longer reads any of those fields, so the notice would have told a reader the archive was waiting on content that will never appear there. Its own framing also changed: after the 3.02 fill **no published season is fully empty**, so the notice went from „what ~all 74 seasons look like" to a guard.
- **Decision:** Re-word the chips to the six things the redesigned page actually renders — тимска фотографија · табела на сезоната · тренер и статистика · резултати · приказна за сезоната · фотографии — and update the component's header to say it is now a guard rather than the common case. All copy stays structural (it describes the archive's state, claims no fact), so it needs no `facts.md` entry.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Leave the old chips* — rejected: they name fields the page does not read, which is a false statement about the archive's state. *Drop the chips and keep only the sentence* — rejected: the chips are what makes „which parts are missing" concrete, and they are the registered `[PLACEHOLDER]` surface for PL-10.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: the notice can no longer be reached by any published season, so its new wording is verified against a temporary local fixture rather than live content (§5). The register entry PL-10 now covers a different field list.
- **Links:** Phase 3.04; `src/components/archive/SeasonEmptyNotice.tsx`; D-2.02-8; PL-10; completion report §4.

### D-3.04-8 · 2026-07-26 · Season titles render verbatim, guillemets included („Сезона 1985/86“)
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** Every generated season title is stored **with** Macedonian quotation marks — the H1 reads „Сезона 1985/86“, quotes and all — and the one hand-entered title (`1992-93`) has none (the cosmetic inconsistency already recorded at D-2.09-4). Stripping the quotes for display would give a cleaner page title.
- **Decision:** Render `season.title` verbatim everywhere it appears — H1, `<title>` metadata, breadcrumb crumb, and the prev/next cards. Do not strip, normalise, or reformat.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Strip the guillemets in the template* — rejected twice over: the title would then differ between `/arhiva` (which renders it verbatim on every card) and `/arhiva/<slug>`, and a template quietly rewriting stored content is the presentation layer overruling the record. *Fix the titles in Sanity* — rejected: this phase writes no content.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: the page's one H1 is visibly quoted, and `1992-93` looks different from its 95 siblings. Flagged to the content pass — one normalisation of the 96 titles fixes it everywhere at once, with no code change.
- **Links:** Phase 3.04; D-2.09-4; completion report §7.

### D-3.04-9 · 2026-07-26 · `lineupAndStats` and `results` get their own dense renderer; `SeasonStory` is left untouched
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** `SeasonStory` is the project's Portable Text renderer and is shared with `/legendi/<slug>`'s Биографија. It styles `normal` blocks as prose paragraphs at `mt-6`. But every one of the **1401** published `lineupAndStats` blocks is a single line — either one fact („Најдобар стрелец на сезоната: …") or one roster entry („1. Љ. Димовски25+0/0") — and up to 38 of them per season. Through a prose rhythm a roster becomes a page of orphaned sentences. `results` will have the same shape, one match per block.
- **Decision:** Add `src/components/archive/SeasonRecordList.tsx` with two variants: `roster` (tight line list) and `results` (one hairline-separated row per match, giving the reference site's per-match cadence). Both use `tabular-nums`, since these lines are mostly digits and lining figures hold the apps/goals and scorelines in visual columns without imposing a table the source text does not have. Nothing is reformatted — the transcribed text ships verbatim, source OCR quirks included (D-3.02S-3). `SeasonStory` renders `story` exactly as before.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Reuse `SeasonStory` for all three fields* — rejected: measured against the real 24–38-block rosters it reads as broken prose. *Add a `variant` prop to `SeasonStory`* — rejected: it is shared with the person page, and a prop that changes paragraph rhythm is the kind of change that regresses another route silently. *Parse the roster lines into a real `<table>`* — rejected: it would require inferring where a name ends and the figures begin („Љ. Димовски25+0/0" has no separator), i.e. deriving structure the source does not state.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: two Portable Text configurations now exist, with some duplicated mark/link mapping between them. Kept deliberately so the person page cannot be regressed from the season page.
- **Links:** Phase 3.04; `src/components/archive/SeasonRecordList.tsx`; `SeasonStory.tsx`; D-2.03-2; D-3.02S-3; completion report §4.

### D-3.04-10 · 2026-07-26 · Gallery dedupe stays at document `_id`; asset-level dedupe rejected
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The `_id` exclusion (D-3.04-2) holds on all 96 seasons, but a full-archive sweep of the rendered HTML found **3 seasons** (`1969-70`, `1982-83`, `1992-93`) where the same uploaded image asset is rendered twice, because the 2.09 ingestion created **two distinct `photo` documents sharing one asset**. On `1992-93` one of those two is the season's `teamPhoto`, so the lead picture visibly reappears in the grid. Deduping by `image.asset._ref` as well would remove the repeat.
- **Decision:** Do **not** add asset-level dedupe. Keep the `_id` exclusion the brief specifies, and flag the three duplicate document pairs as a content fix. The deciding evidence is `1992-93` itself: the gallery copy (`32991331-8812-437f-9529-5a89fd201c57`) carries the only caption and date that photo has („Екипа на ФК Беласица, есен 1992" / „1992"), while the `teamPhoto` reference points at the **bare** ingested duplicate (`photo-6ce62638…`). Asset-dedupe would therefore delete the page's only captioned record of that photograph — exactly the data loss D-2.02-6 was written to prevent.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Dedupe by asset, keeping the first in the deterministic order* — rejected: on the one season where it fires it drops the captioned document. *Dedupe by asset, preferring the richer document* — rejected: that is the template deciding which of two published records is canonical, which is a content call, not a presentation one. *Re-point `season.teamPhoto` at the captioned document* — rejected **for this phase only**: it is a Sanity write, and this brief writes no content. It is the right fix and is recommended to the content pass.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: on those 3 of 96 pages a reader sees the same picture twice, and on `1992-93` the lead shows no caption even though a caption for that photograph exists. Both clear with one content edit (unpublish the bare duplicates; re-point `1992-93`'s `teamPhoto`), with no code change.
- **Links:** Phase 3.04; D-3.04-2; D-2.02-6; D-2.09R-2 (the ingestion that created the duplicates); completion report §7.

### D-3.04-11 · 2026-07-26 · The season gallery adopts the deterministic caption-first photo order (D-2.08-3)
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The pre-3.04 season page ordered photos `coalesce(date,"9999") asc` (D-2.03-3). Because 881 of the 889 published photos have neither a `date` nor a `caption`, that key is not a total order, so two cold reads could return the grid in different sequences — the exact problem 2.08 fixed on the homepage.
- **Decision:** Order the gallery by the 2.08 key: `select(defined(caption) && caption != "" => 0, 1) asc, coalesce(date, "9999") asc, _id asc`. Captioned photos lead (a caption is the editor's curation lever), then date, then `_id` as a stable total-order tiebreak.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Keep `coalesce(date,"9999") asc`* — rejected: not a total order on this data, so the grid is not stable across reads. *Order by `_id` alone* — rejected: it throws away the curation signal a caption carries.
- **Consequences:** The grid is deterministic and puts an editor's captioned photo first. Downside accepted: the season page and the archive index now use different photo-ordering keys (the index still uses D-2.03-3 for its card lead) — the index is out of scope this phase and is worth aligning later.
- **Links:** Phase 3.04; D-2.08-3; D-2.03-3; completion report §4.

### D-3.04-12 · 2026-07-26 · `cn()` silently drops this project's custom `text-*` size tokens — fixed in this phase's files, repo-wide fix deferred
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** Found while measuring the rendered season page: the lead photo's caption came out at **16px instead of 14px**, and its DOM class was just `text-paper` — the `text-small` had vanished. Cause: `cn()` is `twMerge(clsx(...))`, and `tailwind-merge` is configured with **Tailwind's default scale**, not this project's. It therefore classifies `text-display` / `text-h1` / `text-h2` / `text-h3` / `text-body-l` / `text-body` / `text-small` / `text-overline` (brand.md §Typography, defined in `globals.css @theme`) as **text-colour** utilities, so any single `cn()` call containing a size **and** a colour keeps only the last one. Confirmed directly: `twMerge("text-small text-paper")` → `"text-paper"`.
- **Decision:** In the files this phase created or rewrote, never pass a size token and a colour token through the same `cn()` call — the size goes on the wrapper, the colour on the text (or the size goes in a plain, unmerged class string). Verified by measurement on the built page: the lead caption is now 14px, roster rows 16px, story 18px, and the heading scale is H1 44 → H2 30 → H3 22. **Do not** change `cn` itself in this phase.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Fix `cn` globally with `extendTailwindMerge({ extend: { classGroups: { "font-size": [...] } } })`* — the correct one-file fix, and it would repair all **8** remaining pre-existing call sites at once — but **rejected for this phase**: those 8 sites are in `SiteHeader`, `SiteFooter`, `PlaceholderChip`, `SectionOverline`, `ContactForm` and `RoleChips`, so the change would alter rendered type sizes on the homepage, the footer, the navbar, `/kontakt`, `/legendi` and `/statistika` — every one of which this brief lists as **do not touch** — and it would ship those visual changes unreviewed. *Fix the 8 sites individually here* — rejected for the same scope reason, and it leaves the trap in place for the next component. *Leave my own files broken too, for consistency* — rejected: a known-wrong render is not consistency.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: the repo still has 8 call sites rendering at the wrong size — most visibly **every `[PLACEHOLDER: …]` chip** (16px instead of 14px) and **every `SectionOverline`** (16px instead of 12px, which is why the season page's „1980-ТИ" decade label looks larger than the 12px the token specifies). That is a **pre-existing** defect, not introduced here, and it is now measured, listed and handed to the next phase with a one-file fix. A `// NB` comment in `MattedPhoto.tsx` warns the next author at the point where it bites.
- **Links:** Phase 3.04; `src/lib/utils.ts`; `src/components/archive/MattedPhoto.tsx`; `SeasonRecordList.tsx`; brand.md §Typography; `globals.css @theme`; completion report §7.


### D-3.04C-1 · 2026-07-26 · Phase 3.04-Cowork ran in the cloud from Sanity assets + Google Drive + MCP writes (not the local mirror + token script)
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The phase prompt assumed the local photo mirror `~/belasica-ingest-source/` and a local `SANITY_API_WRITE_TOKEN` scratchpad script (the D-2.09R-5 / D-3.02 precedent). This is a cloud Cowork session with no local folder connected to the device bridge.
- **Decision:** Classify photos from their already-ingested Sanity `photo` documents (CDN thumbnails downloaded + built into per-season contact sheets), read the source `.docx`/`.xlsx` from Ace's Google Drive (fully accessible via the connector), and write via the Sanity MCP under the project owner's OAuth session (`patch_documents` → `publish_documents`).
- **Alternatives considered:** Request device-bridge access to the local mirror + local token — rejected: no folder is connected and the desktop went offline mid-phase; unnecessary because every photo is already a `photo` doc with a CDN URL and a resolved `relatedSeason`. Fabricate/skip the docx sources — rejected: Drive holds them.
- **Consequences:** No local token was used or created (nothing to leak). The MCP caps at 25 docs per `patch_documents` call, so the 10 results were written in two calls; trivial. Same `production` dataset updated and verified via GROQ.
- **Links:** Phase 3.04-Cowork; supersedes the *method* (not the intent) of D-2.09R-5 / D-3.02-5 for this phase.

### D-3.04C-2 · 2026-07-26 · Photo re-verification by 8 parallel per-decade agents over per-season contact sheets
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** Re-verifying team+table photos for all 96 seasons means viewing ~883 source images — too much for one context, too slow serially.
- **Decision:** Reuse the proven D-3.02R-1 pattern: 8 per-decade subagents, each viewing labelled per-season montages (contact sheets built from CDN thumbnails) and returning structured picks; a single central step validates every picked `_id` against the photo's real `relatedSeason` and applies the writes.
- **Alternatives considered:** Classify all images in the main context — rejected: context blow-up, serialised. Let subagents write to Sanity — rejected: keep all writes central to avoid races and to run one validation pass.
- **Consequences:** Fast and parallel; the agents surfaced 5 era-misfiled photos and confirmed the 3 old borderline flags. Downside: delegated visual judgement — mitigated by the 0-anomaly `_id` validation, my own spot-checks (2007-08, 1943, 1982-83), and the owner-review artifact.
- **Links:** Phase 3.04-Cowork; D-3.02R-1; D-3.02-1 (rubric).

### D-3.04C-3 · 2026-07-26 · Wrong images corrected by clearing / repointing the season reference (photo docs left as-is, re-flagged)
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** Four seasons' current picks were wrong on inspection: 1952 `teamPhoto` is the 1951 squad photo reused on the 1952 book page; 1991-92 `tablePhoto` is a penultimate (not final) table; 1999-2000 and 2000-01 `tablePhoto` were penultimate/round-25 tables while the true „КОНЕЧНА ТАБЕЛА" exists in the same folder.
- **Decision:** Clear the two that have no valid replacement (1952 team, 1991-92 table) and repoint the two that do (1999-2000, 2000-01 → the final table). Do **not** edit any photo's `relatedSeason` (out of the season-field scope) — re-flag the 5 misfiled photos for Lazar to re-file in Studio.
- **Alternatives considered:** Leave the wrong images (ships a 1951 squad labelled 1952 and non-final tables labelled final) — rejected on content-truth. Re-`relatedSeason` the misfiled photos myself — rejected: photo-document edits are out of scope and higher-risk.
- **Consequences:** Two seasons lose a currently-set image (reversible; nothing renders publicly yet). Data is now source-true. Five photos remain mis-filed in their season galleries until Lazar moves them (owed item, OV-12 extended).
- **Links:** Phase 3.04-Cowork; OV-12; D-3.02R-2.

### D-3.04C-4 · 2026-07-26 · Borderline team images kept and flagged where no posed squad exists
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** A few seasons have no posed squad photo at all — only a youth-team shot, a portrait montage, a two-team pre-match photo, or a goal celebration.
- **Decision:** Use the best genuine Belasica team image and flag it, rather than leave the season with no team photo: 1950 (youth „подмладок"), 1955-56 (composite portrait montage), 1988-89 (two-team Bokelj–Belasica), 2007-08 (on-pitch goal celebration).
- **Alternatives considered:** Null those seasons — rejected: loses a genuine, if imperfect, team image the owner may accept. Substitute a rejected type (formation graphic, roster form) — rejected on content-truth.
- **Consequences:** Four "team photos" are not posed squads; each is a flagged owner-review item and self-omits cleanly on the redesign if the owner clears it. No fan/result/table graphic was ever used as a team photo.
- **Links:** Phase 3.04-Cowork; D-3.02R-2 (same principle).

### D-3.04C-5 · 2026-07-26 · `results` extracted only from a genuine pre-structured listing, verbatim; prose-embedded scores left in `story`
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** Round-by-round results sit inside `story` for parts of the archive (D-3.02S-3). Some seasons open with a clean structured match list; others mention scores only inside narrative sentences.
- **Decision:** Fill `season.results` only for the 10 seasons whose `story` begins with a genuine structured listing (1930-31 → 1940-41), extracting the leading story blocks **programmatically and verbatim** (verified each published `results` is a verbatim substring of its source `story`). Leave `results` empty for seasons where scores appear only in prose (the 1980s–early-90s qualification runs, 1942/1943/1948-49).
- **Alternatives considered:** Reconstruct results lists from prose sentences — rejected: reformatting narrative into a match list is fabrication under content-truth. Hand-retype the lists — rejected: transcription risk; programmatic block extraction is exact.
- **Consequences:** Results coverage is 10/96 (correct — most seasons genuinely have no clean per-match source). The extracted lists duplicate the leading portion of `story` (list in „Резултати" + narrative in „Приказна"); `story` was left untouched (scope = fill `results` only). A future pass could trim the leading list from `story` if the redesign prefers no overlap.
- **Links:** Phase 3.04-Cowork; D-3.02S-3.

### D-3.04C-6 · 2026-07-26 · Trainer/lineup gap-fill bounded to named sources; pre-1950 prose squad names not promoted to `lineupAndStats`
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** 29 seasons had no `trainer` and 16 no `lineupAndStats`. „Тренери на Беласица.docx" is blank for all 29 gaps and „Најдобри стрелци…docx" names none of the 16; a few pre-1950 stories name a squad in prose but with no appearances/goals.
- **Decision:** Fill only what a source actually names: 1957-58 `trainer` = „Коста Ефински" (the history book names him succeeding Чедо Хаџиски; the trainer docx had left it blank). Leave the other 28 trainer gaps and all 16 `lineupAndStats` gaps empty; do **not** promote pre-1950 prose squad *names* into `lineupAndStats`.
- **Alternatives considered:** Add prose squad names as `lineupAndStats` — rejected: duplicates `story`, carries no apps/goals, and is inconsistent with 3.02-Story (which added rosters only where apps/goals existed).
- **Consequences:** Task C nets 1 trainer fill and 0 lineup fills — the correct, expected outcome (most early gaps are genuine). Early-season squad names remain available in `story` prose for a future structuring pass if wanted.
- **Links:** Phase 3.04-Cowork; D-3.02-2; D-3.02S-2.

### D-3.04b-1 · 2026-07-26 · Every season section now renders on every season page, with a one-line empty note — reverses the self-omit rule (owner decision)
- **Status:** Accepted (owner decision, in-session) — supersedes the self-omit half of D-2.02-3 for `/arhiva/<slug>` only
- **Context:** Since 2.03 a season section omitted itself when its field was empty (D-2.02-3, reaffirmed for the new fields at D-3.01-6 and required by the 3.04 brief: „a missing section simply doesn't render"). After 3.04 shipped, the owner looked at the live archive and asked for „all the parts of a season page on all season pages" — the pages are structurally uneven because the underlying content is uneven: `results` exists on 10/96, `story` on 66/96, `trainer` on 68/96, `lineupAndStats` on 80/96, `tablePhoto` on 88/96. The owner was shown the cost (86 pages gain an empty „Резултати" block, 30 an empty „Приказна") and chose consistency anyway.
- **Decision:** All five sections — Табела · Тренер и статистика · Резултати · Приказна за сезоната · Фотографии — render on every season page, in the same order, with the same jump rail. A section with no content shows one line of structural copy in a bordered card (`SeasonSectionEmpty`) stating what the archive does not hold. Two refinements kept from the old behaviour: the **two halves inside „Тренер и статистика" still self-omit individually** (a season with a trainer but no roster shows the trainer, not a second note), and a season with **no lead photo and nothing in any section** still collapses to the single archive notice (D-2.02-8) rather than stacking five identical cards. The photo-less **hero** is unchanged — it stays the navy title band with no photo (2.02 §6.2b), because a greybox at hero scale is the one thing that rule exists to prevent.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Keep self-omit and fill the content instead* — rejected by the owner; also largely impossible: the 3.04-Cowork sweep established most gaps have no source at all (it recovered 1 trainer and 0 lineups from an exhaustive pass), so the pages would stay uneven. *Show the sections but reuse `StatsEmptyNotice`* — rejected: it renders a `PlaceholderChip`, and always-on sections would emit ~170 chips across the 96 pages (86 for `results` alone), swamping the placeholder register — a launch blocker that must be empty at cutover — with entries that are genuine source gaps, not outstanding work. *Show all sections except `results`* — offered as a middle option and not chosen.
- **Consequences:** Downside accepted: a sparse season is now a longer, airier page carrying up to four near-identical empty cards, and the „a sparse season must look deliberate" argument behind D-2.02-3 is traded for structural consistency. Verified across all 96 built pages: all five sections and all five rail links on 96/96, and the empty-note counts match the data exactly (rezultati 86 · prikazna 30 · trener 13 · tabela 8 · fotografii 5 — „trener" is 13 not 28 because the section note fires only when **both** halves are empty). Zero `[PLACEHOLDER]` chips rendered.
- **Links:** Phase 3.04b; owner decision in-session; `src/components/archive/SeasonSectionEmpty.tsx`; supersedes the self-omit half of D-2.02-3 for the season page; D-2.04-3 (the pattern borrowed); D-2.02-8; D-3.01-6.

### D-3.04b-2 · 2026-07-26 · `/arhiva` cards now lead with the curated `season.teamPhoto`
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The archive index picked each card's lead image as `[0]` of the season's back-referenced photos ordered by `coalesce(date,"9999") asc` (D-2.03-3). On this data that is effectively arbitrary — **886 of the 889 photos carry no `date`** — so the card showed whichever document happened to sort first: a newspaper clipping, a table screenshot, a formation graphic. Measured before the change: **69 of the 96 cards were showing something other than the season's curated `teamPhoto`.** `season.teamPhoto` is exactly the field 3.01 modelled and 3.02/3.04-Cowork filled so a curator could choose each season's face, and the season page has read it since 3.04 — so the index and the detail page disagreed about the same season. This is the „curated lead is modelled but not wired" note that has stood in `current-state.md` since 3.01.
- **Decision:** `ARCHIVE_QUERY` takes `coalesce(teamPhoto->{image, caption}, <back-reference>)`. The back-reference stays as the fallback for the 13 seasons with no `teamPhoto`, but now ordered by the deterministic 2.08 key (non-empty caption first, then date, then `_id`) instead of the unstable date-only key, so those 13 cards are at least stable across cold reads.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Replace the back-reference entirely with `teamPhoto`* — rejected: the 13 seasons without one would lose their card image and fall back to a greybox, which is worse than an imperfect real photo. *Leave it and fix it in a later redesign phase* — rejected: it is a one-clause query change, the owner asked for it directly, and the two pages disagreeing about the same season is the kind of inconsistency that reads as a bug.
- **Consequences:** 83 of 96 cards now show the curated photo and 13 use the fallback; verified against live content — 83/83 match the `teamPhoto` asset, 0 mismatches, and no card lost its image. Downside accepted: `SeasonCard`'s alt text is still the generic „Фотографија од <season>" rather than the photo's own caption (only one `teamPhoto` has a caption, so this changes nothing today). The archive index and the season page now agree.
- **Links:** Phase 3.04b; `src/app/(site)/arhiva/page.tsx`; D-2.03-3 (the ordering it replaces); D-2.08-3 (the fallback key); D-3.01-3; closes the „modelled but not wired" item in `current-state.md`.

### D-3.04d-1 · 2026-07-27 · `cn()` rebuilt with `extendTailwindMerge` — the custom type scale is now registered (fixes D-3.04-12)
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** D-3.04-12 documented the repo-wide bug: stock `tailwind-merge` does not read `@theme`, classifies the eight custom `text-*` size tokens as text COLOURS, and silently drops the size whenever a size and a colour share one `cn()` pass. Mechanically proven on 6 call sites: every `SectionOverline` rendered 16px instead of 12px, every `PlaceholderChip` and `RoleChips` chip and the header/footer nav 16px instead of 14px.
- **Decision:** `src/lib/utils.ts` builds its merger via `extendTailwindMerge`, registering all eight sizes (`display`, `h1`, `h2`, `h3`, `body-l`, `body`, `small`, `overline`) under the `font-size` class group — and `tracking-overline` under `tracking`, whose latent failure differed (never dropped, but `tracking-overline tracking-tight` kept BOTH classes, leaving the winner to CSS source order instead of deterministic last-wins). `cn()`'s signature and export are unchanged; no call site edited. The two workaround comments the old bug had forced (SeasonRecordList, MattedPhoto) were rewritten to describe the class placement as layout choice, not bug-dodging.
- **Alternatives considered:** Fix the 8 call sites by splitting size/colour into separate elements (the SeasonRecordList/MattedPhoto workaround) — rejected: leaves the trap armed for every future call site. Drop `twMerge` for plain `clsx` — rejected: legitimate override merging (e.g. `text-h2` → `text-h3`) is used and wanted.
- **Consequences:** Proven by a 14-case node script transpile-importing the real `utils.ts`: all six drop cases keep both classes; size, colour and tracking conflicts still merge last-wins. Verified in the rendered site: overlines 12px, chips/nav/legends chips 14px at 1280 and 375. One maintenance duty: a future font-size token added to `@theme` must also be added to the list in `utils.ts` (the in-file comment says so; nothing enforces it mechanically).
- **Links:** Phase 3.04d; D-3.04-12 (the finding); `src/lib/utils.ts`; `src/components/archive/SeasonRecordList.tsx` + `MattedPhoto.tsx` (comments updated).

### D-3.04d-2 · 2026-07-27 · Tap targets to WCAG 2.5.8 (≥24px) — padding into the link, rail heights held invariant, negative margins where rhythm must not move
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The season/decade jump-rail links, footer text links and breadcrumb links were ~21px tall — under the 24px WCAG 2.2 AA minimum. The decade rail is sticky under the 77px header and `/arhiva`'s anchor offset (`scroll-mt-[calc(var(--spacing-header)+3.25rem)]`) was measured against its rendered height, so its height must not change.
- **Decision:** Rails: container `py-3` → `py-1`, link gains `py-2` — same 12px per side visually, rail heights byte-identical (46px / 47px measured before and after), hit targets 21 → 37px; a comment in `DecadeJumpNav` ties the container+link padding total to the anchor calc. Footer links: `py-1 -my-1` (29px hit area; the negative margin cancels the padding out of the flex `gap-3` rhythm, so every visual distance is pixel-identical). Breadcrumb links: `py-0.5 -my-0.5` (25px) — deliberately capped below `py-1` because wrapped crumb rows sit `gap-y-1` (4px) apart and larger padding would overlap adjacent hit areas. No horizontal padding anywhere (all targets already exceed 24px wide; adding `px` would alter the gap rhythm for no compliance gain).
- **Alternatives considered:** Compensate the footer via `gap-3` → `gap-1` — rejected: also shrinks the heading→first-link distance (the heading has no padding), visibly changing the column rhythm. Enlarging the rails without the container split — rejected: changes the sticky/anchor arithmetic.
- **Consequences:** Every changed link ≥24px with zero visual drift (verified by computed styles at 1280 + 375 and by eyeball at both). Focus rings now enclose the taller padded box — intended (hit area and focus box agree). A pre-existing benign 6px overshoot in `/arhiva`'s anchor offset (constant 3.25rem = 52px vs the 46px rail) was found, NOT introduced, and preserved exactly.
- **Links:** Phase 3.04d; `SeasonAnchorNav.tsx`, `DecadeJumpNav.tsx`, `SiteFooter.tsx`, `Breadcrumb.tsx`; D-2.02-13/-17 (the arithmetic protected).

### D-3.04d-3 · 2026-07-27 · Launch metadata: Vercel-origin `metadataBase` in three files, portrait crest as the OG image, root-only Open Graph
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The site had no `metadataBase`, no Open Graph/Twitter card, no robots, no sitemap. Per-page `<title>`/description already existed on every route (audited — nothing was missing there). No custom domain exists yet; no site-URL env override exists in the codebase.
- **Decision:** `metadataBase` = `https://belasica-v2.vercel.app`, hardcoded in `layout.tsx` + `robots.ts` + `sitemap.ts` (route files cannot import from a layout module without dragging in `globals.css`); each copy carries a comment naming the other two for when a domain lands. OG: `siteName`/`title`/`description` reuse the existing verified strings (unofficial-archive self-description kept), locale `mk_MK`, image = `/crest.png` at its true 864×1220 (`sips`-verified) with alt „Грб на ФК Беласица"; Twitter `summary` card (a portrait crest suits the small square card, not `summary_large_image`). OG stays root-only: Next does not deep-merge `openGraph` per page, pages keep their `title` via the template, and inventing a 1200×630 banner asset is outside content-truth.
- **Alternatives considered:** A shared site-url module — rejected under the route/layout import constraint; revisit if a domain lands. A designed OG banner — deferred to an owner-gated asset task; the crest is the only real, rights-cleared brand asset in `/public`.
- **Consequences:** Share cards render the crest + unofficial-archive framing on every page (inner pages share the site-level card — acceptable, noted for review). Preview deployments emit production absolute URLs in og:url/sitemap/robots (standard fixed-`metadataBase` behaviour). All meta tags verified in the served HTML.
- **Links:** Phase 3.04d; `src/app/layout.tsx`; D-3.04d-4.

### D-3.04d-4 · 2026-07-27 · `sitemap.ts` from a live GROQ read with a static-routes fallback; `robots.ts` disallows only `/studio`
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** 96 season + 7 person slugs are published content that changes without redeploys (ISR 60 site-wide, D-1.05-4).
- **Decision:** `sitemap.ts` queries the public read client for every `defined(slug.current)` season/person — the exact filter both `generateStaticParams` implementations use, so the sitemap lists precisely the URLs that resolve — with `_updatedAt` as the honest `lastModified`, `revalidate = 60`, and a catch-fallback to the six static routes so a failed read never breaks the route. `robots.ts`: allow all, disallow `/studio` (an editing tool, not archive content), sitemap pointer.
- **Alternatives considered:** A build-time-only sitemap — rejected: goes stale between deploys against ISR content. Listing `/studio` — rejected: not archive content and should not be crawled.
- **Consequences:** 109 URLs verified in the built output (6 static + 96 seasons + 7 people); live-GROQ proof returned the same counts with 0 entries missing slug or `_updatedAt`.
- **Links:** Phase 3.04d; `src/app/sitemap.ts`, `src/app/robots.ts`; D-1.05-4.

### D-3.04d-5 · 2026-07-27 · LCP priority hints + honest image `sizes`; no hero quality reduction — lantern 92 accepted with devtools 96 on record
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** Post-fix Lighthouse (v13.4.1, local prod build): home mobile 91, season mobile 93, desktop 99, LCP the only weak metric. Diagnostics: the LCP images carried no `fetchpriority` — in Next 15.5 the `priority` prop preloads but does not priority-hint (verified in `node_modules`); and the homepage legends grid requested portraits at the `/legendi` grid's `sizes` (est. 106 KiB overfetch).
- **Decision:** (1) `fetchPriority="high"` on the homepage hero `Image`, and in `PhotoFrame`/`MattedPhoto` as `priority ? "high" : undefined` — the hint rides the existing LCP marker, non-priority images untouched. (2) `LegendCard` gains an optional `sizes` prop (default = the `/legendi` string); the homepage passes its own grid's true tracks (`20vw/33vw/50vw`). (3) The hero photo's quality was deliberately NOT reduced: the remaining simulated-LCP gap is the lantern estimator (the hero variant is a 39 KB webp; observed load phases sum ~147ms), and degrading THE squad photo for simulator points fails archive dignity.
- **Alternatives considered:** `quality={60}` on the hero — rejected as above. Preconnect to cdn.sanity.io — rejected: images route through the same-origin `/_next/image` optimizer, so there is no cross-origin request to warm. Critical-CSS inlining — rejected: invasive for ~150ms simulated.
- **Consequences:** Home mobile 92 / season 92-93 / desktop 99 simulated (lantern); **96 with `--throttling-method=devtools`** (applied throttling — the ≥95 gate met under the more realistic method); legends overfetch 106 → 21 KiB; `fetchPriority` verified in the served HTML of both page types (React 19 serializes it camelCase — valid, attributes are case-insensitive). Production numbers should be confirmed on the Vercel preview (HTTP/2 + CDN) at PR review.
- **Links:** Phase 3.04d; `page.tsx`, `PhotoFrame.tsx`, `MattedPhoto.tsx`, `LegendCard.tsx`; the 2.08 Lighthouse baseline in `current-state.md`.

### D-3.04d-6 · 2026-07-27 · Phase id „3.04d" (not „3.04c") to avoid decision-ID collision with 3.04-Cowork
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** This owner-directed audit-fix phase was initially branched `phase-3.04c-audit-fixes`, but the 3.04-Cowork phase already owns the `D-3.04C-*` decision namespace — a case-only difference in an append-only log two operators grep by hand.
- **Decision:** Renamed to `phase-3.04d-audit-fixes` / `D-3.04d-*` before anything was pushed.
- **Alternatives considered:** Keep `3.04c` lowercase — rejected: `D-3.04c-1` vs `D-3.04C-1` is a collision in every case-insensitive search.
- **Consequences:** None beyond naming; nothing had been pushed under the old name.
- **Links:** Phase 3.04d; D-3.04C-1…-6 (the Cowork namespace avoided).

### D-3.05a-1 · 2026-07-28 · The three variants live in their own route group with variant-local everything, sharing only data, copy and a styling-free reveal observer
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The brief requires three full homepages including a variant-local header and footer, with the live site pixel-identical afterwards and no shared component modified. The `(site)` route group supplies `SiteHeader`/`SiteFooter`/`<main>`, so the variants had to escape it; and the comparison is only honest if all three render the same content.
- **Decision:** New route group `src/app/(predlozi)/` whose layout contributes nothing but the pre-paint `.js` flag script; each page renders its own header, `<main id="main">` and footer. Everything with a visual opinion is copied into `predlog-<x>/_components/`. Exactly three things are shared: `_shared/home.ts` (the live homepage's GROQ transcribed unchanged, plus its hero-fallback / legend-sort / record-order derivations), `_shared/copy.ts` (every user-visible string), and `_shared/Reveal.tsx` (an IntersectionObserver with **zero styling** — each variant re-times `[data-reveal]` inside its own scope). The variants import from `src/lib/*` (pure helpers) and `src/sanity/*` (the read client) but from **no** `@/components/*`.
- **Alternatives considered:** Three copies of the query and copy — rejected: the two would drift and the owner would be comparing content, not design. Importing the live `Reveal`/`Container`/`PlaceholderChip` unmodified — rejected: it satisfies "pixel-identical" but not the brief's intent that a winning direction be liftable as a unit. Putting the routes under `(site)` — impossible: they would inherit the chrome being compared.
- **Consequences:** ~1,900 lines of deliberately duplicated component code that is thrown away when the owner picks. Verified isolation: `grep` finds no `@/components/` import in the group, and the rendered body markup of all eight sampled live routes is **byte-identical** to a build of the same commit with the group removed.
- **Links:** Phase 3.05a; `src/app/(predlozi)/`; D-1.04-3 (the `(site)` group's reason for existing).

### D-3.05a-2 · 2026-07-28 · Seven exploration faces, Cyrillic verified from each font's own cmap rather than from the Google subset declaration
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The brief requires a Cyrillic-complete serif for А and a condensed Cyrillic display face for В, with the choice logged. Google Fonts' `cyrillic` subset only declares which file a browser should fetch for U+0400–045F; it does not promise the designer drew Ѓ, Ќ, Ѕ or Џ, and Macedonian needs all four.
- **Decision:** А = **Playfair Display** 700/900 (Didone display) + **PT Serif** 400/700 with real italic (Cyrillic-native text) + **PT Sans Narrow** 400/700 (agate). Б = **Cormorant Garamond** 400/600 + **Commissioner** 400/500/600. В = **Oswald** 600/700 + **Golos Text** 400/700. Each was verified by downloading its cyrillic and latin `woff2` and parsing the `cmap` table out of the WOFF2 brotli stream: all 62 Macedonian letters present in the cyrillic subset, digits and „ “ ” — – · ( ) / . , : % in the latin subset. Script kept in the phase scratchpad, output quoted in the completion report.
- **Alternatives considered:** Anton, Bebas Neue and Archivo Narrow for В's condensed display — all rejected: no Cyrillic subset at all, which is exactly why Oswald wins despite being a common face. Trusting the subset list — rejected: it is a fetch hint, not a coverage guarantee. Rendering a test string and eyeballing for tofu — rejected as weaker evidence than the font's own character map.
- **Consequences:** Oswald is a widely-used face; it is used anyway because it is one of the very few heavy condensed families with real Cyrillic, and the direction earns its distinctiveness in colour, blocking and scale instead. `next/font` accepted every family and weight, so the choice is also build-verified.
- **Links:** Phase 3.05a; `src/app/(predlozi)/predlog-*/fonts.ts`; brand.md §Typography (Cyrillic-coverage precedent).

### D-3.05a-3 · 2026-07-28 · One font module per variant, not one shared module — `next/font` preloads at module granularity
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** All seven faces were first declared in a single `_shared/fonts.ts`. The built pages then preloaded **26 woff2 files on every `/predlog-*` route**, including the four families that route never renders: `next/font` associates preload hints with the module in a route's graph, not with the fonts actually used.
- **Decision:** Split into `predlog-a/fonts.ts`, `predlog-b/fonts.ts`, `predlog-c/fonts.ts`, and drop the weights nothing sets (Oswald 500, Golos 500/600 — confirmed by scanning each variant stylesheet for `font-weight` per family).
- **Alternatives considered:** `preload: false` on the non-owning families — rejected: it fixes the hint but still ships every family's `@font-face` CSS to every route. Keeping one module for tidiness — rejected: the owner will judge these on a phone.
- **Consequences:** Preloads per route 26 → **18 (А) / 8 (Б) / 8 (В)**; А is highest because it genuinely runs a three-tier newspaper system with a real italic. The live homepage is unchanged at 4, and no variant face appears in its HTML.
- **Links:** Phase 3.05a; `src/app/(predlozi)/predlog-*/fonts.ts`; D-3.04d-5 (the phase that established measuring rather than assuming image/font cost).

### D-3.05a-4 · 2026-07-28 · Variant tokens as scoped CSS custom properties + semantic classes; Tailwind keeps `display` and layout
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** Each direction needs its own colour, type and spacing system, but `brand.md` and `globals.css @theme` are out of scope — adding three token sets to the shared theme is exactly the "no shared token changed" line the brief draws.
- **Decision:** One stylesheet per variant (`a.css` / `b.css` / `c.css`), imported by that route only, defining `--pa-*` / `--pb-*` / `--pc-*` under a `.pv-a` / `.pv-b` / `.pv-c` scope plus semantic classes built from them. Markup uses Tailwind for layout (grid, flex, spacing, breakpoints) and the variant classes for everything colour/type. No hex value appears in any variant `.tsx` (verified by grep; the two `bg-white` are the crest tile, whose artwork has a white ground).
- **Alternatives considered:** Extending `globals.css @theme` with three token families — rejected: it is a shared-token change, and it would ship all three directions' utilities to the live pages. Tailwind arbitrary values (`bg-[var(--pa-navy)]`) throughout — rejected after hitting v4's data-type ambiguity on `text-[length:…]` / `font-[family-name:…]`, and because scattering the system across markup is what makes a re-skin impossible.
- **Consequences:** One hazard, hit twice and now documented in the stylesheets: a variant class and a Tailwind utility that set the **same property** have equal specificity, so source order decides — `.pc-burger{display:inline-flex}` beat `md:hidden` (burger visible at desktop) and `.pc-card-photo{position:relative}` beat a later `.pc-photo-fill` (hero photo collapsed to 0px). Rule adopted: variant classes never declare `display`; where a variant class must beat another, it is written as a two-class selector, not moved up the file.
- **Links:** Phase 3.05a; `predlog-*/[abc].css`.

### D-3.05a-5 · 2026-07-28 · Б's hero label is paper, not brass — re-proving D-3.03-5 rather than trusting it
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** Б's system sets every wall label in brass (`#D2984C`, 7.0:1 on the wall). The hero label sits over a photograph under a navy scrim, and the hero photograph is *dynamic* — it is whichever season's `teamPhoto` is newest, so it can change to a bright scan via ISR without a deploy.
- **Decision:** Compute the worst case a photograph can present rather than sample the current one: composite the scrim's alpha at the label's height over a pure-white pixel. Brass measured **1.99:1**. The scrim was deepened so the whole text band sits under α ≥ 0.93, and the label switched to paper — **13.5:1 worst case**. Brass survives as the rule beside it.
- **Alternatives considered:** Keeping brass after the scrim fix — it does pass at 5.87:1 worst case, and was still rejected: an 8pp margin on a photograph nobody controls is not worth the system consistency. Scrimming harder still — rejected: it hides the photograph, and a scrim that hides the picture is a curtain, not a museum.
- **Consequences:** One label in Б breaks the brass rule deliberately; the `--on-photo` modifier documents why in the stylesheet. Independently re-derives the live site's D-3.03-5 on a different palette.
- **Links:** Phase 3.05a; `predlog-b/b.css` (`.pb-label--on-photo`, `.pb-scrim`); D-3.03-5.

### D-3.05a-6 · 2026-07-28 · No invented masthead furniture — the standing line carries only the VERIFIED self-description
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** А is a newspaper and Б is a museum, and both forms come with furniture that reads as authentic: a dateline, an edition number, a city of publication, a founding year, an accession number. `facts.md` has the club's founding year **UNVERIFIED** and records no home town at all.
- **Decision:** The only standing line in any variant is „Неофицијална архива" (VERIFIED, Ace via Lazar, 2026-07-16). No dateline, no edition, no „Струмица", no „од 1922", no accession numbers on Б's object labels — those show the photo's own `date` field or nothing. The footers read both strings from `src/lib/facts.ts` unchanged.
- **Alternatives considered:** A dateline showing today's date — rejected: it implies an edition and makes an archive look like a publication that went to press today. „ФК Беласица · Струмица" — rejected: the town is not in `facts.md`, and it is the single most plausible-looking invention available here.
- **Consequences:** А's masthead is quieter than a real sports paper's, which is the correct trade. The club's founding year still appears on all three variants inside `siteSettings.description` — owner-authored CMS copy already live on the homepage, unchanged by this phase and flagged since 3.03 as worth confirming into `facts.md`.
- **Links:** Phase 3.05a; `_shared/copy.ts`, `predlog-a/_components/Masthead.tsx`; facts.md; D-3.03 content-dependency note.

### D-3.05a-7 · 2026-07-28 · В's tile accent moved from a left border to a top bar after the Impeccable detector flagged it
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** `/impeccable audit` ran the mechanical detector over the route group and returned two findings, both `border-left: 6px solid var(--pc-orange)` — on `.pc-tile` (15 instances: every decade tile and quick link) and `.pc-moment-caption`. Its verdict: a thick coloured border on one side of a card is the most recognisable tell of generated UI. Verified in context, not dismissed: it was the repeated card treatment across the whole direction.
- **Decision:** Rotate the bar to the top edge, where it becomes the **same** full-width 6px orange bar that already opens the header and closes the footer (`.pc-topbar`) — one motif banding the page, its blocks and its tiles. The hover translate changed from `translateX(4px)` to `translateY(-4px)` to match. The mobile nav keeps a left marker at 3px: that is a stacked list reading its state down the left edge, not a card.
- **Alternatives considered:** Dropping the accent entirely — rejected: the tiles lose definition against the navy block. Suppressing the detector rule — rejected: the finding was correct.
- **Consequences:** Detector returns `[]` across the group. Verified in the browser: `.pc-topbar`, all 15 `.pc-tile`s and `.pc-moment-caption` share a 6px `rgb(238,122,22)` top border and carry no left border.
- **Links:** Phase 3.05a; `predlog-c/c.css`; the 3.05a Impeccable audit.

### D-3.05a-8 · 2026-07-28 · Placeholder-chip hatch alpha set to 0.18 across all three variants, measured against the hatch stroke rather than the surface
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The automated contrast sweep skips any element whose ancestor paints a background image — which silently exempts every `PlaceholderChip`, since the chip's anatomy (`brand.md` §Components) is a hatched fill. The chips were therefore unverified on all three variants.
- **Decision:** Measure each chip's text against its **hatch stroke colour**, the worst case where a hatch line crosses a glyph, rather than against the chip surface. At the alphas first shipped, four of six chip states fell below AA (worst 3.80:1; А's chip used the rule colour at full opacity). All six hatch strokes were set to α 0.18, which clears 4.5:1 in every state (4.55–7.63:1).
- **Alternatives considered:** Judging against the predominant surface only, as WCAG allows for patterned backgrounds — rejected: the stroke is where the text actually sits, and the honest number is cheap to compute. Removing the hatch — rejected: it is a content-truth signal in `brand.md`, not decoration.
- **Consequences:** Chips are slightly fainter than first drawn and legible in every state. The same latent gap exists on the live site's `PlaceholderChip` (mist hatch on paper), where it happens to pass comfortably — noted, not changed, since shared components are out of scope this phase.
- **Links:** Phase 3.05a; `predlog-*/[abc].css` (`.pa-chip` / `.pb-chip` / `.pc-chip`); brand.md §Components.

### D-3.05a-9 · 2026-07-28 · `npm run build` fails intermittently on a random season page — an upstream Sanity read, not a code defect; recorded rather than worked around
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** Four of eight production builds this phase died with `Error occurred prerendering page "/arhiva/<slug>"`, each time on a **different** season (2009-10, 1981-82, 1977-78, 2013-14) with an identical error digest. One run surfaced the cause directly: `ConnectTimeoutError … f8rmnfry.apicdn.sanity.io:443`, and a plain `curl` to the same host failed at that moment and succeeded minutes later.
- **Decision:** Diagnose and record; change no code. The season template (`src/app/(site)/arhiva/[slug]/page.tsx`) has no try/catch around its fetch, so any one failed read among 96 static pages kills the build — pre-existing behaviour, on a file this phase may not touch. Builds were retried until clean and the clean run is the one reported.
- **Alternatives considered:** Adding a catch/retry to the season page — rejected: out of scope, and it would silently ship a season page with sections missing rather than failing loudly. Reducing `generateStaticParams` — rejected: changes the live site.
- **Consequences:** Verified pre-existing: it reproduced on a build with the entire `(predlozi)` group removed from disk, and no tracked file differs from `main`. ⚠️ **Carried forward:** the same flake can fail a Vercel deploy. Worth a small hardening pass on the season template (catch → render the archive notice, or a bounded retry) in a later phase.
- **Links:** Phase 3.05a; `src/app/(site)/arhiva/[slug]/page.tsx`; D-1.05-4 (ISR 60).

### D-3.05a-10 · 2026-07-28 · В's hero rebuilt as a matchday poster — owner rejected the first cut
- **Status:** Accepted (owner-directed)
- **Context:** Lazar reviewed the three proposals, chose В „Трибина" as the direction he likes, and rejected its hero specifically („i like it but i dont like the very top part"). Diagnosed against the rendered page rather than guessing: (1) the hero repeated **both** the crest and the wordmark that the sticky header carries ~60px above it, which reads as a mistake rather than a hierarchy; (2) the crest sat on a plain white rectangle — a sticker pasted onto navy, the least considered element on the page; (3) the hero used **none** of the direction's own vocabulary — it was a plain two-column split with a photo rectangle beside a vertical text stack, while hard navy/orange blocking is what makes the rest of В work.
- **Decision:** Rebuild as a poster. The photograph now leads **full-bleed** (`4/5` → `16/10` → `21/8`), and the crest becomes a real **block** — a white panel capped by the same 6px orange bar that opens the header, closes the footer and tops every tile — pinned over the photograph's bottom edge by a negative margin. Badge and wordmark form one bottom-aligned lockup: only the crest carries the negative margin, so the `<h1>` sits entirely on solid navy. The kicker, the heritage line and the two CTAs sit on one row beneath. The block is `--pc-white`, not `--pc-paper`, because the crest artwork has its own white ground and a cream panel made it read as a white card inside a cream card.
- **Alternatives considered:** Dropping the crest from the hero to kill the duplication — rejected: the brief for В calls for a crest-forward hero. Keeping the two-column split and only restyling the crest — rejected: it leaves the composition, which was the actual problem. Setting the wordmark over the photograph — rejected: contrast would then depend on whichever `teamPhoto` ISR serves (the trap D-3.05a-5 documents on Б); on navy it is a deterministic 14.95:1.
- **Consequences:** Everything through the CTAs is above the fold at 1280×860 (CTA bottom 803px), which the previous cut missed. The wordmark holds **one line** from `lg` up beside the badge (92px at 1280) and wraps to a full-width line below it on mobile (52px at 375) — `.pc-hero-name` uses its own clamp rather than `--pc-t-display` for exactly this. Re-verified at 1280 + 375: **0 problems** (contrast, headings, alt, targets, overflow); `npm run build` + `npm run lint` clean. The other two directions are untouched.
- **Links:** Phase 3.05a; `predlog-c/page.tsx`, `predlog-c/c.css` (`.pc-hero-photo`, `.pc-hero-lockup`, `.pc-hero-crest`, `.pc-hero-name`); D-3.05a-5 (the text-over-photo trap avoided), D-3.05a-7 (the orange-bar motif reused).

### D-crest-2 · 2026-07-30 · crest.png background made transparent in place — edge-connected white removal on the owner-supplied artwork (out-of-phase, owner-requested)
- **Status:** Accepted (owner decision)
- **Context:** Lazar supplied a cut-out crest (640×904 WebP → transparent PNG) and asked to compare it with `public/crest.png` and swap the transparent version in. Comparison showed the repo file is the better source: owner-supplied (D-crest-1), 864×1220, complete pennant tip; the supplied cut-out is crisper per pixel but smaller, from a different digitization, and its tip is truncated by the source crop. In both files the pennant spans the canvas edge-to-edge — the only true background is the two white triangles beside the bottom point.
- **Decision:** Keep the owner-supplied 864×1220 artwork and remove its background **in place**: flood-fill from the canvas edges over near-white (all channels ≥ 225, 4-connected) so only white *outside* the pennant outline turns transparent (8.0% of pixels — the two bottom triangles); the white left half and all interior whites are untouched (single opaque component verified, no leaks). Dimensions stay 864×1220, so the OG `width`/`height` in `layout.tsx` remain true with zero code edits; the PNG shrank 1.25 MB → 949 KB. The white-tile treatment stays everywhere (the white left half still needs a light backdrop — D-crest-1's tile decision stands; only its premise that the artwork is opaque is superseded). Three code comments claiming the artwork "has a white ground/background" were reworded to name the white left half (`SiteHeader.tsx`, `(site)/page.tsx`, `predlog-c/TerraceHeader.tsx`).
- **Alternatives considered:** *Swap in the supplied 640×904 cut-out* — rejected: lower resolution, truncated tip, replaces owner-supplied artwork, and forces an OG-dimensions edit. *Keep crest.png opaque and add a second transparent asset* — rejected: two crest sources drift apart; every usage either benefits from or is indifferent to transparency. *Also crop to content* — moot: the pennant reaches all four canvas edges; there is nothing to crop.
- **Consequences:** The OG image (`/crest.png`) is now a transparent PNG — scrapers composite it on their own background (e.g. Twitter dark-mode summary card shows it on dark; the pennant carries its own outline so it reads, but the card look changes from today's white). Where the crest sits directly on a surface it now prints on it instead of showing a white box — verified improvement on predlog-a's paper masthead and predlog-b's mat; header/footer/hero white tiles render pixel-identical. Favicon set (`icon.png`, `apple-icon.png`, `favicon.ico`) deliberately untouched — still the opaque crest-on-white squares from D-crest-1. Pre-existing quirk, unchanged: the tip's tassel touches the canvas bottom edge in the artwork itself.
- **Links:** D-crest-1 (tile treatment stands; opacity premise superseded); `public/crest.png`; `src/app/layout.tsx` (OG block, dims unchanged); `src/components/SiteHeader.tsx`; `src/app/(site)/page.tsx`; `src/app/(predlozi)/predlog-c/_components/TerraceHeader.tsx`.

### D-3.05-1 · 2026-07-30 · Direction В „Трибина" adopted site-wide; `brand.md` amended rather than replaced
- **Status:** Accepted (owner-directed)
- **Context:** Phase 3.05a shipped three homepage proposals and explicitly made no recommendation. Lazar picked **В „Трибина"** and asked for it as the official homepage; asked how far it should reach, he chose **full site-wide adoption now** (over homepage-only or homepage-plus-chrome) and **delete all three proposals**.
- **Decision:** Amend `brand.md` in place — §Color, §Typography, §Spacing & layout, §Motion, §Components, §Brand rules, §Design tokens — keeping its structure, its amendment-note convention and its 1.02/2.02 history in a collapsed block. The direction's `--pc-*` variables were **not** copied across; every value was re-derived into the project's own token names so components keep reading `bg-navy` / `text-paper` / `u-h2` and nothing carries an exploration-era prefix.
- **Alternatives considered:** Homepage-only — rejected by the owner and, on inspection, incoherent: the header and footer are part of what В is, so `/` would have swapped chrome shape on every click into an inner page. A new `brand-v2.md` — rejected: `brand.md` is named as the only token source in `CLAUDE.md`, and two files would immediately disagree.
- **Consequences:** `brand.md` no longer describes the shipped 1.02 look; the 3.05a handover §В stays the intent document behind it. Everything downstream that reads a token got the new value for free — which is what made a ~5,100-line restyle tractable.
- **Links:** `brand.md`; `docs/design-handovers/Part-3-Phase-05a-Directions.md` §В; D-3.05a-10.

### D-3.05-2 · 2026-07-30 · `font-serif` retired in favour of `font-display`; Oswald is not a serif
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The direction's display face is **Oswald**, a condensed gothic. The repo's display utility was `font-serif` (Source Serif 4) at 41 call sites across 24 files.
- **Decision:** Delete `--font-serif` from `@theme`, add `--font-display`, and rename every call site. A utility named `font-serif` resolving to a condensed sans is a lie that every future editor has to discover the hard way.
- **Alternatives considered:** Pointing `--font-serif` at Oswald and leaving the name — rejected for exactly that reason. Keeping both names as aliases — rejected: two names for one face is how drift starts.
- **Consequences:** `font-serif` no longer exists anywhere; `grep font-serif` returns 0. Most call sites then collapsed further into the `u-h1`/`u-h2`/`u-h3` role classes, since the direction's headings also carry a case change and tracking that a size token cannot express.
- **Links:** `src/app/globals.css`; `src/app/fonts.ts`; brand.md §Typography.

### D-3.05-3 · 2026-07-30 · Heading roles became CSS component classes, not utility strings
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** In this direction a heading is face + size + weight + `text-transform: uppercase` + tracking. Tailwind's `@theme` can carry size, line-height, weight and letter-spacing on a `--text-*` token, but **not** `text-transform`, so `u-h2`'s defining characteristic could not live in the type scale.
- **Decision:** Define `u-display` / `u-h1` / `u-h2` / `u-h3` / `u-stat` / `u-label` / `u-tile` / `u-card` / `u-btn` / `u-link` / `u-cap` in `@layer components`, and deliberately **omit `color`** from all of them so a `text-*` utility always wins and each call site states its own surface.
- **Alternatives considered:** Repeating `font-display text-h2 font-semibold uppercase tracking-[…]` at every call site — rejected: five utilities that must stay in lockstep across 50-odd sites is the drift this phase exists to remove. A `<Heading>` React component — rejected: it would have to wrap headings inside `PortableText` serializers and table cells too.
- **Consequences:** Headings read as `u-h2 text-navy` / `u-h2 text-paper`. The raw `text-h1`…`text-stat` sizes stay available as utilities for one-off use.
- **Links:** `src/app/globals.css` `@layer components`; brand.md §Typography.

### D-3.05-4 · 2026-07-30 · The focus ring is deliberately UNLAYERED — inside `@layer components` it silently did nothing
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** `focus.ts` moved from Tailwind `ring-*` utilities to an `outline`-based `.u-focus` class, defined alongside the other role classes in `@layer components`. Measured on a real keyboard `Tab`, the focused element computed **`outline-style: none`**: the vendored shadcn stylesheet carries `&:focus-visible { outline-style: none }` in `@layer utilities`, and `utilities` sorts **after** `components`, so it won on every focusable element on the site. The old code was unaffected because its ring was a `box-shadow`, so `outline-none` cost it nothing.
