- **Decision:** Move `.u-focus` / `.u-focus--on-navy` **out of every layer** — unlayered CSS outranks all layers — and set `outline-color` as its own longhand rather than through the `outline` shorthand, because the base layer's `outline-ring/50` on `*` otherwise bled through and produced navy at **50% alpha** (a ~3.4:1 ring where 14.95:1 was intended).
- **Alternatives considered:** `!important` — rejected: it hides the cascade problem instead of stating it. Moving the rules into `@layer utilities` — rejected: it would work today and break the next time layer order or the vendored sheet changes; a focus ring should not be switchable off by a utility at all.
- **Consequences:** Verified on a real `Tab` at 375: **3px solid `rgb(13,31,60)`** at 2px offset on light, **3px solid `rgb(238,122,22)`** on navy. ⚠️ Standing rule: keep these rules out of `@layer`, and prefer `outline-*` longhands here.
- **Links:** `src/app/globals.css`; `src/lib/focus.ts`; brand.md §Color.

### D-3.05-5 · 2026-07-30 · `PhotoFrame`'s `fit` now selects the surround as well as the crop
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** brand.md's amended §Photo treatment wants presentation photographs as hard-edged blocks (no mat, no border, no radius) but keeps the **mist mat** for archival sets, where a scan's true aspect is information (the mixed-quality rule). Those are the same two cases `fit="cover"` / `fit="contain"` already distinguished.
- **Decision:** Fold the surround into `fit`: `cover` renders a hard block on a navy ground, `contain` keeps the mist mat and hairline border. The placeholder chip follows the same switch (`onNavy` on cover, light on contain).
- **Alternatives considered:** A separate `frame` prop — rejected: two props that must always be set together is an invitation to set one of them wrong. Dropping the mat everywhere — rejected: it would break the mixed-quality rule the archive grid depends on.
- **Consequences:** Every call site changed behaviour without changing its arguments; the season gallery and `MattedPhoto` keep their mats because they already passed `contain`. Card leads and the hero lost their borders, which is what the direction wanted.
- **Links:** `src/components/home/PhotoFrame.tsx`; brand.md §Photo treatment; D-2.02-7.

### D-3.05-6 · 2026-07-30 · Every inner page opens with a shared navy `PageHeader` block
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The direction is built from full-bleed blocks that butt against each other, and the 3.05a handover names the hero's block as "the reusable pattern a season-page or person-page hero would inherit". The inner pages each opened with a bare paper `Container` holding a breadcrumb and an H1 — six slightly different openings.
- **Decision:** Add `src/components/PageHeader.tsx` — navy block carrying breadcrumb → H1 → intro → counted meta — and use it on `/arhiva`, `/statistika`, `/legendi`, `/za-nas`, `/kontakt`. The season and person pages keep their own richer heroes but were moved onto the same navy block, with the **breadcrumb pulled inside it** (`Breadcrumb` gained an `onNavy` variant). `PersonHero`'s two variants (portrait / monogram) both moved onto navy, so the page opening no longer changes shape depending on whether a photograph happens to exist.
- **Alternatives considered:** Leaving the inner pages on paper — rejected: the header, the homepage and the footer are all navy blocks, and a paper page opening under a navy header read as an unfinished port. Making the block `navy-2` — rejected: the sticky jump rails are `navy-2`, and three values stacked lost the distinction between them.
- **Consequences:** The block order is now navy (site header) → navy (page header) → `navy-2` (sticky rail, where present) → paper (content) on every route. `--spacing-header` re-measured at **78px** and the token updated; an anchor jump lands at exactly 78px, verified in-browser.
- **Links:** `src/components/PageHeader.tsx`; `src/components/legends/PersonHero.tsx`; `src/components/archive/Breadcrumb.tsx`; D-2.02-5.

### D-3.05-7 · 2026-07-30 · Font weights normalised to the two weights actually loaded
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** brand.md pins Golos Text at 400/700 and Oswald at 600/700, but the tree carried 8 `font-medium` (500) and 19 `font-semibold` (600) on body-face text. With only 400 and 700 fetched, CSS font matching silently renders 500 as **400** and 600 as **700** — so those classes described a weight the user never saw.
- **Decision:** Rewrite every body-face `font-medium` / `font-semibold` to `font-bold`, and leave the two `font-semibold` in `SiteHeader` alone because they sit on Oswald, where 600 **is** loaded.
- **Alternatives considered:** Loading 500 and 600 as well — rejected: four extra woff2 files per face to preserve distinctions the design does not use. Leaving the classes — rejected: a class that lies about its result is worse than no class.
- **Consequences:** `grep font-medium` returns 0; `font-semibold` survives only in `SiteHeader`. Also removed: two `italic` blockquotes, since neither shipped face has an italic and the browser was synthesising an oblique on Cyrillic.
- **Links:** `src/app/fonts.ts`; brand.md §Typography.

### D-3.05-8 · 2026-07-30 · Three tap targets under 24px fixed with an explicit `min-h`, not padding-plus-negative-margin
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The 1280/375 sweep found three WCAG 2.5.8 failures introduced by the type change: breadcrumb links at **22px** (the crumb moved from 14px body text to a 12px overline), every `/statistika` in-table link at **16.5px**, and the person-page back-link at 16.5px.
- **Decision:** Give each an explicit `inline-flex min-h-6 items-center`. Deliberately **not** the `py-N -my-N` trick D-3.04d-2 used elsewhere: cancelling padding with a negative margin makes adjacent hit areas overlap once a breadcrumb wraps to a second row — exactly when it is hardest to tap. The back-links became `u-link`, which carries its own padding and is the direction's text link anyway.
- **Alternatives considered:** Claiming the spec's "inline, in a sentence" exception for the table links — rejected: each is the entire content of its cell, not a link inside prose.
- **Consequences:** 0 targets under 24×24 on all eight routes at both widths. The stats rows grew ~7px each.
- **Links:** `src/components/archive/Breadcrumb.tsx`; `src/components/stats/StatTable.tsx`; D-3.04d-2.

### D-3.05-9 · 2026-07-30 · `/predlog-a|b|c` deleted with the whole `(predlozi)` group
- **Status:** Accepted (owner-directed)
- **Context:** With В adopted, its code existed twice — once under `(predlozi)/predlog-c`, once in the live site. Asked what should happen to the proposals, Lazar chose **delete all three**.
- **Decision:** Remove `src/app/(predlozi)/` entirely — all three pages, their per-variant `fonts.ts`, their scoped stylesheets and their components, plus `_shared/{home.ts,copy.ts,Reveal.tsx}` and the group layout.
- **Alternatives considered:** Keeping А and Б `noindex` for reference — rejected by the owner; they would also have kept five typefaces in the build for pages nothing links to. Keeping В as a staging copy — rejected: two copies of one design drift.
- **Consequences:** The build drops from 120 to **117** pages. The three directions survive in git history and, as intent + token deltas, in `docs/design-handovers/Part-3-Phase-05a-Directions.md`, which is **kept**. Five typefaces (Playfair Display, PT Serif, PT Sans Narrow, Cormorant Garamond, Commissioner) leave the project; Oswald + Golos Text are promoted to `src/app/fonts.ts`.
- **Merge note (main ← this branch):** `D-crest-2` landed on `main` while this phase was in flight and reworded the same crest comment in three files. Two of them (`SiteHeader.tsx`, `(site)/page.tsx`) were rewritten by this phase, so its **correction was carried into the new text** — the white tile stays because the crest's left half is white, not because the artwork has a white ground. The third (`predlog-c/TerraceHeader.tsx`) went away with the group; the delete wins.
- **Links:** D-3.05a-3; D-crest-2; `docs/design-handovers/Part-3-Phase-05a-Directions.md`.

### D-3.05-10 · 2026-07-30 · Radius tokens kept at `0` rather than deleted
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** brand rule 6 sets every corner radius to 0. That made `rounded-card` / `rounded-photo` / `rounded-chip` no-ops at 39 call sites.
- **Decision:** Strip the now-dead utilities from the components, but **keep the three tokens defined at `0px`** in `@theme` (and `--radius: 0px` for shadcn).
- **Alternatives considered:** Deleting the tokens too — rejected: any stray or future `rounded-card`, and every shadcn component pulled in later, would then fall back to a non-zero default and quietly reintroduce rounding. Keeping the utilities in place — rejected: 39 dead classes reading as intent.
- **Consequences:** `grep -E "rounded-(card|chip|photo|none)"` returns 0 in `src/`, while a stray one would still render flat.
- **Links:** `src/app/globals.css`; brand.md §Spacing & layout.

### D-3.05-11 · 2026-07-30 · The on-page crest is served as an unoptimized UI derivative — Vercel's optimizer 402s on `/crest.png` for this account
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** Immediately after 3.05 merged, the club crest was **missing from production** in both places it renders (site header, homepage hero). Diagnosed from response headers rather than code: every `/_next/image?url=%2Fcrest.png&w=…` variant returns **402 `OPTIMIZED_IMAGE_REQUEST_PAYMENT_REQUIRED`**. ⚠️ **First diagnosis was wrong and is corrected here.** An initial probe suggested remote Sanity images were exempt (a novel width returned 200), so this was recorded as a limit on *local* source images only. A wider sweep disproved it: across `w=384…3840` on the **same remote Sanity image**, every `x-vercel-cache: MISS` returns **402** and only `HIT` returns 200 — the earlier 200 was a cache hit, not an exemption. **The account is over its image-optimization quota outright**, for remote and local sources alike; anything not already in the CDN cache 402s. Measured on the production homepage: **12 of 53 optimizer URLs fail**. `D-crest-2` changed `crest.png`'s bytes, invalidating its cached variants, which is why the crest in particular went from working to missing; it broke at `fa7f0bd`'s deploy (17:22 UTC), **48 minutes before** the 3.05 merge (18:10 UTC). This phase contributed by changing `sizes`/widths on several components, which mints further uncached variants — but the root cause is the quota, not the redesign.
- **Decision:** Render the on-page crest from a **UI-sized derivative of the same artwork**, `public/crest-ui.webp` (400×565, 57.6 KB), with `unoptimized` on both `<Image>` calls so the request never reaches the optimizer. `/crest.png` is **untouched** and remains the canonical asset for the Open Graph card (`layout.tsx` still declares its true 864×1220) and the favicon lineage. Generated with the `sharp` already in the tree as Next's own optimizer dependency — **no new npm package**.
- **Alternatives considered:** *`unoptimized` on `/crest.png` itself* — rejected: `/public` is served `max-age=0, must-revalidate`, so a **972 KB** payload would revalidate constantly behind a 44px header logo, against a project with an explicit ≥95 Lighthouse gate and D-3.04d-5's LCP work. *Cache-busting the optimizer with a new query* — rejected: it cannot mint quota, and it burns more of it. *Reverting D-crest-2* — rejected: the owner asked for the transparent crest. *Waiting for the plan to reset* — rejected as the only action: it leaves the club's identity mark missing from every page meanwhile.
- **Consequences:** The crest renders again at **57.6 KB instead of 972 KB** (–94%), with no optimizer quota consumed. Verified the derivative is the same artwork: 7.9% vs 8.0% transparent, aspect 0.7080 vs 0.7082. **This fixes the crest only.** ⚠️ The site-wide problem is untouched: **~23% of images on the production homepage are still broken** (12 of 53 optimizer URLs), and any newly-minted variant — local or remote — will 402 until the quota is resolved. That is an **account-level** matter (raise the Vercel plan, or wait for the quota window to reset) and is **owed to Lazar as a decision**, alongside one code-level alternative: setting `images.unoptimized = true` in `next.config` would take the whole site off Vercel's optimizer and serve straight from `cdn.sanity.io`, which already returns format- and width-optimized images (the URLs this repo builds carry `?w=…&auto=format`). That trades Next's srcset generation for guaranteed delivery and would need a Lighthouse re-baseline, so it was **not** taken unilaterally. If the plan is raised instead, the honest cleanup here is to drop `unoptimized`, delete `crest-ui.webp` and point both call sites back at `/crest.png`.
- **Links:** D-crest-2 (the byte change that triggered it); `public/crest-ui.webp`; `src/components/SiteHeader.tsx`; `src/app/(site)/page.tsx`; `src/app/layout.tsx` (OG block, still `/crest.png` at 864×1220).

### D-3.02F-C-1 · 2026-07-30 · A shared bounded-retry Sanity read helper replaces the silent `catch → notFound()` in both fan-out templates
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The archive grew to 96 seasons + 160 people, so `npm run build` now prerenders **270 pages** making ~540 Sanity reads. D-3.05a-9 recorded that a single `ConnectTimeoutError` to `apicdn.sanity.io` fails a whole build, and described the season template as having „no try/catch around its fetch". **That description was wrong, and the truth was worse:** both templates have carried `try { … } catch { x = null }` since 2.03 (`496a580`), and `null` falls straight into `notFound()`. A transient CDN timeout therefore did not fail the build — it silently emitted a **404 for a season that exists**, leaving the build green and the archive quietly one page short. The observed build deaths came from the *unwrapped* `generateMetadata` and `generateStaticParams` reads instead.
- **Decision:** New `src/sanity/fetch.ts` exporting `fetchOrThrow(query, params, label)` — bounded retry, then **throw** with the slug and the underlying error (`cause` unwrapped, because undici's own message is only „fetch failed"). Wired into **all six** reads across the season and person templates: `generateStaticParams`, `generateMetadata` and the page body of each. Every retry is also `console.warn`ed, so a wobble the build absorbs is still visible in the Vercel log rather than invisible. A query that legitimately matches nothing still returns `null` and still 404s — only a *thrown* read failure is retried and escalated.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Keep `catch → notFound()`* — rejected: it is precisely the silent-incomplete-archive failure the brief forbids. *Catch and render the page with sections missing* — rejected for the same reason; a partial archive page is a lie about the archive. *Retry silently with no logging* — rejected: it hides the very condition the helper exists for, and would have made this phase's diagnosis impossible. *Wrap every read on the site* — deliberately **not** done: the brief scopes this to the two fan-out templates, which are 262 of the 270 pages. The homepage, `/arhiva`, `/legendi`, `/statistika` and `sitemap.ts` still read unwrapped, and a wobble on any of them still fails the build (proven incidentally during this phase's forced-failure test, where `/` died first). Carried as a recommended follow-on.
- **Consequences:** A transient read no longer costs a page. A **genuine** failure now fails the build loudly and by name — verified by forcing an unreachable host: `Error: Sanity read failed for person „robert-popov" after 3 attempts — TypeError: fetch failed (cause: getaddrinfo ENOTFOUND …)`, alongside Next's own `Export encountered an error on … /legendi/robert-popov`. ⚠️ Behaviour change to state plainly: builds that previously went green while dropping a page will now **fail** instead. That is the intended trade.
- **Links:** D-3.05a-9 (the flake, and its inaccurate description of the code); D-3.02F-C-2, D-3.02F-C-3; `src/sanity/fetch.ts`; `src/app/(site)/arhiva/[slug]/page.tsx`; `src/app/(site)/legendi/[slug]/page.tsx`.

### D-3.02F-C-2 · 2026-07-30 · Five retry attempts, not the briefed three — the number was measured, not assumed
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The brief specified „bounded retry with backoff — **3 attempts is enough**". Built that way first (backoff 500 ms / 1500 ms) and measured it: of three consecutive 270-page builds, **two still died**, each after exhausting all three attempts on a *different* random page — `/arhiva/1971-72` and `/legendi/zlatko-ilievski` — always with `Connect Timeout Error (attempted address: f8rmnfry.apicdn.sanity.io:443, timeout: 10000ms)`. Because undici burns its own **10 s** connect timeout per attempt, three attempts already span ~32 s; the wobble simply outlives it.
- **Decision:** `ATTEMPTS = 5` with exponential backoff `1 s / 2 s / 4 s / 8 s`, so one unlucky page waits out a **~65 s** window before failing. A healthy read still retries zero times and costs nothing.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Keep 3 as briefed* — rejected: measurably does not meet the phase's own Definition of Done („three consecutive clean builds"), and shipping a number known not to work would be following the letter of the brief against its purpose. *Retry indefinitely / very long backoff* — rejected: an actual Sanity outage must fail fast and loudly, not hang a deploy. *Lower `staticGenerationMaxConcurrency`* — rejected as the primary lever: a concurrency probe showed the CDN serving 16 parallel requests fine (connect 0.08 s → 0.44 s, zero timeouts), so the stalls are intermittent link jitter, not steady saturation; permanently halving Vercel's build parallelism to work around that is the wrong trade.
- **Consequences:** Worst case a genuinely-unreachable Sanity delays the build ~65 s per page before the loud failure, up from ~32 s. ⚠️ **Environmental caveat, stated so the number is not over-trusted:** this machine's default route is a **VPN tunnel (`utun5`) over a phone hotspot** — 63–95 ms to the CDN with 12 ms jitter. That is a plausible contributor to how often the flake fires here, so 5 may be more headroom than a Vercel builder needs. It is cheap headroom, and D-3.05a-9 observed the same class of failure on the same machine.
- **Links:** D-3.05a-9; D-3.02F-C-1; D-3.02F-C-3; `src/sanity/fetch.ts`.

### D-3.02F-C-3 · 2026-07-30 · Next's own `staticGenerationRetryCount` added as a second layer under the read helper
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** With 5 attempts in place, a build **still** failed once (`/arhiva/1974-75`, exhausting all five on a `generateMetadata` read). Retrying the *read* harder has diminishing returns once a wobble outlasts any sane per-read window, and each extra attempt lengthens the tail on a genuine outage.
- **Decision:** Add `experimental.staticGenerationRetryCount: 2` to `next.config.ts` — Next's own documented mechanism for re-attempting a page whose generation failed. The two layers are complementary: the helper absorbs a flaky *read*, this re-runs the *page*.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Push the read helper to 8–10 attempts instead* — rejected: minutes-long tails on a real outage, and it still cannot outlast an arbitrary wobble. *Reduce build concurrency* — rejected, see D-3.02F-C-2. *Accept the flake and retry builds by hand, as 3.05a did* — rejected: that is exactly what this phase exists to end, and it does not help an unattended Vercel deploy.
- **Consequences:** **Three consecutive clean builds, exit 0, 270/270 pages** — while absorbing **125 transient read failures** across the three runs (85 / 15 / 25 retry warnings), i.e. the link was *worse* during the passing runs than during the failing ones. This cannot mask a broken page: a retried render either completes in full or the build still fails loudly. ⚠️ It is an `experimental.*` key — a Next major upgrade should re-check that it still exists.
- **Links:** D-3.02F-C-1; D-3.02F-C-2; `next.config.ts`.

### D-3.02F-C-4 · 2026-07-30 · „Клупски рекорди" renders as a label/value register, not as the brand's scoreboard
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** brand.md §Components makes the **scoreboard** the records voice, and both existing record surfaces use it — the homepage strip and this page's own `BalanceSummary` set their value in `u-stat` (Oswald, `clamp(1.375rem, 3vw, 2.25rem)`). That works for „1982/83 и 1987/88 (2 титули)". It does not survive this set: of the 30 records, the longest `value` is a **481-character** 17-name roster of internationals, and several others are full clauses. Set in condensed display caps those produce a wall, not a record.
- **Decision:** One block per category — a **white block capped by the signature 6px orange bar** (`u-cap`, the same treatment `StatsEmptyNotice` already gives a block on this page) — holding a `<dl>` whose rows are divided by `mist` hairlines. The **label keeps the scoreboard's vocabulary** (`text-overline` tracked caps, navy, exactly the `dt` treatment of the homepage strip and the balance band); the **value is body copy** (`text-body`, ink, capped at `max-w-measure`). Two columns from `md` up (`minmax(0,15rem) / minmax(0,1fr)`, baseline-aligned), stacked below — so a long value grows downward and never widens the page. Uniform across all four groups.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Scoreboard for every record* — rejected: unreadable on the long values, and it would make the page's headline section its ugliest. *Scoreboard for short values, body copy for long ones* — rejected: typography keyed to string length is brittle by construction and would visibly reshuffle the moment an editor lengthens a value. *Scoreboard for the `honours` group only* (all five of its values are short today) — rejected for the same reason: it makes one group's design contingent on content that may change.
- **Consequences:** The records section deliberately looks **unlike** the homepage strip, while sharing its label vocabulary and its orange-bar motif. Measured at 1280: every new pair 14.95–18.11:1 (H2/H3 navy-on-paper 14.95, `dt` navy-on-white 16.43, `dd` ink-on-white 18.11), zero below AA. At 375 the 481-character value renders 295 px wide inside a 335 px row with no box overflow, and `window.scrollTo(500,0)` leaves `scrollX` at **0** — the page does not scroll sideways. **No new `brand.md` token, no new component vocabulary.**
- **Links:** brand.md §Components (Scoreboard, Card, Placeholder chip); D-3.03-3; D-2.02-3 (self-omit); `src/components/stats/ClubRecordList.tsx`.

### D-3.02F-C-5 · 2026-07-30 · The group order on /statistika deliberately differs from the homepage strip's (D-3.03-3)
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The homepage orders records **honours → appearances → scorers** (D-3.03-3), because it features the appearance record. This phase's brief specifies **honours → scorers → appearances → other** for `/statistika`. Reusing the homepage's `CATEGORY_PRIORITY` would have quietly produced the wrong order.
- **Decision:** `groupClubRecords` in `src/lib/stats.ts` owns its own order, with the four group titles taken **verbatim** from the Studio's `clubRecord.category` option list (`Трофеи и признанија`, `Стрелци`, `Настапи`, `Друго`), so an editor finds a record under the exact heading they filed it under. `src/components/home/ClubRecords.tsx` is left untouched.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Share one ordering constant between both surfaces* — rejected: they are genuinely different orders for different reasons, and collapsing them would force one page to misrepresent its own emphasis. *Re-order the homepage to match* — rejected: out of scope, and D-3.03-3's reasoning still holds there.
- **Consequences:** Two category orders exist in the codebase, ~40 lines apart in intent; each is commented with a pointer to the other so neither reads as a bug. On `/statistika` the order mirrors the two ranking tables below it, which run scorers first. An uncategorised record falls into „Друго" rather than vanishing.
- **Links:** D-3.03-3; `src/lib/stats.ts`; `src/components/home/ClubRecords.tsx`.

### D-3.02F-C-6 · 2026-07-30 · The contradicting all-time total is rendered, not suppressed
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** Rendering the records surfaced a collision the brief anticipated only as a source-document question. The `clubRecord` „Вкупна екипна статистика низ историјата" states **2275 натпревари, 1030-421-824, 3907:2976, 2885 бода** (the history book's figure). Directly below it on the same page, „Севкупен биланс на клубот" now sums the 92 entered `finalTable` rows to **2240, 1018-411-811, 3851:2928, 2849**. Both are faithfully rendered content, and they are a third and fourth number alongside the two the brief's owed item already names — the page now visibly disagrees with itself.
- **Decision:** Render both, unchanged, and record the collision as an owed-verification item for Ace. The existing coverage line — „Составено од 92 сезони со внесена конечна табела." — already scopes the band to what the archive holds, which is the honest distinction between „our sum over 92 entered tables" and „the book's all-time claim".
- **Alternatives considered:** *Suppress the `clubRecord` when the balance band renders* — rejected on the D-3.04-10 principle: a template that hides one of two published fields is overruling the record. *Reword the record or the band* — rejected: content-truth, and this phase makes **no Sanity write**. *Compute a reconciliation* — rejected outright: no number on this site is computed to look complete (brand rule 5).
- **Consequences:** Until Ace reconciles the sources, a careful reader can find two different all-time totals on `/statistika`. This is a **content** defect, not a template one, and it is now visible rather than latent — which is the point. Added to the owed register and to Lazar's eyeball checklist.
- **Links:** D-3.04-10 (same principle, photo duplicates); brand rule 5; `src/lib/stats.ts`; owed item OV-13.

### D-3.05b-1 · 2026-07-30 · The homepage's six records are an explicit label whitelist, superseding D-3.03-3's category sort
- **Status:** Accepted (supersedes the *homepage* half of D-3.03-3; `/statistika`'s D-3.02F-C-5 order is untouched)
- **Context:** `ClubRecords` sorted **every** `clubRecord` document by category priority then `order` and rendered all of them. That was right at 7 records; the archive now holds **30**, so the homepage strip had become the whole register — 30 cells between the legends band and the decade grid. The brief names six records to keep.
- **Decision:** A `HOMEPAGE_RECORD_LABELS` array inside `ClubRecords` naming the six by their exact `label` string, in render order, resolved with `find` per label. The array **is** the order; `category` and `order` are no longer consulted on this surface. `CATEGORY_PRIORITY`/`sortRecords` are deleted rather than left dead, so nothing suggests the order still comes from the category.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Slice `[0...6]` after the existing sort* — rejected: publishing a 31st `honours` record, or renumbering `order` in Studio, would silently change what the front page claims about the club. *Filter to `category == "honours"`* — rejected for the same reason, and it cannot express a set that deliberately mixes honours, a scorer and an appearance record. *Whitelist at the `page.tsx` call site* — considered and rejected only because `ClubRecords` is used by the homepage **and nowhere else** (verified: `/statistika` renders its 30 through `ClubRecordList` + `groupClubRecords`), so the component is the one readable place; the brief's task 13 permits this explicitly.
- **Consequences:** Changing the homepage's six is now an editorial decision made in one named list, not an emergent property of Studio metadata. A whitelisted label absent from Sanity renders **nothing** in its place. The featured cell is pinned to „Шампион на Македонија" specifically rather than „whichever survived first", so an unpublished championship would leave the strip with no orange cell instead of promoting the runner-up into it — back-filling the feature would overstate a lesser record. Verified live: 6 cells, in the briefed order, feature orange `rgb(238,122,22)` with navy `rgb(13,31,60)` ink; `/statistika` still 30 in 5/13/8/4.
- **Links:** D-3.03-3 (superseded here); D-3.02F-C-5; brand.md §Components (Scoreboard); brand rule 5; `src/components/home/ClubRecords.tsx`.

### D-3.05b-2 · 2026-07-30 · The scoreboard's last cell widens so the strip's final row ends flush
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The strip's cells sit **on** the orange ground with a 2px gap, which is what makes the orange read as the rule between them. With the whitelist the grid holds five cells against `sm:grid-cols-2 lg:grid-cols-3` — 3+2 at `lg`, 2+2+1 at `sm` — so both breakpoints left one empty track. An empty track there is not whitespace: it is an **orange rectangle the size of a record cell**, sitting beside the one deliberate orange cell (the feature). It reads as a hole. The old 7-record set happened to divide evenly, so this never appeared before.
- **Decision:** A four-line `lastCellSpan(count)` gives the final `<li>` `sm:col-span-2` / `lg:col-span-2|3` when the count leaves a remainder, so the last row fills its tracks. Classes are literals so Tailwind keeps them.
- **Alternatives considered:** *`lg:grid-cols-5`, all five in one row* — rejected: „Најдобар стрелец на сите времиња на Беласица" over a 76-character value would set ~5 lines deep in a 240px cell. *Give the `<ul>` a navy background so the empty track reads navy* — rejected: the 2px gaps would then also be navy, killing the orange-rule motif that defines the component. *Leave the gap* — rejected: it is the most visible thing in the section. *Cap the strip at a count that divides evenly* — rejected: the six are the brief's editorial set, not a layout convenience.
- **Consequences:** The strip closes flush at any count, not just today's five, and no record is dropped to make the grid tidy. Verified at 1280 (`sm:col-span-2 lg:col-span-2` on the final cell, row full) and at 375, where the grid is single-column and the helper is inert.
- **Links:** D-3.05b-1; brand.md §Components (Scoreboard); `src/components/home/ClubRecords.tsx`.

### D-3.05b-3 · 2026-07-30 · The lightbox is opt-in on `PhotoGrid`, and the overlay never imports Sanity
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** `PhotoGrid` is shared: the season page renders the season's scans through it and **`/legendi/<slug>` renders a person's photos through the same component**. The brief scopes the lightbox to the season page's „Фотографии" only. Separately, the overlay needs image URLs, and the obvious shortcut — importing `urlFor` into the client component — would pull the Sanity image builder into the client bundle.
- **Decision:** A new `lightbox` boolean prop on `PhotoGrid`, default `false`, set only by the season page — the additive-prop pattern `PhotoFrame`'s `fit` already established (D-2.02-7). `PhotoGrid` stays a **server** component: it builds every overlay URL with `urlFor(...).width(2400).fit("max")` and passes only plain serialisable data (`id · url · width · height · caption · date`) into the new client `PhotoLightbox`. `fit("max")` rather than a bare `width()` because Sanity's `w` upscales: a 1080px scan asked for 2400 comes back interpolated, which is exactly what brand.md's mixed-quality rule forbids — a large scan is capped, a small one is left at its native size.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Make the whole grid a client component* — rejected: it would move the frames, captions and reveal into the bundle for no gain, and the person page would pay for it too. *Turn the lightbox on everywhere* — rejected: out of scope this phase (the brief's task 20). *Render the overlay in a portal* — considered; not needed, since `aria-modal="true"` on a `position: fixed` dialog already scopes AT to it, and a portal adds a mount point for no measured benefit.
- **Consequences:** Verified that `/legendi/petar-andreev` renders its grid with **0** lightbox trigger buttons and no dialog — the person page is byte-identical in behaviour. `ArchivePhoto` gained optional `width`/`height`, projected only by the season page's GROQ; the fallback (1200×800, the grid's own 3/2) never fires there. The Impeccable `broken-image` detector fired on this file for most of the phase; it turned out to be matching a literal `<img>` written in **comment prose**, not an element at all (the drifting line number as the file was edited was the tell). Resolved by rewording the comment — **no ignore rule, no inline waiver, no design change**; the detector now returns `[]` across all six changed files.
- **Links:** D-2.02-7 (the additive-prop precedent); brand.md §Photo treatment (mixed-quality rule); `src/components/archive/PhotoGrid.tsx`; `src/components/archive/PhotoLightbox.tsx`; `src/app/(site)/arhiva/[slug]/page.tsx`.

### D-3.05b-4 · 2026-07-30 · The homepage keeps its graceful fallback under `fetchOrThrow`, unlike the season and person templates
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** Task 21 wraps `HOME_QUERY` in `fetchOrThrow` „the same way the season and person templates do". Those two templates deliberately **throw** on final failure (D-3.02F-C-1), because a silent `null` there became a 404 for a season that exists. The homepage already had `try { … } catch { data = EMPTY }`, which renders a placeholder front door.
- **Decision:** Wrap the read in `fetchOrThrow` — so all five attempts, the backoff and the per-attempt log apply — but **keep** the surrounding catch and the `EMPTY` fallback.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Throw like the templates* — rejected: their reasoning does not transfer. A missing season page is an invisible hole in an archive; a homepage that fails to build takes the whole site down, and a visibly placeholder front door is the more honest degradation. *Leave the bare `client.fetch`* — rejected, it is the task.
- **Consequences:** The homepage now absorbs the CDN wobble that 3.02F measured, and logs it either way; on genuine failure it still renders placeholders and never invented filler. The other four uncovered read sites the 3.02F report names are deliberately untouched.
- **Links:** D-3.02F-C-1; D-3.05a-9; `src/sanity/fetch.ts`; `src/app/(site)/page.tsx`.

### D-3.05b-5 · 2026-07-30 · The overlay's scroll lock goes on the scrolling element, not on `<body>` — the common recipe measured as a no-op
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The lightbox first shipped the usual `document.body.style.overflow = "hidden"`. Measured with the overlay open, the page still scrolled: `window.scrollTo` moved it 3000 → 3800, and `getComputedStyle(body).overflow` read back `hidden` — the tell that the body's overflow had **not** propagated to the viewport, since propagation leaves the body computing `visible`. `document.scrollingElement` is `<html>` on this site, so the lock was decorative.
- **Decision:** Apply `overflow: hidden` (and the scrollbar-width padding compensation) to `document.documentElement`. Still `overflow` rather than the `position: fixed` technique, so the scroll offset is never lost and there is nothing to restore.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Lock both `<html>` and `<body>`* — rejected as cargo-culting; the root is where the scrolling is, and locking two elements hides which one matters. *`position: fixed` + restore* — rejected: it discards and re-applies the offset, which is a jump waiting to happen, for no benefit here.
- **Consequences:** Verified open→close on a real season page: `documentElement` overflow `hidden` while open and `visible` after, `body` computing `visible` throughout, the page unmoved on open, and the scroll offset **identical** before and after (17269.5 both times). ⚠️ Worth knowing for any future modal: `overflow: hidden` blocks *user* scrolling, not `window.scrollTo`, so a `scrollTo` probe is not a valid test of a scroll lock — it was what made the first diagnosis ambiguous.
- **Links:** `src/components/archive/PhotoLightbox.tsx`.

### D-3.05b-6 · 2026-07-30 · Neither focus restoration nor the entry motion may depend on `requestAnimationFrame`
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** Two parts of the overlay were built on rAF: focus returned to the triggering thumbnail inside a `requestAnimationFrame` callback, and the entry motion mounted the dialog at `opacity-0` and flipped a React state flag on the next frame. **rAF does not fire while the document is hidden.** Measured with `visibilityState: "hidden"`: on close, `document.activeElement` was `BODY` and the trigger was never refocused; and with the dialog open its class list was stuck on `opacity-0` — a fully transparent modal still trapping the keyboard.
- **Decision:** Focus restoration moves into a `useEffect` keyed on the open state, which runs on commit after React removes the dialog and needs no frame. The entry motion becomes a pure CSS animation (`animate-in fade-in slide-in-from-bottom-1`, 160ms ease-out) from **`tw-animate-css` 1.4.0 — already a pinned dependency and already imported by `globals.css`**, so no dependency and no `@theme` change. The `entered` state is deleted.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Keep rAF and accept it* — rejected: focus management should not depend on the compositor ever painting, and both failures were reproducible. *A `setTimeout(0)` instead of rAF* — rejected: it swaps one timing guess for another. *Hand-written `@keyframes` in `globals.css`* — rejected once `tw-animate-css` turned out to be present, since the brief bars new tokens and this needs no new CSS at all.
- **Consequences:** Verified under the exact failing condition (`visibilityState: "hidden"`): focus now returns to the triggering thumbnail on both Escape and backdrop-click. The animation's `animation-fill-mode` is **`none`**, so the dialog's *resting* state is fully opaque and the transparent state exists only while the animation is genuinely running — the previous version's resting state was invisible, which is the substantive difference. On paint it settles at `opacity: 1`, `transform: none`. Honest scope note: with a **visible** page rAF fires within a frame, so neither defect was reachable by a user watching the screen; both were latent, and the fixes remove a class of failure rather than a reported bug.
- **Links:** D-3.05b-5; brand.md §Motion; `src/components/archive/PhotoLightbox.tsx`; `src/app/globals.css` (unchanged).

### D-3.05b-7 · 2026-07-30 · The crest keeps `unoptimized` — now for a structural reason, and D-3.05-11's 402 has cleared
- **Status:** Accepted (refines D-3.05-11; does not reverse it)
- **Context:** D-3.05-11 set `unoptimized` on the crest because every `/_next/image` variant 402'd on this account. This phase points the header and hero at the new vector `/crest.svg`, which raises the question of whether the workaround is still needed. Measured against production before deciding: `/_next/image` now returns **200** on the season gallery's assets at `w=640`, `w=1920` **and** `w=3840` — the quota has recovered.
- **Decision:** Keep `unoptimized` on the crest regardless, and record the reason as structural rather than circumstantial: **Next refuses to optimize SVG** unless `dangerouslyAllowSVG` is set, and an SVG has no raster variants worth generating. Do **not** re-enable optimization anywhere as part of this phase.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Drop `unoptimized` now that the quota is back* — rejected: it would 400 on the SVG, and re-enabling an optimizer path is not this phase's scope. *Set `dangerouslyAllowSVG`* — rejected outright: it relaxes a real security boundary to buy nothing for a 31KB static asset.
- **Consequences:** `/crest.svg` returns 200 `image/svg+xml`, is fetched once at 31,291 bytes, and produces **zero** `/_next/image` requests on either surface. The lightbox's own `next/image` **does** use the optimizer, which the same measurement shows is safe again. `public/crest-ui.webp` stays as the raster fallback and the `/crest.png` lineage's source; nothing references it after this phase. The rebuilt artwork is 916×1294 (ratio 0.7079 vs the old 0.7080), so the retained `width={400} height={565}` renders identically and `layout.tsx`'s Open Graph block stays correct and untouched.
- **Links:** D-3.05-11; D-crest-1; D-crest-2; `src/components/SiteHeader.tsx`; `src/app/(site)/page.tsx`.

### D-3.07-1 · 2026-07-31 · Route slug `/pravni-informacii`, Latin-transliterated like every other route
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The legal page needed a URL. Every existing route is a Latin transliteration of its Macedonian label (`/za-nas`, `/kontakt`, `/legendi`, `/statistika`, `/arhiva`) per D-0.00-4, and the page's own label is „Правни информации".
- **Decision:** `/pravni-informacii` — the transliteration of the label, consistent with the six existing routes. No trailing English fallback, no `/legal`.
- **Alternatives considered:** *`/legal`* — rejected: the site is Macedonian-only (`lang="mk"`), and an English slug is the one URL a Macedonian reader could not guess. *`/pravni-informacii` under a `(legal)` route group* — rejected: a route group buys nothing for a single page and would move it out of `(site)`, costing it the header/footer chrome the brief requires.
- **Consequences:** The URL is stable and guessable. If a privacy policy or cookie notice is ever added, it should sit as a sibling route on the same convention rather than as a section of this page.
- **Links:** D-0.00-4; brief 3.07 task 1; `src/app/(site)/pravni-informacii/page.tsx`.

### D-3.07-2 · 2026-07-31 · The legal link lives in the footer's bottom bar, not in the nav and not as a fourth column
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The page must be reachable from every page, but it is not a destination anyone browses to. The brief fixed the placement; this entry records the reasoning and what it costs.
- **Decision:** „Правни информации" renders in the footer's **copyright bottom bar**, beside the © line, grouped with it in a flex row so the two read as one legal/administrative cluster. `src/lib/nav.ts` and `SiteHeader.tsx` are untouched and stay the single source for the six real destinations.
- **Alternatives considered:** *A seventh `NAV_ITEMS` entry* — rejected: it would put a legal notice in the header at equal weight with „Архива" and „Легенди", and would also add it to the footer's „Навигација" column, which reuses the same array. *A fourth footer column* — rejected: the footer already runs `1.3fr + 3×1fr`, and a fifth track would either crush the brand block or wrap awkwardly at `md`. *Inside the „Контакт" column* — rejected: it is not a contact channel.
- **Consequences:** The link is on every page in the `(site)` group (verified on `/`, `/arhiva/<slug>`, `/legendi/<slug>`, `/statistika`, `/za-nas`, `/kontakt` and the legal page itself) at `text-small` with the footer's own hover/focus treatment and a 144×35px hit area. Accepted downside: at 375 the bottom bar wraps to two rows — the identity line, then the link + ©. That is the intended wrap, not a defect.
- **Links:** D-3.07-1; `src/components/SiteFooter.tsx`; brand.md §Components („Footer").

### D-3.07-3 · 2026-07-31 · The rights posture is takedown-on-request, not a bare non-liability claim
- **Status:** Accepted (owner-authored copy; recorded because it changes what the site promises)
- **Context:** The archive publishes ~889 photographs, most of them third-party screenshots whose authors are unknown (`facts.md` §Content provenance; the P0.1 audit caveat). Until now the site made no public statement about that at all. The owner-supplied §Copy resolves it with an explicit, unconditional promise rather than a disclaimer.
- **Decision:** Render §5 as written: copyright stays with its owners, the archive claims nothing, authors are credited where known, and any rights holder who asks gets the material removed „без одлагање и без прашања" — with the page stating that no legal process is needed. §7 limits liability but routes complaints to „контактирајте нè прво".
- **Alternatives considered:** *A conventional „we are not liable" disclaimer alone* — rejected by the copy the owner supplied, and rightly: a non-liability claim does not help a rights holder who simply wants a photo taken down, and it reads as defensive on an archive that depends on goodwill. *Softening the promise to „ќе разгледаме"* — not considered available: §Copy is final and native-reviewed, and weakening it would be the executor overruling the owner.
- **Consequences:** The archive is now publicly committed to unconditional takedown on request, and `info@belasicahistory.mk` is the channel that must actually be watched for it. This is an **operational** commitment, not just copy — it is listed as an owed item for Lazar. It also partly answers the standing OV-RIGHTS caveat by giving affected parties a stated route.
- **Links:** OV-RIGHTS; `facts.md` §Content provenance; brief 3.07 §Copy §5/§7/§10.

### D-3.07-4 · 2026-07-31 · The email link drops the text-link role's uppercase — and only that
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** brand.md §Components specs the text link as „condensed caps with a 3px orange underline". Applied literally to `info@belasicahistory.mk`, the `u-link` role renders `INFO@BELASICAHISTORY.MK`.
- **Decision:** Compose the link from the role's own anatomy — 3px orange underline, hover swaps the underline to the text colour, navy ink, `focusOnPaper` ring — but omit `text-transform: uppercase` and the condensed display face, keeping the address in body type at its true case. Measured: `17px`, `rgb(13,31,60)` on paper (14.95:1), `border-bottom 3px rgb(238,122,22)`, `text-transform: none`.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Use `u-link` as-is* — rejected: an email address is a value to be copied, not a label to be styled; uppercasing it misrepresents the string and reads as shouting on a page about restraint. *A separate `u-link--plain` role class in `globals.css`* — rejected: one call site does not justify a new global role, and brand.md's role set is deliberately small.
- **Consequences:** Two link treatments now exist on paper surfaces — the condensed-caps `u-link` for navigation-ish links and this lowercase variant for literal values. If a second literal-value link ever appears (a phone number, an external URL), promote this to a role class rather than copying the utility string a third time. The same composition is used on `/kontakt` at `text-body`.
- **Links:** brand.md §Components („Buttons" → text link), §Color; `src/app/(site)/pravni-informacii/page.tsx`; `src/app/(site)/kontakt/page.tsx`.

### D-3.07-5 · 2026-07-31 · `/kontakt`'s email chip was cleared too — PL-3 was open on two surfaces, not one
- **Status:** Accepted (scope call by the executor)
- **Context:** The brief's Task 5 names only the footer, but Task 6c instructs „Clear PL-3 (contact email)". The register records PL-3 as **„Open — VISIBLE on two surfaces"**: the footer *and* `/kontakt`'s „Директен контакт" block (D-2.07-4). Clearing only the footer would have left `/kontakt` rendering „[PLACEHOLDER: адреса за е-пошта]" directly above a footer, on the same page, showing the real address.
- **Decision:** Replace the `/kontakt` chip with the same `mailto:` link, so PL-3 closes on both surfaces at once. The change is one element; the page's socials chip (PL-15) and Formspree chip (PL-14) are untouched, and no other part of `/kontakt` moved.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Footer only, leave PL-3 half-open* — rejected: it satisfies Task 5's letter while breaking Task 6c and shipping a page that contradicts itself. *Clear the chip and also wire the Formspree endpoint* — rejected: the endpoint is unset configuration (PL-14 / OV-8) and genuinely out of scope; the form stays disabled.
- **Consequences:** `/kontakt` now shows 2 chips in `<main>` (Formspree, socials) instead of 3, verified against the built HTML. `src/app/(site)/kontakt/page.tsx` appears in a diff the brief's „Outputs" section did not list — flagged in the completion report so the deviation is visible rather than silent.
- **Links:** D-2.07-4; PL-3; PL-14; PL-15; OV-8.

### D-3.07-6 · 2026-07-31 · The address is one constant in `src/lib/facts.ts`, not three string literals
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** `info@belasicahistory.mk` now renders on three surfaces. `src/lib/facts.ts` already exists as the place VERIFIED `facts.md` strings live as code constants, and OV-6's resolution set the precedent that such a string „should end up in exactly one place".
- **Decision:** Add `CONTACT_EMAIL` to `src/lib/facts.ts` with its verification source in the docblock; all three call sites import it and use it for both the `href` and the visible text, so the link target can never drift from the label.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Hardcode it per component* — rejected: three copies of a fact that changes as a unit, and exactly the drift OV-6 was opened about. *Read it from `siteSettings` in Sanity* — rejected: it would make a static legal page fetch, and the CMS field carrying unverified copy is precisely the OV-6 trap.
- **Consequences:** Changing the address is a one-line edit. A grep for `@` in `src/**/*.tsx` now returns no address literal at all — the only remaining email-shaped string in `src/` is `ime@example.com`, the contact form input's `placeholder` attribute (a typing hint on an IANA-reserved domain, not an archive address).
- **Links:** OV-6; `src/lib/facts.ts`; `facts.md` §Contact & links.

### D-3.07-7 · 2026-07-31 · The copy is a data array, so „verbatim" is machine-checkable
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The brief's hardest requirement is that ten sections match §Copy „character for character — verified by diffing the rendered text against this brief, not by eye". Ten hand-written JSX sections make that diff awkward and let per-section styling drift.
- **Decision:** Hold the visible text in a `SECTIONS` array of `{id, heading, blocks}` with a three-case `Block` union (`p` / `ul` / `email`), rendered through one `LegalBlock` component in the same file. No new component file, no new token.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Ten literal JSX sections* — rejected: it invites §7 to be styled unlike §4, which on a legal page invites the reader to weigh them differently. *Portable Text from Sanity* — rejected outright: this text must not be editable without a `facts.md` change, and a legal statement that can be silently edited in a CMS is the opposite of what the page is for.
- **Consequences:** The rendered text was extracted from the built HTML and `diff`ed against a transcription of the brief: **41/41 copy lines identical, zero differences.** Adding a section is a data edit. Downside: the copy is not visible as JSX when skimming the render tree.
- **Links:** brief 3.07 Definition of Done; `src/app/(site)/pravni-informacii/page.tsx`.

### D-3.07-8 · 2026-07-31 · No `revalidate` on this route, breaking the D-1.05-4 house habit deliberately
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** Every other route sets `export const revalidate = 60` „for consistency", including `/za-nas` and `/kontakt`, which read no Sanity content either.
- **Decision:** Omit it. This page fetches nothing, so an ISR window is a value with no referent — it would suggest to the next reader that something here can change without a deploy, which for a legal statement is the wrong signal.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Add `revalidate = 60` to match* — rejected: consistency with a value that does nothing is not consistency. *Also strip it from `/za-nas` and `/kontakt`* — rejected: out of scope, and both are expected to gain Sanity reads.
- **Consequences:** The build reports `/pravni-informacii` as `○ (Static)` with an **empty** Revalidate column, unlike its neighbours' `1m` — visible confirmation in the build table that the page is a build-time constant.
- **Links:** D-1.05-4; build output, Phase 3.07.

### D-3.07-9 · 2026-07-31 · The two quote pairs render as „…“, the repo's existing convention
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** §Copy contains two quoted phrases — „ФК Беласица" in §6 and „како што е" in §7. The opening glyph is unambiguously U+201E (the low-9 opener used in Macedonian), but the closing glyph could be read as either U+201C („…“, the Macedonian/German pair) or U+201D („…”, the English pair).
- **Decision:** Render U+201C, giving „ФК Беласица“ and „како што е“. This is the pair Macedonian typography uses with a U+201E opener, and it matches the repo: `src/**/*.tsx` contains **4** instances of U+201C and **0** of U+201D.
- **Alternatives considered:** *U+201D* — rejected: it would pair a Macedonian opener with an English closer and would be the only U+201D in the codebase. *A straight `"`* — rejected: it discards the typography the copy specifies.
- **Consequences:** This is the single character-level judgement made against §Copy; everything else is a literal transcription. Flagged in the completion report so the owner can overrule it in one edit if the intent was U+201D.
- **Links:** brief 3.07 §Copy §6/§7; D-3.07-7.

### D-3.07-10 · 2026-07-31 · The snapshot was stale by one phase, not „several" — corrected against the tree, and 3.06a's missing paperwork recorded rather than invented
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The brief states `current-state.md` „still reads as if Phase 1.01 just closed" and `file-map.md` „stops around 2.04". Both are wrong: the snapshot was current through **3.05b-Code** and the file map through 3.05b. The real gap is that **3.06a** (merged to `main` as PR #35 + commits `bcd6196`/`2c94141`) filed **no completion report and logged no decisions** — `grep -c "D-3.06" decisions.md` returns **0**.
- **Decision:** Sync the snapshot and the file map from the working tree rather than from the brief's description of them, and record 3.06a's shipped surface from its diff and commits. Do **not** author a completion report or back-fill `D-3.06a-*` decisions for another phase — 2.06 set the precedent of not writing into another phase's namespace, and inventing rationale for work this session did not do would put fiction in an append-only log.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Rewrite the snapshot to the pre-3.02 state the brief describes* — rejected: it would destroy accurate history to match an inaccurate instruction. *Back-fill `D-3.06a-*` from the diff* — rejected: the diff shows *what* changed, never *why*, and a decision entry without its real rationale is an assertion.
- **Consequences:** 3.06a's decisions and completion report remain permanently missing — the **fourth** occurrence of a brief written against repo state that does not match the repo (cf. D-2.03-1, D-2.04-2, D-2.06-1). Carried into the snapshot's Known issues so it is not rediscovered a fifth time.
- **Links:** D-2.03-1; D-2.04-2; D-2.06-1; commits `b637b5e`, `bcd6196`, `2c94141`.
### D-3.09-1 · 2026-07-31 · `PhotoFrame` splits in two so a client component can render a photo without importing Sanity
- **Status:** Accepted (extends D-3.05b-3 from the lightbox to the card layer)
- **Context:** `/legendi`'s client bundle contained the whole `@sanity/image-url` builder. `LegendsBrowser` is a `"use client"` component, so everything it imports crosses the boundary — and it reaches the builder through `RoleBandGrid → LegendCard → PhotoFrame → urlFor`. Measured in the built output: the route's page chunk was **17,266 bytes** and carried `cdn.sanity.io`, `projectId`, `dataset`, `hotspot` and `crop`. Every other public route's page chunk scored zero on the same fingerprints. The phase's Definition of Done requires no Sanity import in any client bundle.
- **Decision:** Split the frame. `PhotoFrameView` (new, `src/components/home/PhotoFrameView.tsx`) owns the markup and takes a resolved `FramedImage = { src, objectPosition }`; `PhotoFrame` keeps its existing API, resolves the asset through the new `framedImage()` helper (`src/sanity/frame.ts`) and delegates. `LegendCard` imports **only** the view, and `LegendCardData.portrait` becomes a resolved `FramedImage`. The two server call sites (`/legendi/page.tsx`, the homepage marquee) resolve their portraits before handing them down. Rendered markup is byte-identical.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Pass the server-rendered bands into `LegendsBrowser` as `children`* — rejected: the browser filters individual cards, and it cannot hide individual `<li>`s inside an opaque children tree. *Make `/legendi` search a server action or a URL param* — rejected: that is a layout/behaviour redesign of a route this phase is explicitly barred from restructuring. *Leave it* — rejected: it is a stated DoD item, and it is 17 KB of client JS whose only job is to build a string the server already knows.
- **Consequences:** `/legendi` page chunk **6.74 kB → 3.79 kB**, First Load JS **126 → 124 kB**; every per-page client chunk now scores **0** on the Sanity fingerprints. New rule to hold: a **client** component must import `PhotoFrameView`, never `PhotoFrame`. The seam is documented in both files.
- **Links:** D-3.05b-3; `src/components/home/PhotoFrameView.tsx`; `src/sanity/frame.ts`; `src/components/legends/LegendCard.tsx`.

### D-3.09-2 · 2026-07-31 · Exactly one priority image per template, chosen from the Lighthouse trace — and the crest is not it
- **Status:** Accepted (refines D-3.05b-7; does not reverse it)
- **Context:** The crest carried `priority` in both `SiteHeader` and the homepage hero, which put a `<link rel=preload as=image>` for a **33 KB** file on the critical path of **every** route — while never being the LCP element on any of them. Meanwhile `/arhiva`'s real LCP, the first season card's photograph, carried no priority hint at all (`priorityHinted: false` in the trace). Both facts came from the trace, not from reading the JSX.
- **Decision:** Read the LCP element from `lcp-discovery-insight` per route, then give the priority hint to exactly that element and nothing else. `SeasonCard` and `LegendCard` gain a `priority` prop, set on the first card only. The crest becomes a plain `<img loading="lazy">` in both places: it is `unoptimized` (D-3.05b-7), so `next/image` was contributing nothing but the preload. Routes whose LCP is **text** (`/statistika`, `/za-nas`, `/kontakt`, `/arhiva/1931-32`) now carry **no** image preload at all.
- **Alternatives considered:** *`priority` off but `loading="eager"`* — measured, **rejected**: React 19 hoists any non-lazy SSR image into a preload of its own, so the link survived. *No `loading` attribute* — measured, rejected for the same reason (`eager` is the HTML default). *Inline the SVG* — rejected: 89 KB of markup in every page's HTML, uncacheable across routes. *Shrink `crest.svg`* — rejected: the artwork is owner-iterated (3.06a) and re-tracing it to save bytes is not this phase's call.
- **Consequences:** Verified in the served HTML — exactly **one** `fetchPriority="high"` image per template, and it is the measured LCP element. Honest note: removing the crest preload moved the score **not at all** (within ±2 run-to-run noise) — Lantern appears to model the preloads as parallel rather than blocking. Kept anyway for the genuine 33 KB reduction in critical-path bytes on a real connection. CLS is unchanged at **0.000**: the `<img>` keeps explicit `width`/`height`.
- **Links:** D-3.05b-7; D-3.05-11; `src/components/SiteHeader.tsx`; `src/app/(site)/page.tsx`; `src/components/archive/SeasonCard.tsx`; `src/components/legends/LegendCard.tsx`.

### D-3.09-3 · 2026-07-31 · `Reveal` gains `immediate`, because the reveal was hiding the LCP element
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The single largest performance defect in the phase, and it was invisible without the trace. On `/arhiva` and `/legendi` the LCP element is the first card's photograph, and that card sits inside a `Reveal` wrapper — so it is held at `opacity: 0` until hydration, an IntersectionObserver callback, a state update and a 260ms transition have all completed. An element at `opacity: 0` cannot be the LCP, so the paint simply waits. Measured `elementRenderDelay`: **370 ms** on `/arhiva`, **331 ms** on `/legendi`, against **14 ms** on the homepage, whose hero was never wrapped.
- **Decision:** `Reveal` takes `immediate`. When set it omits `data-reveal` entirely — so the CSS hidden state has nothing to match, there is no class to wait for and no observer to register — and skips the stagger delay. It is set on the LCP card of `/arhiva` and `/legendi`, tied to the same `priority` flag so the two can never drift apart.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Drop the reveal from card grids altogether* — rejected: it is brand.md §Motion and applies to 96/160 cards below the fold where it is doing its job. *Reveal the whole first row (3 cards)* — considered; kept to the single LCP card because mobile (where the gate is missed) is one column, and desktop already passes. The visible consequence is that card 1 appears solid while cards 2–3 fade in 0/60 ms later, which is the existing stagger extended by one step. *Shorten the transition* — rejected: it treats the symptom and costs the motion its character.
- **Consequences:** `/arhiva` mobile **86 → 92** and `/legendi` **89 → 94** from this change alone, `elementRenderDelay` down to 41 ms and 46 ms. Conceptually right as well as faster: content already in the first viewport has nothing to reveal *from*, because the visitor has not scrolled yet. The LCP card ships with no `data-reveal` attribute at all, so no code path can hide it.
- **Links:** D-1.05-5; brand.md §Motion; `src/components/home/Reveal.tsx`.

### D-3.09-4 · 2026-07-31 · AVIF added ahead of WebP — a format change, explicitly not a quality change
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** After the image and reveal fixes, the remaining LCP cost on the homepage and the rich season page was **bytes**, not delay: the trace's `resourceLoadDuration` dominated its breakdown. D-3.04d-5 forbids reducing photograph quality to buy simulator points, which rules out the usual lever.
- **Decision:** Set `images.formats: ["image/avif", "image/webp"]` in `next.config.ts`. `quality` stays at `next/image`'s default 75 on every photograph — not one archive scan is degraded. Browsers without AVIF negotiate down to WebP and then to the original.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Lower `quality`* — refused outright (D-3.04d-5). *Tune `deviceSizes`/`imageSizes`* — measured and rejected: Lighthouse mobile emulates DPR **1.75** at 412 px, so a 372 px card needs 651 device px and Next already picks the correct 750 bucket; there was no waste to remove.
- **Consequences:** Verified by content negotiation on the running server — an AVIF-capable client receives `image/avif` at 135,521 bytes where a WebP-only client receives `image/webp` at 150,776 for the same variant. Worth **+1 to +2 points** on the routes that were bytes-bound. Cost: AVIF encodes more slowly on first request; these are Vercel-cached optimized images, so it is a one-off per variant.
- **Links:** D-3.04d-5; `next.config.ts`.

### D-3.09-5 · 2026-07-31 · The skip link takes the project focus ring
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** Measured on a real Tab press, the skip link's focus outline was `1px auto` — Chrome's **default** ring, not the project's 3px one. It was the only interactive element on the site still falling back to the UA default, and it is the first thing a keyboard user reaches.
- **Decision:** Add `focusOnNavy` to the skip link. It lands on its own navy chip, so it takes the orange ring like every other control on a navy surface.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Leave it* — rejected: Chrome's default ring is visible but is neither the design system's nor guaranteed to hold its ratio against a navy chip; and the site-wide rule from D-3.05-4 is that focus is `.u-focus`, everywhere.
- **Consequences:** Its SC 2.4.11 behaviour was re-checked at the same time and is **not** a failure: `focus:z-50` against the header's `z-40` means the link paints **over** the sticky bar. Verified by hit test rather than by rectangle — `document.elementFromPoint` at the focused link's centre returns the link itself. A rectangle-only check reports a false failure here.
- **Links:** D-3.05-4; `src/app/(site)/layout.tsx`.

### D-3.09-6 · 2026-07-31 · Scroll regions must be `position: relative`, or their `sr-only` labels break page reflow
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** A genuine SC 1.4.10 failure that Lighthouse's 100 did not see. At a 375 px viewport `/statistika` scrolled **the page itself** 279 px sideways (`documentElement.scrollWidth` 654 against `clientWidth` 375, and `window.scrollX` reaching 279 after a `scrollTo`). Cause: `StatTable`'s `sr-only` column labels are `position: absolute`, and the `overflow-x-auto` region around them was `position: static` — so their containing block was the **initial** one, they escaped the region's clip, and they extended the document's scrollable width. The offending span („Бодови") sat at **x = 654**.
- **Decision:** Add `relative` to every horizontal scroll region — `StatTable`, `StandingsTable`, `SeasonAnchorNav`, `DecadeJumpNav` — making each a containing block so absolutely-positioned descendants are clipped with everything else.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Move the `sr-only` text out of the scroll region* — rejected: it belongs to its `<th>`, and detaching it would break the column's programmatic name. *`overflow-x: clip` on `<html>`* — rejected: it hides the symptom site-wide and would mask a real overflow later. *Drop the `sr-only` labels* — rejected: they carry the full Macedonian column names (D-2.02-14).
- **Consequences:** Page horizontal scroll is now **0** on all nine routes at 375 px **and** at 320 px (= 400 % zoom at 1280). The tables still scroll inside their own regions exactly as designed — measured at 375 px, `scrollWidth` 520 vs `clientWidth` 333, scrolled to 187, while the page stayed at 0. This is a non-layout fix, so it is inside the phase's remit on `/statistika`. `StandingsTable`'s `focus-visible:outline-none` + `ring-*` (box-shadow) pair — the last one on the site, the shape D-3.05-4 removed everywhere else — was replaced with the unlayered `.u-focus` outline in the same edit.
- **Links:** D-3.05-4; D-2.02-14; D-2.02-10; `src/components/stats/StatTable.tsx`; `src/components/archive/StandingsTable.tsx`.

### D-3.09-7 · 2026-07-31 · The breadcrumb separator moves `paper/40 → paper/60` (a measured AA failure)
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The „/" separator between crumbs computed to **3.48:1** against the navy block — under the 4.5:1 floor. It is `aria-hidden`, but it is still visible text a sighted reader uses to parse the trail, so the decorative exemption is not safely available. Found only because the contrast sweep composites alpha correctly; a regex-based sweep mis-reads Tailwind v4's `oklab()` output entirely.
- **Decision:** `text-paper/40 → text-paper/60`, measured at **6.14:1** on the real composite. The paper variant's `text-mist` moves to `text-neutral-500` for the same reason — mist is a hairline *border* colour, not a glyph colour.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Declare it decoration and leave it* — rejected: it is rendered text carrying visible structure, and the phase's DoD asks for zero contrast failures. *`paper/50`* — measured at 4.68:1; rejected as too close to the floor to survive a future token nudge. *Change the token in `brand.md`* — unnecessary: an existing opacity step fixes it, so **no `brand.md` token changed in this phase**.
- **Consequences:** The separator stays a clear step quieter than the crumbs themselves (9.93:1), so the hierarchy the design intends is unchanged. Zero contrast failures now on all nine routes.
- **Links:** D-1.02-1; D-2.02-9; `src/components/archive/Breadcrumb.tsx`.

### D-3.09-8 · 2026-07-31 · Font preloading is KEPT, and two mobile routes stay at 94 as a result
- **Status:** Accepted (applies D-3.04d-5's principle to type)
- **Context:** After every image fix, `/` and `/arhiva` sit at **94** on mobile — one point short — and LCP is the only failing audit on either. `/arhiva`'s simulated critical path carries 204 KB at High priority, of which the four preloaded woff2 files are **92 KB** and the LCP image only 37 KB. Dropping the preload was built and measured, in two variants.
- **Decision:** **Keep both fonts preloaded** and report the two routes at 94 with the tradeoff named, rather than take the points.
- **Alternatives considered, with numbers:** *`preload: false` on both* — `/arhiva` **98**, `/` **95**; **rejected**, because homepage FCP became bimodal at **760 / 1518 / 1512 ms** across three runs against a stable 759–809 ms with preload. *`preload: false` on Golos only (Oswald kept)* — `/arhiva` 95, `/` 94, FCP still bimodal at 805 / 1213 / 1214 ms; rejected for the same reason. *Drop the `latin` subset* (58 KB of the 92) — rejected on inspection: digits live in the Latin range (`u+00??`) and this archive is full of them (season titles, stat tables, counts), so every number on the site would fall back to a system face.
- **Consequences:** Two of nine mobile routes miss the ≥95 gate by one point, both reaching 95 in one of their three runs. This is the same trade D-3.04d-5 refuses for photographs: doubling and destabilising the visitor's first paint to move a simulated number is a worse site, not a better one. **Escalated to the owner** in the completion report as a named tradeoff rather than decided silently. If it is ever revisited, the honest lever is a **subset-per-script build** of the two faces, not `preload: false`.
- **Links:** D-3.04d-5; D-3.05a-2; D-3.05-7; `src/app/fonts.ts` (unchanged); `docs/audits/Part-3-Phase-09-results.md` §2.

### D-3.03b-1 · 2026-07-31 · The Formspree endpoint is wired, and OV-8 is cleared against the live form
- **Status:** Accepted (completes the step D-0.00-7 assigned to 3.03, which shipped without it)
- **Context:** `/kontakt`'s form has been fully built since 2.07 but rendered inside a disabled `<fieldset>` on production for two weeks, because `NEXT_PUBLIC_FORMSPREE_ENDPOINT` was never set (PL-14). 3.03 was supposed to wire it and did not, so the visible state on the live site was still the notice „Формуларот сѐ уште не е активен". The owner supplied the endpoint `https://formspree.io/f/mojgzzep`.
- **Decision:** Set the endpoint in **`.env.local`** (local) and on **Vercel, Production + Preview** — matching the placement of the three `NEXT_PUBLIC_SANITY_*` vars, and matching OV-8's own instruction. `.env.example` keeps an **empty** value: it is a template, and the disabled-form fallback (D-2.07-3) must stay reachable for a fresh clone. **No secret enters the repo** — a Formspree form action is public by design (D-2.07-2), which is why it can be `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` at all.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Hardcode the endpoint in the component* — rejected: it would delete the disabled-form fallback and the placeholder path with it, and put deployment config in source. *Commit the value in `.env.example`* — rejected: `.env.example` documents shape, not values, and a populated template would silently point every clone at the owner's live inbox. *Add it to Vercel's Development environment too* — rejected: the three existing Sanity vars are Production + Preview only, and `.env.local` already covers local dev; adding it would also make `vercel env pull` a trap that overwrites `.env.local`.
- **Consequences:** **PL-14 retires and OV-8 clears.** Both states were exercised end-to-end in the browser at 1280 rather than against a fixture: `idle → „Се испраќа…" → success` against the **real** endpoint (Formspree returned 2xx — the handler throws on non-ok, so the success panel is itself the proof), and `idle → „Се испраќа…" → error` against a deliberately dead endpoint (`/f/zzzznotreal`), which put the `role="alert"` banner **above** the form, **retained all three typed values**, re-enabled the fields and set the button to „Испрати повторно". One real test message was sent to the owner's Formspree inbox, subject-marked „ТЕСТ" — **it should be deleted there.** ⚠️ `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` is **inlined at build time**, so the Vercel variable does nothing until a redeploy; production picks it up when this branch merges. PL-3 (email) and PL-15 (socials) are **untouched and still open** — the form is a separate channel from the direct contacts.
- **Links:** D-0.00-7; D-2.07-2; D-2.07-3; OV-8; PL-14; `.env.example`; `src/components/contact/ContactForm.tsx`; `src/app/(site)/kontakt/page.tsx`.

### D-3.03b-2 · 2026-07-31 · The provisional banner narrows to the direct contacts only
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The banner on `/kontakt` read „Контакт-каналите подолу допрва се поставуваат" — *the contact channels below are still being set up*. With the form live that sentence is now false for the larger half of the page, and it would actively discourage a visitor from using a form that works.
- **Decision:** Narrow it to „Формуларот е активен; директните контакти допрва се поставуваат." The banner itself **stays** — the page is still provisional under D-2.05-1 (the second Ace sit-down has still not happened) and the direct-contact block is still two placeholder chips.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Remove the banner outright* — rejected: D-2.05-1 has not been lifted, and PL-3/PL-15 still render as visible chips two columns over; removing it would overstate how finished the page is. *Leave the copy as-is* — rejected: it is a factual claim about the site's own state and it became untrue the moment the endpoint was set.
- **Consequences:** Structural copy only — it describes the page, and claims nothing about the club, so `facts.md` is not involved. Macedonian, consistent with D-2.07-1.
- **Links:** D-2.05-1; D-2.07-1; D-3.03b-1; `src/app/(site)/kontakt/page.tsx`.

### D-3.03b-3 · 2026-07-31 · The banner is rewritten a second time, because 3.07 cleared PL-3 mid-branch
- **Status:** Accepted (supersedes the copy chosen in **D-3.03b-2**; that entry's Status is unchanged, per the append-only rule)
- **Context:** D-3.03b-2 rewrote the `/kontakt` banner to „Формуларот е активен; директните контакти допрва се поставуваат." while this branch was open. **3.07 merged to `main` a few hours later and made the contact email live** on both its surfaces (D-3.07-5) — so „директните контакти допрва се поставуваат" was false by the time this branch merged. The same trap D-3.03b-2 was written to escape, sprung a second time by a parallel phase: two operators, one repo, overlapping work on the same page.
- **Decision:** Rewrite it once more, naming the one thing that is genuinely still missing: „Формуларот и е-поштата се активни; профилите на социјалните мрежи допрва се поставуваат." The banner still **stays** — D-2.05-1 has not been lifted and PL-15 is still a visible chip.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Keep D-3.03b-2's copy and let 3.07's author fix it* — rejected: it would have shipped a false statement about the site's own state in the very merge that made it false, and this branch is the one that introduced the sentence. *Drop the banner now that only one chip is left* — rejected: same reason as in D-3.03b-2; the page is still provisional and the sit-down still has not happened. *Edit D-3.03b-2 in place* — rejected outright: `decisions.md` is append-only and reversals get a new entry linking the old (CLAUDE.md).
- **Consequences:** `/kontakt`'s `<main>` now carries **one** placeholder chip (PL-15), down from three at the start of the day — PL-14 cleared by this phase, PL-3 by 3.07. Structural copy only, so `facts.md` is not involved. **Worth noting as a process point:** the branch was cut before 3.07 landed and the conflict surfaced only at merge time; the state-file conflict was real and resolved by keeping both phases' entries, the same way D-3.09/3.07 were reconciled at `d9ddf2c`.
- **Links:** D-3.03b-2; D-3.07-5; D-2.05-1; `src/app/(site)/kontakt/page.tsx`.

### D-3.10-1 · 2026-07-31 · The `/kontakt` provisional banner is removed, retiring D-2.05-1 and the two rewrites it caused
- **Status:** Accepted (supersedes **D-2.05-1**, **D-3.03b-2** and **D-3.03b-3**, and the banner half of **D-3.07-5**; those entries' Status lines are unchanged, per the append-only rule)
- **Context:** The banner said „Страницата е во подготовка" and, after two rewrites in a single day, „Формуларот и е-поштата се активни; профилите на социјалните мрежи допрва се поставуваат." Both halves of that sentence were by then reporting good news: the form works, the email is published. What remained was a page apologising for itself over a single unverified item that already renders as its own visible chip two columns away. The owner asked for it to go.
- **Decision:** Delete the whole `<Reveal>` block and the `mt-10` that spaced it, so the two-column grid opens the paper section on the same rhythm as every other Part-2 route. The page is no longer marked provisional anywhere.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Keep it and narrow the copy a third time* — rejected: three rewrites in one day is the signal that the element itself is wrong, not its wording. *Keep it until the second Ace sit-down happens* — rejected: that was D-2.05-1's condition, and the owner has now lifted it directly; a standing decision outranked by the person who set it. *Remove the socials chip too* — rejected outright: **PL-15 is unverified and stays an open chip** (content-truth); the banner going does not make the socials known.
- **Consequences:** `/kontakt` renders no „в подготовка" copy at all. The form, the `mailto:` link and the PL-15 chip are untouched and all still render — verified in the browser. **PL-15 remains the page's one open placeholder.** Note that „Страницата е во подготовка" still legitimately appears on **`/za-nas`**, which has its own separate `ProvisionalBanner` with its own copy and its own open fields — see D-3.10-9.
- **Links:** D-2.05-1; D-3.03b-2; D-3.03b-3; D-3.07-5; PL-15; `src/app/(site)/kontakt/page.tsx`.

### D-3.10-2 · 2026-07-31 · The legends search moves into the navy page header, and `LegendsBrowser` takes ownership of that header
- **Status:** Accepted
- **Context:** The search field opened the paper section on its own, leaving a wide empty band between the navy header block and the first band's orange rule — a field floating in white space rather than part of the page's opening.
- **Decision:** `LegendsBrowser` now renders `PageHeader` itself, passing the field as `children` below a new archive-total `meta` line, and keeps the bands in the `Container` beneath. The server page keeps every server concern — the GROQ read, the Cyrillic collation, the band placement — and passes `bands` down.
- **Alternatives considered:** *Render the field as a separate navy strip under `PageHeader`* — the brief's fallback, unnecessary: `PageHeader`, `Breadcrumb` and `Container` are all purely presentational and import cleanly into a client component (verified — the build prerenders `/legendi` as static, and all 160 people are in the server HTML). *Lift the query into context or the URL* — rejected: far more machinery than a page-local filter needs, and it would put a search term in the address bar that nothing else reads.
- **Consequences:** The header carries breadcrumb → H1 → intro → „160 личности" → the field, and the paper block below opens directly on the first orange rule. Measured at 375/768/1280/1408: no white strip at any of them, only the standard `py-section` rhythm (48/48/64/70 px). The field's chrome moves to the navy scale — label `text-paper` (14.95:1), count and clear button `text-paper/80` (9.95:1), focus ring `focusOnNavy`, confirmed orange 3 px / 2 px offset under a real Tab. The input surface stays white, so its own text (18.11:1) and placeholder (6.69:1) are measured against white — see D-3.10-8.
